# Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā 2004

## Introduction by Dravida dāsa

Like Kṛṣṇa consciousness itself, Śrīla Prabhupāda’s Vyāsa-pūjā book is simultaneously repetitive and uniquely varied. By this I mean that in this book, as in those that came before, we will find expressions of gratitude, prayer, confession, and glorification. But like every day in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, there is unlimited variety and freshness as well.

This year we find several themes predominating. The first is deep appreciation of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s gift of the installation of the Pañca-tattva in Māyāpur. For all who were there, it was a life-changing experience. This is how Romapāda Mahārāja put it:

This year, on the 108th anniversary of your appearance, your disciples, under your order, arranged to have the deity forms of Śrī Śrī Pañca-tattva make their appearance in Śrīdhām Māyāpur. What an experience! The installation ceremony of the Pañca-tattva was like being in your physical presence once again! It was ecstatic!

I openly declare that the likeness of experiencing your personal presence in Māyāpur and the experience of the Pañca-tattva installation allowed me to realize in a deeper way than ever before what an intimate associate of the Pañca-tattva you actually are!

Rādhikā Ramaṇa dāsa of Boise, Idaho, put it this way:

This year we saw a historic event take place in Śrīdhām Māyāpur—a significant step in the fulfillment of your vision for Lord Caitanya’s birthplace. Ten thousand devotees chanted, danced, and welcomed Śrī Śrī Pañca-tattva. Each one left personally transformed. “I’ve never experienced anything like this in my life. It was beyond words.” The power of your desire and instructions is clearly visible.

The Vyāsa-pūjā book over the years has served as a kind of thermometer indicating the “temperature” of ISKCON, whether it was feverish and troubled with some internal ailment or healthy and flourishing. This year it seems the temperature has returned closer to normal—there was a lot of healthy hope and determination expressed, and much of it flowed from the experience of the devotees in Māyāpur witnessing the installation of Śrī Pañca-tattva.

Another repeated theme this year, one that I expect will recur in future books, is the heightened sense of mortality and urgency of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s disciples, brought out especially by the passing of His Holiness Śrīdhara Mahārāja in Māyāpur. Though devotees expressed sadness at his passing, there was great appreciation for the way he passed—in Śrīdhām Māyāpur right after the installation ceremony, and surrounded by loving devotees chanting the Holy Name. Here is how His Holiness Rādhānāth Swami expressed his feelings:

This year our dear Godbrother His Holiness Śrīdhara Swami Mahārāja departed from this world. His glorious passing was a special lesson for all of us. When he arrived at the gate in Śrīdhām Māyāpur, thousands of sincere devotees from all over the world assembled in *saṅkīrtana* to receive him. It was an incredible offering of love to your dedicated servant. Everyone felt Your Divine Grace presiding over this heart-melting experience. In his presence we participated in the historical installation of ŚrīPañca-tattva. By your prayers of compassion, Śrīla Prabhupāda, these extraordinary Deities have descended to deliver all the world. Śrīdhara Mahārāja offered the first *ārati* to Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Mahārāja brought joy to all of our hearts as he affectionately united devotees from all over the world to chant the Holy Names at his bedside. Just after Gaura Pūrṇimā, surrounded by the sweet *kīrtana* of the *mahā-mantra,* he graciously gave up his earthly body while lovingly gazing upon your photograph. In the glorious passing of Śrīdhara Swami Mahārāja you have once again revealed your infinite love for your followers. I pray that I may forever aspire to reciprocate with that most precious love.

The Vyāsa-pūjā book is like a snapshot of ISKCON. Like all snapshots, it only shows a tiny slice of reality at a given point in time, but because its central focus is the most exalted pure devotee, Śrīla Prabhupāda, it reveals profound truth as well. The same holds for the “snapshots within the snapshot”the photographs in the book. In this edition we are happy to include a rare photo of Śrīla Prabhupāda in Hawaii sent in by Daśaratha Paṇḍita Prabhu. You’ll find it in the color section. There is Śrīla Prabhupāda about to hand a flower to a two- or three-year-old girl, who eagerly reaches for it. Prabhupāda is obviously relishing the exchange. Just think! There is the *jagad-guru,* the representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva, the founder-*ācārya* of the institution that embodies the *śakti* of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and the entire *paramparā,* that will transform the world and save countless of its inhabitants from hellish suffering for ten millennia by bestowing on them the bliss of Kṛṣṇa consciousness—there is Śrīla Prabhupāda taking the time, out of his infinite and infinitely personal love and compassion, to give a *mahā-prasādam* flower to a little girl and increase her attachment to him and Kṛṣṇa for her eternal benefit. I pray to one day be as eager to receive all of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s gifts as that little girl was eager to receive that flower on that day from His Divine Grace.

Draviḍa dasa  
Vyāsa-pūjā book editor, 1987–2004

## The Meaning of Vyāsa-pūjā by Śrīmatī Rambhoru Dāsī

Whether one is trying to become skilled in the art of medicine, law, administration, or music, one will soon discover that the quickest way of doing it is to follow the beaten paths of those who have gone before. By sitting at the feet of a master in one’s chosen field, one can learn in five minutes what may otherwise take a lifetime to learn.

*Bhakti-yoga,* like any other craft, is a science that has a right way and a not-so-right way of doing it, a theory and praxis that, if followed strictly, will guarantee one a predictable result. And like every other profession, it has its *mahājanas,* or “heavyweights,” who have mastered the art—in this case, the art of loving God. In the same way that water gushes forth from a river delta, the message of Lord Kṛṣṇa comes to us through a stream of spiritual masters, flowing directly from the original source through the Lord’s literary incarnation, Śrīla Vyāsadeva. On Vyāsa-pūjā day, novices like ourselves, who have been nourished by that stream, take the opportunity of celebrating and honoring that personality who has made the words of Kṛṣṇa accessible to us.

In previous ages, Vedic knowledge was handed down through oral recitation from guru to disciple. But as Kali-yuga approached, Śrīla Vyāsadeva saw that the people of this age would be too short-lived, short of patience, and short of memory to comprehend transcendental knowledge simply by hearing. So he had the Vedic knowledge written down, divided the one *Veda* into four (*Ṛk, Sāma, Yajur,* and *Atharva*), and entrusted each to a different sage for propagation. And to make the knowledge accessible to those with no access to the *Vedas,* Vyāsa wrote the *Purāṇas* and histories, such as the *Mahābhārata*. But he still felt his efforts had fallen short. Finally Vyāsa’s guru, Śrī Nārada, who had received Vedic knowledge from his father, Lord Brahmā, the first to hear the Lord’s pure message, empowered Vyāsa to write a literary work that would illuminate for a modern audience the essence of all spiritual subject matter, devotional service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Vyāsa thus compiled the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* which presents the essence of Vedic teachings through a series of conversations and historical accounts. In this way *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* captivates its readers through its transcendental narrative and attracts them to Lord Kṛṣṇa and His service.

We worship Vyāsa through the spiritual master on his appearance day because he reveals to us the wealth of knowledge Vyāsa compiled. His living example, personifying that knowledge, inspires us to enter onto the path of *bhakti,* persevere on it, and work to fulfill the mission of making it available to everyone. Having mastered the art of loving Kṛṣṇa himself by placing his head at the feet of his own spiritual master, the spiritual master fully understands how to guide us in the revelatory process of loving God and knowing the self. In the same way that an electrical outlet delivers to us, in a way we can use, the powerful current emanating from the power plant, our spiritual master is the conducting rod that connects us to Lord Kṛṣṇa, the source of all knowledge, strength, beauty, wealth, fame, and renunciation. He is our unique link through which we enter into the line of unadulterated instructions flowing directly from Vyāsa, the literary incarnation of the Lord.

For the members of the International Society for Krishna Consciousness, the Vyāsa-pūjā celebration is especially meaningful because we offer homage to Śrīla Prabhupāda, whose teachings, directly in the line of Vyāsadeva, reveal to us how to recover our relationship with Kṛṣṇa. Just as Vyāsadeva wrote literature for the spiritual neophytes, Śrīla Prabhupāda compassionately provided us common folk clear and straightforward commentaries so that we might make progress on the path of loving Kṛṣṇa.

So, on this auspicious day, we join together and express our gratitude to Śrīla Prabhupāda for giving us the sacred message of Lord Kṛṣṇa, exactly as it was spoken through the ages. But although those words have been passed down from generation to generation, we do not experience them as stale or hackneyed, because they have been thoroughly tasted, chewed, and digested by the pure devotee. As it is said of Śukadeva Gosvāmī, *śuka-mukhād amṛta-drava-saṁyutam:* “\[The *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*\] emanated from the lips of Śrī Śukadeva Gosvāmī. Therefore this fruit has become even more tasteful.” Especially for Śrīla Prabhupāda’s followers, the transcendental knowledge has become even tastier for having been received through him.

In contrast to the kind of worship offered superiors in exchange for some temporary material boon, the worship we offer the Lord’s representative on this day benefits us spiritually. It intensifies our faith in Kṛṣṇa and inspires us to rededicate ourselves to the path that leads us to that treasure of pure love for Him. Vyāsa-pūjā is a time for us to meditate even more intensely on the wonderful qualities and activities of Śrīla Prabhupāda. By doing so we not only solidify our relationship with him but increase our ability to perceive reality through the transcendental lens of service to him.In the *Bhagavad-gītā* (18.68–69) Lord Kṛṣṇa says,

*ya idaṁ paramaṁ guhyaṁ mad-bhakteṣv abhidhāsyati*  
*bhaktiṁ mayi parāṁ kṛtvā mām evaiṣyaty asaṁśayaḥ*  
*na ca tasmān manuṣyeṣu kaścin me priya-kṛttamaḥ*  
*bhavitā na ca me tasmād anyaḥ priyataro bhuvi*

**“For one who explains this supreme secret to the devotees, pure devotional service is guaranteed, and at the end he will come back to Me. There is no servant in this world more dear to Me than he, nor will there ever be one more dear.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda is that dearest servant of Kṛṣṇa because he dedicated his every waking moment to explaining the supreme secret of devotional service to the benighted souls of this age. And anyone who purely follows in his footsteps will also one day render intimate devotional service to the Lord. How do we do that? Kṛṣṇa says, “Always think of Me, become My devotee, worship Me and offer your homage unto Me. Thus you will come to Me without fail. I promise you this because you are My very dear friend.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 18.65). The process is clear. Become the servant of the servant of the servant of Kṛṣṇa by serving the mission of Śrīla Prabhupāda, himself the pure servant of all those servants descending in the line of Śrīla Vedavyāsa.

Śrīmatī Rambhoru Dāsī, Mdiv. (Master of Divinity), Claremonot School of Theology

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from the GBC

## Badrinārāyaṇ dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Once you told a story how, while working in your *gṛhastha* days as a pharmaceutical merchant, you were asked by a friend if you could give him the tonic you were taking, the one that produced such pure devotion to the Lord. You humbly replied, “I don’t have that tonic, but I *do* know the ingredients.” You quoted:

*tṛṇād api sunīcena taror iva sahiṣṇunā*  
*amāninā māna-dena kīrtanīyaḥ sadā hariḥ*

You are the embodiment of these qualities.

***Tṛṇād api sunīcena:*** **more humble than the grass**

In Detroit you wanted a painting of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī brought to your *darśana* room and hung behind your desk. When the devotees came to do this, they found you sitting at your desk working. They asked, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, should we come back later?” You said “No, you can do it now.” As the painting was quite large, with a heavy wooden frame, they asked, “Śrīla Prabhupāda, would you like to get up for a moment while we are hanging it?” You said, “No, you can go ahead now.” The devotees made their initial attempt, and feeling that they might jostle or disturb you in the process, again asked you if you would like them to come back later. You replied, “What? For service you can stand on my head.”

Trivikrama Mahārāja narrates how he was massaging you one day in London. In the exuberance of a loving disciple, he was overflowing with praise for the powerful public lecture series you were giving in the evenings. He went on for some time until you stopped him with a slap on the mat and a firmly voiced “No,” then adding softly, “I am simply the servant of my Guru Mahārāja.”

***Taror iva sahiṣṇunā:*** **more tolerant than a tree**

It was the practice that your GBCs would send you a monthly report. The then GBC for Mexico wrote to you expressing his difficulties working with the president of the Mexico City temple, closing his letter by saying ,“But I have to give the devil his due. He is very good at organizing book distribution.” You wrote back, “This is a devotee. He can find good even in the devil.” Then you added, “I ask that you tolerate him, just as I tolerate you.”

On one of your visits to Detroit you were giving the morning class, attended by devotees from throughout the Midwest, the East Coast, and the Rādhā-Dāmodara bus party. The temple room was packed. To prepare for your arrival, devotees had worked around the clock, and practically none of them had slept in forty-eight hours. The morning class was the first time that most of them had found a moment to even sit down, and nature took its course. Almost everyone fell asleep. You were explaining how Lord Caitanya’s mercy is causeless. To illustrate the point, you gave the example that a poor man does not become rich unless something extraordinary happens; he gets an inheritance, he finds some gold on the road, and so on. Coming to a crescendo, you said in a strong voice, “In the same way, this movement is going on by the mercy of Kṛṣṇa alone.” Then, looking over your reading glasses at the sea of sleeping disciples, you added with a gentle chuckle, “*That* is a fact.”

***Amāninā māna-dena:***  
not desiring any respect for oneself  
but ready to offer all respect to others

Anyone who is honest could see that you were directly guided by the hand of the Lord, but once, when you were asked, “Why did you come to the USA to start your movement?” you replied, “Some of my Godbrothers came to England and failed. Another went to Germany and failed. I thought, If I am to fail, at least let me fail in a new place.”

Out of love, it became the fashion that when you arrived at a temple the local and visiting leaders would each arrange for the most luxurious flower garlands possible: gardenias flown in from Florida, frangipani from Hawaii, fragrant roses from Costa Rica. A traveling *saṅkīrtana* party, driving nonstop to be on time for your arrival, picked whatever flowers they could find growing along the highway. Later in the temple room, the *saṅkīrtana* leader, seeing you almost completely covered with these opulent garlands, hesitated to approach you with his party’s modest offering of a garland of simple flowers. Seeing his reluctance, you took off all the other garlands and put on the traveling *saṅkīrtana* party’s humble offering, saying, “Forest flowers—just like Rādhārāṇī makes for Kṛṣṇa.”

Although you were due the greatest respect as an empowered representative of the Lord, you presented yourself in a most humble mood. At the same time, you accepted even the most modest attempts at service, if done in sincere devotion, as worthy of great note.

***Kīrtanīyaḥ sadā hariḥ:*** **always able to chant the name of the Lord**

Being uniquely surrendered, you were uniquely empowered to spread the glories of the Holy Name all over the world. Many years ago, in order to pay for my householder expenses, I would sell paintings. Along with several other devotees, we found a lucrative venue, traveling to large oil production camps. These *campos* housed hundreds of well-paid workers and were often in very remote locations (since that was where the oil was to be found). Once we got a tip about a site named Bintulu on the island of Borneo. I have to admit to being an inveterate tourist. Ever since reading *National Geographic* in my childhood days, I had dreamed of visiting “the wild land of Borneo.” To get to the site, we had to travel by jet, then by prop plane, then by ferryboat, and finally by bus, driving on the sand as wild boars ran on the beach and orangutans swung through the jungle treetops. I was thinking to myself, “I am such an adventurer, out here on the edge of the world.” But as I walked into one of the few guesthouses in the tiny town, there behind the desk was the receptionist reading your *Śrī Īśopaniṣad*. You had arrived long before I had and you were preaching powerfully.

That I have been able to find the shelter of such a personality as you is your causeless mercy, and I hope to some day be able to repay even one drop of your kindness.

*Your insignificant servant,*  
Badrinārāyaṇ dāsa

## Bhakti Caitanya Swami

My dear lord and master, Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you!

This is the 108th Vyāsa-pūjā anniversary of your appearance in this material world. Thank you so much for coming and giving us your mercy, and overlooking our disqualifications and our fallen condition. You are so transcendental that you were able to deal with us completely effectively, despite our extreme shortcomings and weaknesses, and this is one of the great proofs of your exalted spiritual position. You brought our dormant Kṛṣṇa consciousness out from within our contaminated hearts, just as a broadminded person knows how to take out gold from a dirty place, and is not put off by the filth.

We see your glory in how you’ve created so much auspiciousness in our lives. Through contact with you, so many people have found their lives changed very deeply. You are a transcendental touchstone, and when people come into your association something is set alight in their hearts, and then they are never the same again, ever.

We have just finished the 16th annual Jagannātha Ratha-yātrā Festival in Durban, South Africa, and in it we saw all these things being displayed before our eyes. Our festival site was like a spiritual island in the midst of a sea of materialism, and every day for four days thousands of people were flocking in from all over, finding refuge there and getting a profound taste for Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Some people would come in every day and spend the whole day there, just immersed in the transcendental atmosphere, and when we finally finished, so many expressed their disappointment that we couldn’t go on longer.

This is all coming from you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Ratha-yātrā is one of your special gifts to us and to the world, and it is one of so many wonderful examples of your intelligence and expertise in giving Kṛṣṇa consciousness in just the right ways. I first participated in Ratha-yātrā in London in 1973, in your presence, and I saw it was certainly a wonderful occasion. However, as time goes on I have come to understand that this festival is not just a matter of chanting and dancing with the Deities in procession (although that is glorious enough in itself), but through it you have given us the chance to enter into a deeper realm of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

In the Ratha-yātrā we re-enact a most sacred pastime of the Lord, in which He is reunited with His dear Vṛndāvana devotees. Normally when we celebrate festivals in our temples, we honor Kṛṣṇa and His pastimes, but nowhere else do we actually participate with Him in His *līlās*. If we tried to re-enact the *rāsa-līlā* dance we would be considered misdirected *sahajiyās,* but in this festival we take the position of the *gopīs,* pulling on the ropes tied to the Lord’s chariot and dragging Him back to Śrī Vṛndāvana-dhāma. Truly this is a most extraordinary gift you have given, and we are eternally grateful to you for that.

As Lord Caitanya danced before Lord Jagannātha He sang:

*yaḥ kaumāra-haraḥ sa eva hi varas tā eva caitra-kṣapās*  
*te conmīlita-mālatī-surabhayaḥ prauḍhāḥ kadambānilāḥ*  
*sā caivāsmi tathāpi tatra surata-vyāpāra-līlā-vidhau*  
*revā-rodhasi vetasī-taru-tale cetaḥ samutkaṇṭhate*

**“That very personality who stole away my heart during my youth is now again my master. These are the same moonlit nights of the month of Caitra. The same fragrance of** ***mālatī*****flowers is there, and the same sweet breezes are blowing from the** ***kadamba*** **forest. In our intimate relationship, I am also the same lover, yet still my mind is not happy here. I am eager to go back to that place on the bank of the Revā under the Vetasī tree. That is my desire.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya-līlā*** **13.121)**

Lord Caitanya expressed the emotions Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī feels at the peak of Her separation from Her beloved Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and Her intense desire to be reunited with Him in Their abode, Vṛndāvana. We are also separated from Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual world, although our feelings about our situation are greatly different from Hers, but in the Ratha-yātrā festival we take Her role and the role of Her intimate associates, and drag Kṛṣṇa back to the *dhāma*.

In his *Saṅkalpa-kalpadruma,* Śrīla Jīva Gosvāmī describes the scene when Kṛṣṇa returned to Vṛndāvana:

As He entered Vraja the people offered *ārati* to Him with many jewels, showered Him with flowers, called out *Jaya! Jaya!* and greeted Him with auspicious words. Pleasing them with graceful and humble words, Lord Kṛṣṇa gave His association to the people of Vraja. . . . His lotus face blossoming with happiness, He began a great festival of nectar for the bumblebee eyes of His friends. Day after day the people of Vraja loved Kṛṣṇa with a love greater than before. Day after day they saw that Kṛṣṇa’s handsomeness was greater than they had seen before.

So in our small ways we also offered *ārati* to Jagannātha from time to time and chanted His glories in great happiness. The Lord seemed to look more beautiful every day, and by the end of the last day no one wanted Him to go, and no one wanted the festival to come to an end. It was quite overwhelming. At the Ratha-yātrā festival in London in 1972, you spoke the following words, Śrīla Prabhupāda:

So this Ratha-yātrā festival is still observed in Jagannātha Purī, in India. And five hundred years ago Lord Caitanya participated in this festival, and He was in the mode of separation as if Rādhārāṇī was taking back Kṛṣṇa to Vṛndāvana. So this Ratha-yātrā festival is a feeling festival for the Vaiṣṇavas. Lord Caitanya taught us how to feel separation of God. Lord Caitanya never taught us that He had seen God, but He felt the separation of God very severely. Similarly, His next disciples, the *ṣaḍ-gosvāmīs,* they also prosecuted their devotional service by separational feelings. . . . So this Ratha-yātrā festival is very nice, feelings festival for the Vaiṣṇava. And anyone who is participating in this festival, he’ll gradually develop his dormant love for Kṛṣṇa.

We thank you from the bottom of our hearts, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for this amazing gift of Jagannātha Ratha-yātrā. By your mercy may we continue to celebrate it on grander and grander scales every year.

And by your mercy may I celebrate it with you again, personally, some time in the future.

*Your lowly servant,*  
Bhakti Caitanya Swami

## Bhakti Chāru Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. Please allow me to always stay under that most soothing shelter.

When I arrived in Vṛndāvana from Māyāpur in 1977, you asked me how everything was going on in Māyāpur. Although your health was quite bad at that time and you were bedridden, you expressed such deep concern about the condition of your movement and the situation of your devotees there. Since I was quite new in the movement, I was rather hesitant to say very much. However, you emphasized that as a member of ISKCON, I should be concerned about the welfare of our institution and should not hesitate to express my opinion and report on its affairs.

Many a time thereafter, you sent me to observe and report to you about the state of affairs in our temples. Once in Vṛndāvana, although you had eaten practically nothing for weeks, you asked me to find out about the *prasādam* for the devotees, and when I reported to you that the quality was not so good, you called the temple president and questioned him about it and advised him to see that the devotees got nice *prasādam*.

Since your disappearance from this planet, I have often expressed my own agonies and ecstasies to you, but I never really considered reporting to you about the condition of our movement. I know that you are seeing everything; nevertheless, I have decided to submit my report to you today.

You told us that when an *ācārya* leaves the planet there is bound to be a crisis. At that time I felt that although it may have happened in the past, it would not happen in ISKCON. However, it eventually became evident how true your statement was. Today, we can only say, “An *ācārya* like you has left, so how can we expect that there would not be any crisis?”

Since your disappearance, ISKCON has been severely afflicted by many onslaughts from within and without. However, by your mercy, we have been able to overcome most of those difficulties, and today your ISKCON is standing on much firmer ground. You knew perfectly well what sort of difficulties the devotees would face in the future, and you made perfect arrangements to secure the lives of your loyal followers through the institution of ISKCON.

I was very fortunate to receive three very clear instructions from you: 1. “Your love for me will be shown by how you cooperate with one another.” 2. “No matter what happens, don’t leave ISKCON.” 3. “Abide by the GBC as the ultimate managing authority of ISKCON.”

These three instructions have saved and secured my spiritual life through many serious crises and difficulties. I began to realize that these three instructions are actually the key to our spiritual success in this age.

Many times I have heard thoughtful well-wishers of ISKCON state that your real greatness is manifested in the quality of your disciples. Although for various reasons many of them are not so actively involved in ISKCON today, whenever I speak to them I become amazingly aware of their love for you and their concern for the propagation of Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world.

Many of them are coming back after distancing themselves for a few years. Notable among them are Śyāmasundara Prabhu, Mālatī Mātājī, Yamunā-devī dāsī, Madhudviṣa Prabhu, Gopāl Bhaṭṭa Prabhu, Bhavānanda Prabhu, and Haṁsadūta Prabhu.

Mālatī became a member of the GBC body, and in spite of her frail health she has taken on an incredible amount of responsibility and is giving wonderful guidance, like a caring mother, to many deserving devotees.

Madhudviṣa Prabhu is one of the most exemplary devotees in our movement today. Without caring for any recognition or adoration, he is serving your mission with implicit faith and sincerity. Wherever he goes, he enlivens everyone with his ecstatic *kīrtanas* and eloquent classes. He is setting a beautiful example of humility and sobriety for all of us.

Your very dear disciple Yamunā-devī dāsī, in her silent and sincere way, is pushing your movement forward through her cookbooks, seminars, and heartfelt warmth.

Gopāl Bhaṭṭa Prabhu became a very successful businessman. Now, at a very crucial time when the movement is facing some very difficult financial crises, he has come forward to help ISKCON with his experience and expertise.

The members of your GBC body, after many crises and challenges, are becoming very competent and are expertly steering the movement through a very difficult course. Many of them are achieving some extraordinary success in their endeavors to establish Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world:

Śivarāma Mahārāja has established a very beautiful spiritual community in Hungary called New Vraja Dhāma. Hundreds of devotees live there in a very pure spiritual atmosphere. He has indeed transplanted a Kṛṣṇa conscious community, right out of Vedic India, into the middle of Europe. He has also created a deep impression in the hearts of many very important people of that country and established a faculty for Vedic studies in the University of Budapest. In spite of his very busy schedule, he has written some extraordinary Kṛṣṇa conscious literatures and published them in an extremely fascinating way.

Under Rādhānātha Mahārāja’s saintly guidance, the Chowpatty temple in Bombay is experiencing an extraordinary preaching success and setting the most wonderful example for everyone to follow. Thousands of families have become attracted to Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and they are being trained and guided in a very systematic way to a very lofty spiritual standard. He stands out as an ideal disciple of Your Divine Grace with his purity and simplicity.

In Russia, where you planted the seed of Kṛṣṇa consciousness through one short visit, the creeper of devotional service has grown most wonderfully and is bearing amazing flowers and fruits under the ideal leadership and guidance of Bhakti Vijñāna Mahārāja, Nirañjana Mahārāja, Gopāl Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja, and Bhakti Chaitanya Mahārāja. Thousands of Russians are decorating your movement there with their outstanding dedication and conviction. As a result of their sincere involvement, the movement is growing most wonderfully. Moscow’s municipal authorities have offered a beautiful piece of land in the heart of the city, close to Red Square, to build a temple for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. The leaders of the Russian Orthodox Church were very upset about this development and tried their best to stop this from happening. There was a massive confrontation, and by Kṛṣṇa’s mercy not only were they defeated but this victory increased the reputation, glory, and popularity of ISKCON throughout that country.

Kṛṣṇa consciousness is also spreading very effectively in Kazakhstan, under Bhakti Bhṛṅga Govinda Mahārāja’s wonderful leadership. In that Muslim country not only are hundreds of people accepting Kṛṣṇa consciousness but the government has also became very favorable. He has established a self-sufficient farm community there and is preparing to build a gorgeous temple.

In the USA, despite many difficulties and severe attacks from the enemies of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, ISKCON is progressing nicely due to the exemplary leadership of Bīr Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja, Romapāda Mahārāja, Bhakti Tīrtha Mahārāja, Badrinārāyaṇ Prabhu, MālatīMātājī, and others.

Under the expert leadership and able management of Svavāsa Prabhu, the New Dvārakā temple in Los Angeles is flourishing most wonderfully. Because of the wonderful arrangements they have made there, many important people are becoming very favorable to our movement.

Jayapatāka Mahārāja has systematically organized congregational preaching through the Bhakti Vṛkṣa program, which is achieving remarkable results in different parts of the world.

Ambarīṣa Prabhu has become very committed to building The Temple of the Vedic Planetarium in Māyāpur. He has taken up this assignment with utmost sincerity in accordance with your instructions to him. This year he agreed to lead this project by becoming the chairman of the Śrī Māyāpur Project Development Committee. His active involvement has created a sensation in India’s financial and political circles. For example, the governor of Orissa personally contacted him when he was in Calcutta and offered him three hundred acres of land to build a Kṛṣṇa conscious project there. The chief minister of Uttar Pradesh sent his key men to meet him when he was in Delhi and assured him all his help and support if he wanted to do anything in Vṛndāvana.

Indradyumna Mahārāja, although a very competent manager, does not take any active role in the GBC. Rather, he has dedicated himself to directly preaching and propagating Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In Poland, where he spends most of his time and energy, he organizes wonderful musical festivals during the summer where hundreds of thousands of people participate and get introduced to Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Many of your grand-disciples are taking up responsibilities to guide the movement and are displaying their wonderful leadership qualities.

One of them, Praghoṣa dāsa Adhikārī, a disciple of Satsvarūpa Mahārāja, became the chairman of the GBC body this year. His wonderful performance as a leader won everyone’s confidence and gives us the assurance that your movement will continue to progress under the able guidance of your grand-disciples.

Another grand-disciple of yours, who adorns the GBC body, is Bhakti Vijñāna Goswami Mahārāja. He was one of the valiant warriors during the struggles in Russia. He is giving outstanding leadership in Russia, and under his wonderful guidance Kṛṣṇa consciousness is spreading most effectively there.

When England was devastated after two major crises in the early eighties, two of your grand-disciples, Śrutidharma and Prāṇabandhu dāsa Adhikārīs, took it upon themselves to save the spiritual lives of the devotees who were afflicted by those disasters. They worked tirelessly for years together, practically without caring for their own personal needs, and as a result Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* movement is spreading there in a remarkable way. Due to their sincere guidance and their selfless concern for the spiritual welfare of the congregation of devotees, hundreds of families have committed themselves to Kṛṣṇa consciousness so sincerely that they consider their service to the temple their most important business.

You gave us a perfect process, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and if we follow it, our mission will be accomplished without difficulty. The trouble starts when we fail to follow Your Divine Grace. Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, open our darkened eyes and enlighten us with perfect spiritual understanding. We have offered ourselves unconditionally at your divine lotus feet, and all we desire is to become completely surrendered like puppets in your hands so we may take part in Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* movement and fulfill His prediction that Kṛṣṇa consciousness will spread all over the world, in every town and village.

Desiring always to remain at your divine lotus feet as a speck of dust,  
Bhakti Chāru Swami

## Bhakti-Tīrtha Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you as our eternal father and grandfather!

I would like to have written you this very simple but meaningful offering this year:

“Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given all of us so much that we can never fully repay you, but we can only make some small attempts. Where would we all have been if you had not come into our lives? If we had material success without you, this would have increased our illusions. If we had material failures without you, this would have increased our confusion. You have given us such an awesome family! We are so colorful and diversified in every way. This creates wonderful challenges, adventures, and so many wonderful opportunities to serve each other and to serve others together. It is so wonderful to see how your real leaders are so determined to remain loyal to you at any cost. It is so exciting to see how many are maturing more as first-class *sādhus*. It is amazing how, though we may fight amongst ourselves, when it comes to protecting your house and your legacy we stand united. It is even more astonishing how you are beginning to call some of your leaders and followers back to join you in their eternal service to you. You have captured their hearts and they have captured yours.

“However, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, many of your disciples and grand-disciples, as well as *gurukulīs,* are stuck with so many *anarthas*. As bad, or worse, some are experiencing great suffering—even more than before they joined Kṛṣṇa consciousness. For now they have so much knowledge, but too many bad habits, attachments, fears, and lack of faith.

“So now I am fifty-five years old. I never expected to live this long. You have given me a wonderful Kṛṣṇa conscious life. I have experienced practically everything I wanted to experience in material as well as spiritual arenas. I have even just finished another thirty-five-country world tour in your service.

“So in the mood of Vāsudeva Datta, I would like to ask you, Can you arrange that their sufferings come to me so that many can be freed from their anguish and thus joyfully serve you and return to Kṛṣṇa with fewer encumbrances? After all, I and many others have procrastinated in so many ways in our full surrender, so in this way you will force me to fully surrender while helping many others. Only if you force me to become a pure son will I be of any significant value for your mission. Prepare me to become a divine slave who is fully captured by your and Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa’s love.

“After all, I asked all the devotees around the world in the year of 2004 in Māyāpur at the Pañca-Tattva installation to pray with me: ‘Whatever we need in order to be better servants for Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mission—let it happen or come to us; and whatever we need to have taken away to become pure in Śrīla Prabhupāda’s service—let it be taken away.’”

So, in this spirit I submit my offering to you for the year 2005. I want to try to give back—so much I owe to you and have received from you. I have one year to fully present and mean this offering. Or will you, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, see fit to accept this offering for this year of 2004?

*Your son (slave),*  
Bhakti-Tīrtha Swami

## Bhaktimārga Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

My observations, as imperfect as they are, leave me in great wonder about the current world situation. I know that Kali’s power is inevitable and we are in its midst. It is overwhelming at times—the direction in which mankind is going. The acceleration and speed of moral decline far surpass the acts of goodness. Our efforts in ISKCON to improve life’s quality appear futile at times. So I am asking for strength to see light at the end of the tunnel. My contribution in this endeavor is meager, but with bonded co-operation from your noble Vaiṣṇavas and Vaiṣṇavīs, I believe some impact is there. It is a fact that united we are a large force with a loud voice—all by your grace.

**We Wonder the Way of the World**

In Kali’s domain  
The reality is pain  
Absence of peace  
Inauspicious release.  
Unsettled lives  
Between husbands and wives  
Shaky finances  
Turn-over romances.  
Erotic displays  
Mental dismays  
Morals have shrunk  
Who is not drunk?  
Water is tainted  
Walls, graffiti painted  
The air is black  
Earth takes such flack.  
Forests over-cut  
Man’s in his rut  
Laying “sweet” concrete  
Being so indiscrete.  
Elders are hidden  
As if they’re forbidden  
We devalue their worth  
And demean their birth.  
And car culture?  
The mode of a vulture  
We sponge on machines  
With deathly routines.  
And security concerns?  
Away it all burns  
We dwell in zones of fear  
Invoking the human tear.  
Noise fills space  
Sounds are so base  
Silence is rare  
Do we at all care?  
Kali takes his course  
Apathy is his force  
Lack of compassion  
The vogue, the fashion.  
Day by day  
I see further decay  
To Yama we race  
Escaping any grace.  
It’s unwholesome to brood  
Sunk in a dark mood  
I resign to you  
Refreshed and renewed.

Bhaktimārga Swami

## Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Swami

My most worshipful Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
I fall prostrate at your divine lotus feet.

In the Vedic scriptures, the most profound instruction regarding the fate of the disciple is enunciated:

*yasya deve parā bhaktir yathā deve tathā gurau*  
*tasyaite kathitā hy arthāḥ prakāśante mahātmanaḥ*

**“Only unto those great souls who simultaneously have implicit faith in both the Lord and the spiritual master are all the imports of the Vedic knowledge automatically revealed.” (*****Śvetāśvatara Upaniṣad*** **6.23)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I humbly pray for your transcendental blessings so that the divine axiom mentioned above may remain in my heart birth after birth.

The Vedic culture solidly establishes the fact that by pleasing the spiritual master the disciple receives the divine knowledge. In this way the disciple has been receiving transcendental knowledge from the spiritual master since time immemorial—Lord Brahmā from Lord Viṣṇu (Kṛṣṇa), Arjuna from Lord Kṛṣṇa, Vyāsadeva from Nārada Muni, Parīkṣit from Śukadeva Gosvāmī, Vidura from Maitreya, and even Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu from Īśvara Purī, and so on. Thus is the process of transmission of spiritual knowledge.

Your Divine Grace has instructed my insignificant self again and again that scientific knowledge should be engaged wholly and solely for the glorification of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead. In this regard, your greatest concern has been the total misconception of modern scientists in relation to life’s origin. Many of them feel very seriously that the beginning of life is purely atoms and molecules.

However, Your Divine Grace taught us that according to the timeless wisdom of the Vedic culture, atoms and molecules are merely the inferior, material energy of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Indeed, there is another, superior energy of the Lord, which is spiritual and includes the spirit souls of all living organisms. In other words, a living being, or life, is an aspect of reality totally different from matter, one which cannot be reduced to atoms and molecules. The living being is the fundamental particle of the Supreme Spirit. This spiritual particle can be termed a “spiriton,” which is different from material particles because it possesses consciousness.

The present scientific tools, however technologically advanced they may be, have no means to measure the superior energy of the Supreme Lord. Science is capable only of comprehending the inferior, material energy of the Lord.

It is vital that, sooner or later, scientists come to see reality beyond mechanism, reality beyond material sense perception, and reality beyond experimentation and measurement. Thus, Your Divine Grace has pronounced that “Life comes from life,” the divine maxim of life’s beginning on earth.

By your blessings, Śrīla Prabhupāda, a significant number of prominent scientists around the world are now coming forward to promote a global vision of understanding the role of God in creation. If the modern world’s scientists can accept life’s origin as divine, then the potential for establishing a human civilization based on a spiritual foundation is quite promising. I am happy to report that within the Bhaktivedanta Institute of Scientific Preaching, many of your spiritual grandchildren are, with great enthusiasm, helping to propagate your inconceivable transcendental plan.

Undoubtedly, science and religion are the two most important prevailing principles in humanity today. Science must now come to incorporate in its conception of the cosmos a spiritual vision, as religion already has. A purification of both religious and scientific thoughts and actions is vital for the benefit of the entire world.

By having implicit faith in Your Divine Grace’s instructions, especially in regards to scientific preaching, we have been humbly trying to execute “the most important preaching arm of ISKCON,” as stated by Your Divine Grace.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are my only hope. If you are pleased, Lord Kṛṣṇa will be pleased and everything will happen as Your Divine Grace planned and conceived.

Implicit faith in the instructions of the spiritual master is the essence of Vedic culture, and the perfection of life. Please bless me in this way, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most auspicious day: may hold your instructions in my heart, life after life.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!

*Your eternal servant,*  
Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodara Svāmī

## Bhaktivaibhava Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Srila Prabhupada,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet!

First of all I want to beg your forgiveness that for some technical reason my Vyāsa-pūjā offering was not been present in last year’s Vyāsa-pūjā book, although I sent it from India. I should have checked it later on. It was my fault. This reminded me of the importance of paying attention to details, something you yourself taught us on numerous occasions, not only in relation to practical matters but especially in relation to our philosophical understanding and practice of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You painstakingly explained through your teachings and practical example how to deal with the multitude of deviations, both philosophical and in practice. You repeatedly cautioned us to simply stick to the process you have given us and not deviate from it:

Guru is very dear to Kṛṣṇa because under the direction of Kṛṣṇa, by the *paramparā* system, he is training, “You do like this.” This is *paramparā* system. Do not deviate. \[Lecture on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.26.30, 7 January 1975\]

This process will make you stay firm. So don’t deviate. Whatever little regulative principles are there, they are not very difficult. Just stick to this principle, chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, eat *prasādam,* and your life will be successful. \[Lecture on *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.5.15, 19 June 1969\]

We cannot deviate from the path of the previous *ācāryas*. We must strictly follow. That is the qualification. We must follow their instruction. Therefore I repeatedly say to my students that “You chant Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra sixteen rounds, and follow the regulative principles. Your strength is there.” If we— just like Himalayan mountain, nobody can push it. Very simple thing. It is so powerful. *Yāhā haite sarva-siddhi haya*. This is Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s request. Don’t deviate from the instruction. Then you will stand as strong as the Himalayan mountain. \[Lecture on *The Nectar of Devotion,* 9 January 1973\]

I am very glad that you are following despite all impediments. The Deity is nicely decorated, and the Deity is worshiped, and He’s pleased upon you. Be blessed. Continue this process, don’t deviate, strictly follow the regulative principles, chant Hare Kṛṣṇa at least sixteen rounds, and you’ll be happy. There is no doubt about it. You can experience. It is not an imposition. Don’t leave this process. Then, in this life, you will be able to understand Kṛṣṇa. \[Arrival address, Los Angeles, 9 February 1975\]

I have started this movement. I want to see that it is going on nicely. So you kindly help me. Don’t deviate. That is my only request. \[*starts to cry*\] Then you will remain solid. \[Departure Address, 15 July 1974\]

Do not deviate from our high standard. That will mean great dishonor to me. Push on in your preaching work as I have shown you, remain pure, enthusiastic, and optimistic, and Kṛṣṇa will favor you with all good results and benedictions. \[Letter, 22 December 1971\]

At the time you made these statements I could not sufficiently esteem your pleading words against the various digressions from the proper understanding and practice of your teachings of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. As time has progressed, I have begun to understand.

Last year I sent my offering to Your Divine Grace from Gaya, India. While visiting many different holy places along the Ganges and seeing many varieties of sadhus, temples, and worship, I felt myself increasingly fortunate to have been able to take shelter at your lotus feet.

You presented the philosophy and practice of spiritual life in a clear and uncompromising way that simply finds no comparison anywhere. Reading your books, listening to your voice, and trying to remain loyal to your ISKCON, I feel your protection wherever I go.

*Your insignificant humble servant,*  
Dasa A.C. Bhaktivaibhava Swami

## Bhūrijana dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Although we walked with you, you did not truly walk among us. Regardless of whether you sat in a devotee’s home in Fiji, a Kobe railway station, or a San Francisco airport or were exiting a taxi or walking through the crowded streets of downtown New York, your mood was always internal, your consciousness fixed in a different realm.

Yet, although you were grave at all times, you so easily broke into an enchanting smile. You were free from anxiety, relaxed, and always chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa. Your absorption in Kṛṣṇa consciousness was unfathomably deep, as was your attachment to serving your Guru Mahārāja, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa.

Your fingers were delicate, and you were expert at all things, even drumming. Your face, although elderly, was remarkably unwrinkled. You were extraordinarily conscious, Kṛṣṇa conscious.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I’ve meditated upon your face for thirty-five years, yet I am amazed that it still appears uniquely attractive. Even your tongue—as it escapes your mouth to wet your lips—is special. Your eye movements, your hands and legs, are so familiar yet startling in their freshness, as is the aristocratic tilt of your head. Bold lines of *tilaka* rest upon your forehead. You remain both familiar yet simultaneously so mysterious.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, what can I say that can glorify you? Your own accomplishments, born of your own pure devotion, illuminate you. Indeed, you, like Kṛṣṇa, are self-illuminating.

I pray that your natural affection for the fallen and your Vaiṣṇava compassion and tolerance allow my being sheltered at your feet to continue. Please don’t discard me and kick me away. At this point in my life, my shortcomings and insincerity are both abundant and obvious, even to me. Please bestow upon me the heart of a Vaiṣṇava to replace my current arrogant, self-centered consciousness. Without that, how can I properly serve you with devotion and surrendered dependence? Śrīla Prabhupāda, I see in you true wealth—attachment to *kṛṣṇa-kathā, kṛṣṇa-nāma,* and *vraja-dhāma*. Will I be able to attain those jewels despite my miserable, contaminated heart? Please bless me with knowing how I may assist and thereby please you. Thank you.

*Your aspiring servant,*  
Bhūrijana dāsa

## Bīr Krishna dās Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace.

This year we suffered the departure of another of your faithful generals, Śrīla Śrīdhara Mahārāja. I was always enlivened by his association, which impelled me in my service to your lotus feet. He was always exhibiting the qualities of enthusiasm, patience, and determination for your service. By his potency these qualities became contagious.

Two years ago we lost the association of Śrīla Tamāl Krishna Mahārāja, who for me was my shelter in understanding your mood and vision for the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. I have implicit trust in his judgment.

When I reflect on the departure of these two great personalities, my first reaction is one of great sadness, knowing that without their association the progress of our Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement will be more difficult, as will my individual progress in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Of course, Kṛṣṇa has personally arranged for these departures to occur. He has a purpose. The pure devotees are always under the jurisdiction of the Lord’s internal potency (*Bhagavad-gītā* 9.13).

I can understand that you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, desire that they join you in your preaching mission. They are your eternal faithful associates. So, whenever you call your eternal associates to join you, they come regardless of whatever else is transpiring in their activities. Therefore Kṛṣṇa arranges for their departure, which for us is extremely painful but for them means that they will rejoin you.

When I came to the above realization, I asked myself this question: “If Śrīla Prabhupāda wanted me to join him somewhere else, would I willfully leave my present circumstances at the drop of a hat, or would I desire to stay here.” In other words, am I simply dovetailing my desires in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, or am I desiring your pure service?

I am constantly revisiting this question. Do I want to become your eternal servant or not? What would I do if you call me now? This is the most important question in my whole existence.

I pray that somehow or other you accept me as your eternal servant, so that I can serve you forever along with the great personalities you have already chosen as your unalloyed servitors. I am begging for your association, and for theirs too.

*Your hopeful servant,*  
Bīr Krishna dās Goswami

## Devāmṛta Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Your ISKCON is a story of *bhakti*.

Because of your *bhakti*, Kṛṣṇa’s glories traversed the earth and were lavished upon it. Even Kali-yuga wretches could perceive the most attractive and powerful secret of the cosmos—devotional service.

Because of your *bhakti*, all your activities, like *cintāmaṇi* stones in Vraja, radiated a spiritual life of their own.

Unalloyed love of Kṛṣṇa is like an ocean of happiness. If someone gets one drop of it, the whole world can drown in that drop. It is not befitting to express such love of Godhead, yet a madman must speak. But even though he speaks, no one believes him. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 2.49\]

Because of your *bhakti*, our heart and mind endlessly drank of your every word and gesture.

Because of your *bhakti*, we chose to lay prostrate at your feet, and then, gazing upwards into your compassionate face, we could behold the wonders of *kṛṣṇa-prema*.

The exchange of spiritual happiness between Kṛṣṇa and His devotee in which Kṛṣṇa is controlled by His devotee is compared to an ocean of nectar into which the devotee and Kṛṣṇa plunge. This is the verdict of learned scholars who appreciate Kṛṣṇa’s opulence. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 19.229\]

Thank you for your wholly surrendering to Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura.

Yours is the *bhakti* that made a dimension of Vaikuṇṭha possible within this world of hype, hopes, and hellish mentalities.

Yours is the premier *bhakti* that multiplied into progeny *bhaktis*. Yours is the drum of devotion that keeps ISKCON marching.

Your consummate demonstration of disciplehood empowers us with a glimpse of real life as servants of Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* party.

Ecstatic love of Kṛṣṇa makes Kṛṣṇa and His devotees dance, and it also dances personally. In this way, all three dance together in one place. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Antya* 18.18\]

Aspiring to be your unconditional servant—if not in this life then in the next,  
Devāmṛta Swami

## Giridhārī Swami

**Separation**

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

The years go by, and you still teach us in many ways: your books, your conversations, your letters, and your devotees. Your teachings and those of your true followers inspire us, and thus we improve our *sādhana,* share your teachings, and serve your mission.

But a painful lesson still lies ahead: We must learn to feel separation from the Lord. Indeed, Caitanya Mahāprabhu came here to teach *vipralambha-bhāva*. At the same time, we are warned against false devotion and sahajiyāism. We remember well your this teaching of yours: “First deserve, then desire.”

Although true separation from Kṛṣṇa may be a long way off, Mahāprabhu teaches that we can still feel separation from Vaiṣṇavas.

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu asked, “Of all kinds of distress, what is the most painful?” Śrī Rāmānanda Rāya replied, “Apart from separation from the devotee of Kṛṣṇa, I know of no unbearable unhappiness.” \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 8.248\]

Over the past few years, Gour Govinda Swami Mahārāja, Tamāl Krishna Goswami Mahārāja, and more recently Śrīdhara Swami Mahārāja have left us. They were taken abruptly, and our hearts and minds went with them.

These dear servants of yours departed in Māyāpur-dhāma, each during an auspicious day and time. From this fact and their many years of service to you, we can be certain that you and your divine Lord have sheltered them. From that angle, we do not weep for their departure.

But when we think of their service to you, their wonderful qualities, and our friendships with them, we feel the weight of Śrī Rāmānanda Rāya’s words. Separation from a Vaiṣṇava is indeed unbearable.

Feelings of separation from the Lord’s devotees come first, before separation from the Lord Himself, and I am sure, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that you are teaching us the mood of separation through the loss of these great souls. If we cannot learn to serve the Vaiṣṇavas, to love them deeply, and to feel separation upon their departure, we will never feel separation from Kṛṣṇa. As the mercy of the devotees always comes to us first and the mercy of Kṛṣṇa follows, it is the same with feelings of separation: first the devotees, then Kṛṣṇa Himself.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for teaching us and guiding us. And thank you for giving this poor soul the opportunity to serve your dear servants, both present and departed. Please grant that I may always serve them with the utmost devotion and without offenses. Feeling separation from them makes me feel separation from you more deeply, and thus I can get a glimpse of what separation from Kṛṣṇa must be.

*Your humble servant in the mood of separation,*  
Giridhārī Swami

## Girirāj Swami

*You load sixteen tons and what do you get?*  
*Another day older and deeper in debt.*  
*St. Peter don’t you call me ’cause I can’t go:*  
*I owe my soul to the company store.*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

The above refrain from an old folk song came to mind while I was in the shower recently. The words complain of the miseries of *māyā*. Yet for us, the goddess Sarasvatī rendered new meaning to the lyrics, and in this way, the words have come to encapsulate my life . . . and my prayer to you on your Vyāsa-pūjā day.

*You load sixteen tons:*

I carry tons of self-imposed burdens. Some are internal: various *anarthas* and types of *avidyā,* led by pride. Others are external: various obligations and commitments to well-wishers, family, and friends. Still, you continue to bless me with your mercy—and lighten my load.

*. . . and what do you get?*  
*Another day older and deeper in debt:*

As you continue to bless me with your mercy in so many ways—especially through the holy names, the association of devotees, and devotional service—I get deeper and deeper in debt to you. And no matter how much service I may render, my debt increases, because the opportunity to offer service to you itself is your causeless mercy.

*St. Peter don’t you call me:*

One lifetime is not enough for me to repay my debt to you. In fact, no number of lives is enough. Therefore, Uddhava prays to Lord Kṛṣṇa:

*naivopayanty apacitiṁ kavayas taveśa*  
*brahmāyuṣāpi kṛtam ṛddha-mudaḥ smarantaḥ*  
*yo ’ntar bahis tanu-bhṛtām aśubhaṁ vidhunvann*  
*ācārya-caittya-vapuṣā sva-gatiṁ vyanakti*

“O my Lord! Transcendental poets and experts in spiritual science could not fully express their indebtedness to You, even if they were endowed with the prolonged lifetime of Brahmā, for You appear in two features—externally as the *ācārya* and internally as the Supersoul—to deliver the embodied living being by directing him how to come to You.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 11.29.6)

But although I will never be able to repay my debt to you, still, in this present lifetime I have some special obligations or opportunities to serve you: Because I had your personal association, I must share with others what I have learned in your company—from your words and example—especially by writing about you. And because you have instructed us to build the wonderful temple (*adbhuta mandira*) in Māyāpur as a service to the predecessor *ācāryas* and the Pañca-tattva—a colossal project that will take years to complete—I must add my mite to the total effort. So we need more time to render service to you here, in this life.

And personally, too, I need time. I need time to become a better devotee, a better example of *sannyāsa-dharma,* a more selfless servant of you and your mission. I need more time, in fact, so I can become more Kṛṣṇa conscious.

*I owe my soul to the company store:*

I owe my soul—my life—to you. You have given me life—devotional service in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. And so I must serve you with life and soul. But where are you now, that we may serve you? Of course, one answer is clear: You are manifest in your words, your instructions, your *vāṇī,* when we follow them:

\[T\]he spiritual master always keeps company with the disciple as long as the disciple follows strictly the instructions of the spiritual master. This is called the association of *vāṇī* (words). Physical presence is called *vapuḥ*. As long as the spiritual master is physically present, the disciple should serve the physical body of the spiritual master, and when the spiritual master is no longer physically existing, the disciple should serve the instructions of the spiritual master. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.28.47 purport)

Even more specifically, you are present in ISKCON. You even said, “ISKCON is my body.” To serve you also means to serve ISKCON. Thus, I owe my soul to “the company store,” to ISKCON—to you.

And so, on this most auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, I pray to you for sufficient strength and intelligence, health and duration of life, to serve you and your devotees, you and your mission, as you would want, here. And then—whatever.

We know, from authority and experience, your mercy triumphs over all.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

We beg to remain your everlasting debtor and  
*Your eternal servant,*  
Girirāj Swami

## Guru Prasād Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace, whose spotless character and qualities have inspired the whole world to embrace Kṛṣṇa consciousness!

As I did last year, I would like to continue my meditation on how you personify all twenty-six qualities of a Vaiṣṇava to the highest degree. I pray for your mercy in this effort, since I have no independent power to do so.

The next Vaiṣṇava attribute that we see manifest in your person is *nidoṣa,* totally faultless. In the *Bhagavad-gītā* Kṛṣṇa says that all endeavors are covered by some fault—*saha-jaṁ karma kaunteya sa-doṣam*. However, your activities are not only faultless but also remove all faults, sins, and inauspicious elements.

In *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya* 2.93) Śrīla Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī says:

*choṭa baḍa bhakta-gaṇa, vandoṅ sabāra śrī-caraṇa,*  
*sabe more karaha santoṣa*  
*svarūpa-gosāñira mata, rūpa-raghunātha jāne yata,*  
*tāi likhi’ nāhi mora doṣa*

**“I worship herewith the lotus feet of all kinds of devotees, both advanced and neophyte. I request all of them to be satisfied with me. I am faultless because I have written herein whatever I have understood from Svarūpa Dāmodara Gosvāmī and Rūpa and Raghunātha dāsa Gosvāmīs. I have neither added to nor subtracted from their version.”**

This is how you defined a pure devotee and how he achieves *nidoṣa,* his faultless state. You stated that it is not difficult to accomplish—just follow the previous *ācāryas*. Although this instruction is seemingly simple, you also alerted and warned us not to change things, so that your sublime teachings, indications, and instructions would remain transparent, and thus faultless. Therefore the irreproachable quality of your transcendental precepts echo your immaculate nature throughout the three worlds.

At the same time, we have a grave responsibility. We have to bear the burden of representing you perfectly, so that others perceive your unblemished activities and impeccable traits. Such is the mercy of a *mahā-bhāgavata:* he shares the task of demonstrating his perfect nature, thus purifying those who follow him.

I earnestly beg you to make me fit to be an insignificant cog in the perfect transcendental machinery you created to transform all living entities into eligible residents of the most perfect, faultless realm, Goloka Vṛndāvana.

*Your aspiring servant,*  
Guru Prasād Swami

## Hridayānanda dās Goswami

I offer my respectful obeisances at the lotus feet of His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

Each year at this time, we search our hearts and minds to discover and articulate the special form and manner in which the perennial flow of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mercy has manifest at this specific historical moment, both in the world and in our individual lives.

In devotional service, for all things there is a season. There is a time to act and a time to wait, a time to speak and a time to keep silent, a time to pray and a time to obey the answers to our prayers. In the *Bhagavad-gītā,* Arjuna, speaking to this general topic, asks Kṛṣṇa why, or in what circumstances, a realized soul will stay or go, or speak, and in what language. The Lord answers that in all circumstances, the enlightened soul will act without any selfish motive, that is, in pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

It is Śrīla Prabhupāda’s special mercy, which I have to come to realize more and more recently, that as the transparent via media to Lord Kṛṣṇa, he will show the sincere soul how to act, or refrain from acting, without selfish desire, solely for the pleasure of the Lord. This is of course the ultimate gift of Śrīla Prabhupāda: pure devotional service.

Thus we sing, *śrī-guru-caraṇa-padma, kevala bhakati-sadma:* “The lotus feet of Śrī Guru are the abode of pure devotional service.” Thus it logically follows that by serving those feet we achieve Lord Kṛṣṇa, free of the pain and foolishness of material existence.

On this most blessed day, the divine appearance day of Śrīla Prabhupāda, I pray that he continue to teach me the ways of pure devotional service, for only in such purity can we truly be useful to Śrīla Prabhupāda and thus to Lord Kṛṣṇa.

*His reluctant but persevering servant,*  
Hridayānanda dās Goswami

## Kavicandra Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances.

An Indian devotee recently told me how when his father left the business to his young sons, he told them that if there was good news they should write him, and if there was trouble they should take care of it themselves. I remember that in your last days with us you only liked to hear the preaching reports. So I will report some of the preaching events that really impressed me during this past year.

The biggest event, which everyone is talking about, was the installation of the most beautiful life-size deities of the Pañca-tattva. You ordered such deities long ago, and this installation is seen as a giant step toward building the Adbhuta Mandira. It was the biggest Māyāpur Gaura-pūrṇimā festival ever, and the devotees all expressed great happiness and enthusiasm to serve the Pañca-tattva by preaching all over the world.

Even the time leading up to the installation was so enlivening—the Nāma-haṭṭa preaching all over Bengal has been increasing beyond imagination.

Around that time another emotional event occurred. Your dear servant His Holiness Śrīdhara Swami left his body. By your causeless mercy he was well prepared and was able to be in Māyāpur in clear consciousness. In the weeks before leaving, he enlivened everyone, and the devotees exhibited great unity and love in caring for him. We are missing his association, but we know that you wanted him somewhere. The memory of such stalwart preachers is enlivening everyone. He was fearless in preaching and fearless in facing death.

There are more Jagannātha Ratha-yātrā festivals than I can list, and new ones are regularly coming. I especially have to mention the glories of the Ratha-yātrā festival in Durban, South Africa. It goes on for four full days and is so beautifully organized that I could only think of how pleased you must be. The devotees work all year to make each festival even better that the previous one. Everything is “better than first class.”

When you visited Durban you told the devotees to make a Ratha-yātrā and a temple. The temple is so famous that postcards of it are being sold in newsstands. And now they are building a second temple, since they have made so many devotees. I met one Godsister there who said how everything you did had so much depth. She was always thinking of the words *kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya* in your *praṇāma* prayer—you are “very dear to Kṛṣṇa”and trying comprehend the depth of such a statement. I could only agree with her.

Book distribution has increased, and the forecast for the coming year is that the increase will be much bigger. We hear so many stories of how your books have changed lives for the better. Many books have gone unread for more than twenty years but then turned even suicidal people into devotees.

The Russian government has given ISKCON 1.2 hectares of land in central Moscow for a Vedic temple. The site is very near Red Square, which we have seen in the a famous picture of you. We all understand that this is due to your strong desire and intense prayers to Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Your faithful servant Indradyumna Swami has continued to increase his Polish Hare Krishna Festival tour, which contacts many thousands of people every year. This goes on in spite of opposition. With the battle for the Juhu land you showed us how to push on no matter what.

There are so many other things, that if I were to try to list them all this offering would grow too long.

In my area of preaching, in spite of me, Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu has been working his magic. In Indonesia there are many devotees, and the book production and distribution are regularly increasing.

In Greece we now have a preaching center, book distribution has been revived, and new books are being published.

In Singapore, the only country that would not let you in, your influence is now being strongly felt: Nāma-haṭṭa congregational preaching is increasing daily. Many of your books are being distributed, and the devotees hold many *sat-saṅga* programs.

In Israel, with the help of some Druze people, devotees are seriously working on translating your books into Arabic.

The biggest things are happening in Nigeria, were I have learned that patience pays off. There are too many events to list here, but all of them are making the Hare Krishnas very well known. One devotee has become very much recognized amongst the academics. And the devotees have been given a daily television show in Lagos.

I have to mention Drutakarmā Prabhu, who has taken your desire to defeat Darwinism as his life and soul. His preaching of the “*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* version” is being accepted all over the world, and he is a featured preacher at many universities and archeological conventions.

All these things are due to your causeless mercy and pure desire to spread the mission of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. I heard that in London you said that if we could hold on to your *dhotī*you would get us in the “back door.” I pray that I will be able to do that. You told us to “Think big.” You exemplified that, and you showed us how to deal with all kinds of adversities while always depending upon Kṛṣṇa. Your books are keeping us on the right track, and we will go on trying to distribute them and read them more and more.

Your appearance among us is a blazing light in the dense darkness of Kali-yuga. Thank you. And although I know that I can never repay even a small portion of what you have given me, I will at least try and die on the battlefield.

Aspiring to really be  
*Your servant,*  
Kavicandra Swami

## Madhusevita dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to your divine appearance!

In the beginning of April I was asked to give the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class at the temple at Antwerp, Belgium. The verse was the first one of the first chapter of the Second Canto, where Śrīla Śukadeva Gosvāmī says to Mahārāja Parīkṣit:

My dear King, your question is glorious because it is very beneficial to all kinds of people. The answer to this question is the prime subject matter for hearing, and it is approved by all transcendentalists.

In the purport you explain how simply by coming in touch with *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and hearing about Kṛṣṇa one can become absorbed in the Supreme Lord and, upon being released from the miserable conditions of material life, go back home, back to Godhead. The comparison you make is with the waters of the Ganges:

Wherever the Ganges waters go, they purify the land and the person who bathes in them. Similarly, *kṛṣṇa-kathā,* or the topics of Kṛṣṇa, are so pure that wherever they are spoken, the place, the hearer, the inquirer, the speaker, and all concerned become purified.

During the class I was explaining to the best of my ability how powerful *kṛṣṇa-kathā* is, how Kṛṣṇa’s name and the words that describe His form, qualities, and activities can uncover the transcendental Lord, His associates, and His realm, and this can happen anywhere and everywhere.

On the banks of the Ganges, where Mahārāja Parīkṣit was fasting on the verge of death, this wonderful miracle of entering the transcendental dimension by hearing and chanting took place five thousand years ago, and for ages saintly personalities and pure devotees have been willing to undergo great austerities and penances to create occasions for *kṛṣṇa-kathā* to manifest.

At this point of the class, a minute realization of your greatness and position blossomed in my heart: You have been empowered by Kṛṣṇa to ignite *kṛṣṇa-kathā* in hundreds of millions of places simultaneously. Your absolute words in the form of transcendental literature have expanded the Supreme Lord, His associates, and His realm numberless times.

Śrīla Śukadeva Gosvāmī is speaking in millions of places on this planet right now, and Mahārāja Parīkṣit is also listening in those very same places. The banks of the Ganges and the Naimiṣāraṇya forest, along with hosts of pure devotees and sages, have taken up residence in London, Nairobi, Mexico City, Rome. They are in Mr. Brown’s house, as well as in Maria Solana’s kitchen and in the Vatican. No need to travel for thousands of miles to obtain transcendental association: Anyone can meet Kṛṣṇa with a multitude of loving pure devotees in any remote corner of this world by getting in touch with one of your pieces of literature.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are empowered by Lord Caitanya and Lord Nityānanda in a very special way.

After the class, while driving to Amsterdam, I remembered my “first” encounter with Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I had gone to London from Rome with some friends to spend the Christmas holidays; it was December 1969. The level of our superficiality would be very embarrassing to describe.

While walking on Oxford Street and talking all kinds of nonsense with my friends, all of a sudden I saw in front of me a shaven-headed boy dressed in peach-colored sheets who handed me a magazine. On the cover was a picture of a group of people like him in front of a big building, with someone sitting at the center of the group on a big chair. As I looked at the picture, the boy said something to me.

At that moment everything on busy Oxford Street faded away. The forms of the people and buildings were far in the background, and a deep silence fell within my mind: All I could see clearly was that magazine held by the hand of that boy.

Then a very deep voice within told me in Italian: *Sono tornato a prenderti:* “I have come back to pick you up.”

I stood motionless, unable to make even the slightest move or utter a word. Seeing no reaction on my part, the boy moved away from me and started offering the magazine to other people. I could not draw back my attention from him and kept standing in the middle of the sidewalk, motionless. After a couple of minutes, noticing that I was still standing there looking at him with rapt attention, he came back toward me with the magazine, at which point I found that, although I was somewhat overcome by anxiety, I could move again, and so I started running like mad to look for my “friends” again.

This all must have lasted a while, because the group had gone ahead a couple of hundred yards. I did not speak a word to them of what had happened, for fear of being ridiculed.

I had been touched by your literature, the form that *kṛṣṇa-kathā*had taken by your mercy, and even though I managed to run away that time, three years later I could not escape when your lotus feet entered Kathmandu in the form of your devotees speaking the science of Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you know how ungrateful and superficial I am even though so much undeserved compassion was bestowed upon me.

Please protect me so I may not become a source of embarrassment to your ISKCON society. Most important, please free me from unlawful desires for sense gratification and the *rākṣasas* of impersonalism and imitative behavior.

Your ISKCON devotees sometimes tend to get mixed up in mental speculation. Please protect them from this by giving them the realization that the simple methodology you have established for expanding Kṛṣṇa consciousness is, and always will be, the best way to open the doors of the spiritual world for everyone.

*tāṅdera caraṇa sevi bhakta-sane vāsa*  
*janame janame haya, ei abhilāṣa*
