**“Birth after birth I desire to serve the lotus feet of the** ***ācāryas*** **and live in a society of devotees.”**

Please keep me in the shade of your lotus feet. You are my only hope.

*Your unworthy servant,*  
Madhusevita dāsa

## Mālatī-devī dāsī

Dearly beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda, my lord and master,

I offer my grateful and humble obeisances unto your lotus feet.

By your infinite mercy, the entire world was graced with the extraordinary appearance of Śrī Pañca-tattva in the holy *dhāma* of Śrī Māyāpur this year.

It seemed as if they came in answer to your desire, assisted by the far-reaching vision of many sincere disciples who were blessed with the ability to grasp your desire and hold it deep in the core of their hearts. From this vantage point they were able to “do the needful,” empowered directly by you. I bow down to these great and sincere souls who were able to execute your desire and bring it to fruition.

I pray that what I witnessed this year during the installation period in Śrīdhām Māyāpur will forever be etched in my heart of hearts as testimony to your perpetual mercy upon this sad Kali-yuga world. Who can say that you are not the supremely empowered world *ācārya* of all *ācāryas*? Your mercy increases and expands even in your so-called absence. You are saving the whole world. Let the world wake up to this truth and live in the reality of Śrī Pañca-tattva, whose very presence defeats the fallen naysayers. You have kindly sent the all-powerful Lord with His eternal entourage to defeat the pernicious effects of Kali-yuga once and for all.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. May I forever remain in the shelter of your mercy as the lowest servant of your servants,  
Mālatī dāsī

## Mukunda Goswami

**Uncompromising and Ecumenical**

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept this observation, a state-of-play account of your movement today, as my offering on your Vyāsa-pūjā celebration.

It’s not easy for an institution to copy a person’s character. Nevertheless, that’s exactly what a lot of ISKCON devotees think they’re doing. Problem is, they often disagree.

One thing that has developed, as happens with most organizations, is the presence of a right and left wing, conservative and liberal. But the person Prabhupāda was both those things at once.

Creative tension is the kind, charitable, optimistic phrase some use to view the phenomenon that is a common inevitability of social development. But the next question often asked is, “How long will it take for ISKCON to be what Śrīla Prabhupāda wanted it to be and for differences to dissolve or be relegated to the ash heap of history?”

Many vainly ache for Prabhupāda’s return, praying for a second coming. Their wishful thinking sometimes takes the form of dreams or of wanting a return of the “good old days,” when things were simpler and easier and when people were oblivious to things like age and health and gender issues, societal positions and spiritual profiles and ashram statuses. Others thought and think that dedication to guru is all and all, that institutional concerns are secondary, at best.

Then we come to the mentally lazy or intellectually dishonest, those who are happy to rest their respective cases on epigrammatic words of wisdom, poetic passages that ring out the absolute truth. No one can dispute the bell-like clarity of these words: they resound with wisdom that spreads above in loudly ringing peals, and beneath as a toasty magic carpet. The wise words become plinths some make soapbox stands for all time, solid moving highways to the future. The database of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s words is surfed and cited to support private contention. But many times the results spawn division, fragmentation, and irrelevance.

There are those who believe that Śrīla Prabhupāda’s return is already in effect, that his transcendental presence in the form of his words is always with us. According to these devotees, we simply have to concentrate on and preach what he left us in order to get a clear idea of what to do. Why make things unnecessarily complicated? Let us focus more on substance rather than form. Maybe that’s the answer.

If qualities of unwavering strictness in devotional service, coupled with friendliness and the feeling of being equal to all, are the goals, one need look no further than Śrīla Prabhupāda himself, where we find embodied the two seemingly opposed qualities of uncompromising principles on the one hand, and openness and friendliness to everyone on the other.

But unfortunately, a political atmosphere arises almost inevitably in any institution. Some must be right and some must be left, some will be conservative and others liberal and others center, and some will be right and some will be wrong.

In nation states, peacetime political divisions take second place during warfare. And devotees are assumed to be always at war —with Māyā.

Śrīla Prabhupāda encouraged leaders to discuss their differences threadbare, until they reached a deeper understanding. This process is called *iṣṭagoṣṭhī*. Conclusions from this process may reach into gray areas discussed in “right and wrong” contexts, with the conclusions sometimes ending up in gray areas themselves. Among things discussed in *iṣṭagoṣṭhī* are topics like viewing certain films, watching specific television programs, reading named publications, visiting particular places, forming relationships, and eating.

In similarly uncertain situations, Śrīla Prabhupāda’s anecdotal behavior and words, though often tied to time, place, and circumstance, are frequently cited and seriously considered during *iṣṭagoṣṭhī,* in order to help reach proper conclusions. Such conclusions may appear nuanced and wordy, but carefully crafted conclusions are still in the realm of *sādhu-saṅga,* and, according to persons like Rāmānanda Rāya, *sādhu-saṅga* is one the most important practices of devotional service.

Among many other things, Śrīla Prabhupāda personified Rūpa Gosvāmīs teaching of *guhyam ākhyāti pṛcchati,* sharing intimacies with devotees. This may be another type of *iṣṭagoṣṭhī,* or even a form of counseling. But Śrīla Prabhupāda exhibited a wide variety of qualities in his personal behavior.

On days like his Vyāsa-pūjā celebration, I can only thank Kṛṣṇa again and again for allowing me to come into contact with Śrīla Prabhupāda, the living embodiment of all the philosophical works we adore and the walking book of *vijñāna* that beat poet Allen Ginsberg called “Hindu granny wisdom.”

A look, a gesture, a smell, a touch, a move, a sound was all it took to answer some of life’s most perplexing problems. These are the things not found in databases, and sometimes it is these very intangibles that show how it is possible to be strict and open-minded at the same time.

Śrīla Prabhupāda welcomed all, and proved that the enigmatic, impossible philosophical principle of simultaneous oneness and difference was possible. He told us that there was no love in the material world and yet was the most loving and caring personal figure in our lives. This was proof that the “impossible” concept of simultaneous oneness and difference existed. Ultimately, that proved to be the only philosophy that made sense.

A servant of ISKCON,  
Mukunda Goswami

## Niranjana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

On this most auspicious day of your divine appearance in this material world I would like to express my gratitude for the most precious gift you have given to me.

To the degree we realize the value of the gifts we’ve received, to that degree we can feel gratitude for what we’ve been given.

Knowing how happy it would make you, I would be very joyful if I could tell you that I’ve fully realized the value of *prema-ratana-dhana*, the jewel of divine love you so generously and mercifully gave to each and every one of your disciples. But Narottama dāsa Ṭhākura exposed me: “I have been robbed of this \[jewel\] by my own negligence.”

Still, despite my unceasing negligence, how is that I feel so much gratitude in my heart for what you have done for me? It seems to me that I have no qualification for this gratitude. I have not fully surrendered myself to your mission, I have not given up material ambitions, and I have yet to taste the pure holy name you so kindly bestowed upon me more than thirty years ago.

Despite my disqualification, obviously by your mercy alone my gratitude overflows in constant remembrance that it was you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who delivered this most precious jewel to me. It is also impossible to forget that it is you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who keep on pushing me by your presence in these remembrances, by your presence in your books, and by your presence in the hearts of your faithful followers, to give up my negligence and realize all that you want me to have.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for never forgetting me. Thank you for providing me with this most rare and fortunate opportunity to remain eternally indebted to you, despite your knowing full well that I am *adhane*, always in need of the required wealth to offer something back to you in return.

Your eternally grateful and indebted servant,  
Nirañjana Swami

## Paraṁ Gati Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear master,

As we observe the degrading situation of the world, and how the purports of your books perfectly explain the causes of all these terrible problems, the urgent feeling of how important it is to continue preaching and inspiring others to read your books grows stronger and stronger in us.

We see how the bodily concept of “I and mine” causes acute blindness among the people in general, and how eventually they become dominated by pride, anger, arrogance, and lust. We also see how prominent leaders, subjugated by the three modes of material nature, become unqualified to guide the population and, in due course, make decisions that result in disasters.

At some point we may think these problems have become unbearable, but when Kṛṣṇa sees the sincere desire and efforts of His devotees, He extends His hand to help us overcome the obstacles. Śrī Krsna satisfies the desires of His dear devotees, and therefore will certainly fulfill the desires of your sincere followers just as He fulfilled yours. And thus Śrī Caitanyadeva’s message will be understood and accepted by the people of this age.

The effects of this revolutionary *saṅkīrtana* movement may take some time to become obvious to the general population, but we know that a revolution in consciousness is taking place.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, may I continue to be your useful instrument for this mission.

Your humble servant,  
Paraṁ Gati Swami

## Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Once when you were walking on the beach in Jagannātha Purī in early 1977, you motioned with your cane and said to us, “I came here as a young man, just married, dressed in *khadi* cloth, and I was running on the beach. Now just see the position” (referring to the fact that you were walking with difficulty with the help of a cane). Then you stopped and turned to us and said gravely, “Do not think that this will not happen to you” (referring to the fact that old age and death will catch up with all of us).

You often reminded us of the harsh realities of material existence and stressed that the only solution is Kṛṣṇa consciousness, because Kṛṣṇa consciousness elevates one to the transcendental position, whereby one becomes fixed in the realization of the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. You showed us how to act in such a way as to be absorbed in the service of Kṛṣṇa every minute of every day, and thus transcend the misery and anxiety caused by the gradual (or not so gradual) disintegration of the external material body.

As the body grows older, we experience an increase in the number of our devotee friends and acquaintances who are leaving their bodies. On the one hand, it causes us grief and pain to lose their association. However, on the other hand we are awestruck by the strength of their spiritual advancement and Kṛṣṇa consciousness, by which they can stand on the head of death and blissfully proceed to the spiritual world, fixed in the surrendered realization that Kṛṣṇa is taking full charge of them. Their hearts and minds are flooded with the transcendental nectar of your instructions, and thus by your mercy they are able to attain the highest goal of life, going back to Godhead, back to Kṛṣṇa.

I thank you from the bottom of my heart, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us this most wonderful gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, by which all the miseries of material existence become insignificant and the spirit soul shines forth in its true glory. I pray that we may all be eternally engaged in following your transcendental instructions and thus become fixed at the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. May your fame be spread all over the three worlds and beyond.

Your worthless servant,  
Prabhaviṣṇu Swami

## Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa dāsa

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

For me to glorify you on this occasion means to report to you and give an account of what I have done during the last year. I am fully aware that all these activities are a part of your glory. It is actually you who are preaching to the whole world through the agency of your disciples, and we, your grand-disciples, are only assisting them in helping you in your mission of carrying out the *saṅkīrtana-yajña* of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Some of the projects in which I was insignificantly assisting His Holiness Rādhānātha Swami are as follows.

We have more or less completed the sandstone facade of the external walls of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha temple. Last year during your Vyāsa-pūjā, the inner walls of the temple hall were in an incomplete condition. They were being replastered. With the help of His Grace Matsyāvatāra Prabhu, we imported some very beautiful marble from Italy and used it for cladding the inner walls. All the wooden frames and fixtures have also been refixed. For your pleasure the temple hall now looks very beautiful. We have also added a nice fountain in front of the altar for the pleasure of Śrī Śrī Nitāi-Gauracandra, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopīnātha, and Śrī Gopāljī.

In December after the book marathon a group of 1,200 devotees went on a *parikramā* of Śrī Māyāpur-dhāma. It was a spectacular sight to see a cavalcade of twenty-two bus coaches and five other vehicles moving in a single file to the various spots in the holy *dhāma*.

Devotees from our temple were invited by the Times Foundation to impart values education to the students of regular schools in Mumbai. We started this program in six schools. Some of them are very prestigious. Our devotees go to these schools and teach values education once a week during school hours as a part of the school curriculum. We also conduct workshops on character and personality development for these students. As a result, now many other schools and even colleges are inviting us to teach their students the values of life based on the teachings of *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*. Consequently, now many students have started visiting our temple and participating in our regular programs.

In addition to the monthly youth preaching programs, namely Prerana for young boys and Chetana for young girls, we now also have a program exclusively for children between the ages of five and twelve.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

A servant of your servants,  
Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa dāsa

## Rādhānātha Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have blessed the whole world with the most beautiful gift of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Kṛṣṇa consciousness is the awakening of the original, natural love within the heart. You have taught us that the art of perfect love is to reciprocate with the supreme love of Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. Removing all impersonal tendencies from our hearts, you have offered us entrance into Kṛṣṇa’s eternal loving service. The mystery of unmotivated and uninterrupted loving service was unheard of before you descended into our lives. Lord Caitanya wove a wreath of the holy name and *prema* with which he garlanded the entire world.

This service was especially performed through Your Divine Grace. Due to my weak-heartedness, I am slipping from the path at every moment. My only hope is your causeless mercy upon this fallen soul. Your uncompromised preaching is like the lion’s roar, which causes my elephantine misconceptions to flee in fear. It is the inconceivable power of your forgiveness that enables me to continue on the path of *bhakti*. Please keep this helpless soul in the shelter of your lotus feet.

How can I reciprocate with your kindness? Your unparalleled compassion is my only qualification. It is the only hope for my spiritual survival. I pray to someday achieve the perfection of all aspirations: to gratefully reciprocate with your mercy as the all in all of my existence. I pray to sincerely offer my life and soul to be an instrument of your love. To surrender my heart as the truly humble servant of the servant of your servants. For a sinful dog like me to beg for such an exalted boon as this is certainly shameless. But what else can I possibly pray for after coming in contact with a drop of your oceanic love for Kṛṣṇa?

Śrīla Prabhupāda, with the current of Lord Nityānanda’s compassion flowing through your heart, you have expanded Lord Caitanya’s mercy in all directions, without considering who is fit or who is unfit. You came off the *Jaladuta* as a penniless, homeless mendicant. In a few short years you have mercifully implanted the seed of *bhakti* in the hearts of millions. Those who are most fortunate will wholeheartedly reciprocate with your love.

This year our dear Godbrother His Holiness Śrīdhara Swami Mahārāja departed from this world. His glorious passing was a special lesson for all of us. When he arrived at the gate in Śrīdhām Māyāpur, thousands of sincere devotees from all over the world assembled in *saṅkīrtana* to receive him. It was an incredible offering of love to your dedicated servant. Everyone felt Your Divine Grace presiding over this heart-melting experience. In his presence we participated in the historical installation of ŚrīPañca-tattva. By your prayers of compassion, Śrīla Prabhupāda, these extraordinary Deities have descended to deliver all the world. Śrīdhara Mahārāja offered the first *ārati* to Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Mahārāja brought joy to all of our hearts as he affectionately united devotees from all over the world to chant the Holy Names at his bedside. Just after Gaura Pūrṇimā, surrounded by the sweet *kīrtana* of the *mahā-mantra,* he graciously gave up his earthly body while lovingly gazing upon your photograph. In the glorious passing of Śrīdhara Swami Mahārāja you have once again revealed your infinite love for your followers. I pray that I may forever aspire to reciprocate with that most precious love.

Your servant,  
Rādhānāth Swami

## Rāmāi Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

One time in Melbourne you stated,

The materialists are working very hard because they are getting paid for their service. How is it that my disciples are also working hard and not getting paid? Actually, I am paying them, not with money but with love—love of God. Someone who is receiving the mercy of guru and Kṛṣṇa attains love of God and out of affection works much harder than any materialist.

Another time you said,

You are foreigners—you are Americans. I came from another country; I have no account. I cannot also order you: “You must do it, otherwise I will chastise you.” Because there is love; it is a connection of love. I can also become bold enough to chastise you, but you also, in whatever condition, you carry my order due to the basic principle of love.

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (Antya 20.37) there is this statement by Lord Caitanya:

*prema-dhana vinā vyartha daridra jīvana*  
*‘dāsa’ kari’ vetana more deha prema-dhana*

**Life without love is useless and poor, they say, Appoint me in Thy service, O Lord, with love as my pay.**

This principle of love is all-encompassing, for without love, nothing can be sustained. The materialists also work on this principle. Unfortunately, their original love of God has deteriorated into lust, and consequently they have to suffer due to the loss of their former position. “When love of God deteriorates into lust, it is very difficult to return to a normal condition. Nonetheless, Kṛṣṇa consciousness is so powerful that even a late beginner can become a lover of God by following the regulative principles of devotional service.” (*Bhagavad-gītā* 3.41, purport)

Most of us were late beginners, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Only by your mercy were we able to come to the position of devotional service. Although our attempts were feeble, just like those of children trying to serve their mother or father, you were so tolerant that you accepted that service, with love. What amazed me was how dedicated your disciples were to you. I had never seen such ardor before I became a devotee. In material life people go crazy about sports stars, film stars, and music stars, but their feelings are ultimately self-motivated and shallow. The love exhibited by your disciples, especially when you were personally present with them, was something extraordinary and exceptional.

Sanātana dāsa, a Vaiṣṇava poet, has written in his *Guru-vandana:*

*yāna prati gurudeva hana parasanna*  
*kona vighne seho nāhi haya avasanna*

**“Whoever courts the pleasure of Śrī Gurudeva in all respects will not become disheartened by any obstacles.”**

This is the test of real love, Śrīla Prabhupāda; a true disciple will love and serve you under any condition. This love is not like the so-called love of the material world, where someone “loves” another and then six months later hates the same person.

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Antya* 20.52) Lord Caitanya, in the mood of Śrīmatī Rādhārāṇī, says:

*nā gaṇi āpana-duḥkha, sabe vāñchi tāṅra sukha,*  
*tāṅra sukha—āmāra tātparya*  
*more yadi diyā duḥkha, tāṅra haila mahā-sukha,*  
*sei duḥkha—mora sukha-varya*

**“I do not mind My personal distress. I only wish for the happiness of Kṛṣṇa, for His happiness is the goal of My life. However, if He feels great happiness in giving Me distress, that distress is the best of My happiness.”**

When will we come to the position of basing our success and happiness solely on your pleasure, Śrīla Prabhupāda? The more we are freed from personal ambition, the more we come closer to you. It is said that a spiritual master should be accepted in full surrender, and that one should serve the spiritual master like a menial servant, without false prestige. If the servant of a house acts whimsically, the master becomes displeased and may throw him out.

Sanātana dāsa says:

*kṛṣṇa ruṣṭa haila guru rākhibāre pāre*  
*guru ruṣṭa haila kṛṣṇa rākhibāre nāre*

**“If one makes Kṛṣṇa angry, Śrī Guru can protect him, but if one makes Śrī Guru angry, even Kṛṣṇa cannot protect him.”**

From this statement we can understand how the spiritual master is so dear to Kṛṣṇa. Kṛṣṇa is God, but He doesn’t interfere in the relationship between guru and disciple. He has so much affection that He puts Himself in a subordinate position.

Sanātana dāsa says:

*guru pāda-padme rahe yāra niṣṭha-bhakti*  
*jagat tārite sei dhare mahā-śakti*

**“Whoever maintains unflinching devotion to Śrī Guru’s lotus feet possesses the power to deliver the whole world.”**

If the world is going to be delivered, Śrīla Prabhupāda, it can only be done by your mercy and only when we surrender to you completely. I pray that you please help us in our endeavor to serve you.

Your fallen servant,  
Rāmāi Swami

## Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my fallen obeisances at your lotus feet. May your glorious contribution to the welfare of the world become ever more manifest, as the seeds you planted, with so much effort, now sprout, grow, flourish, and bring forth a wondrous harvest. May your fame increase!

This is clearly a period of great challenge for your ISKCON and for those who serve your ISKCON. The challenges wear diverse guises, cloaking themselves variously in philosophical, organizational, legal, or financial garb. To meet them, we must unify ourselves as never before, in cooperative subordinate service unto you. If we are to prevail, we must trim our ship for battle, lightening our load by tossing unnecessary weight overboard. False ego must go. Material attachment must go. Distraction must go. Indecisiveness must go. Procrastination must go.

You told us several times, “There is no lack of anything except Kṛṣṇa consciousness. My Guru Mahārāja used to say, ‘There is no scarcity of anything in this world. The only scarcity is Kṛṣṇa consciousness.’”

This being so, it seems that we really face only one real challenge: to increase our supply of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. For all other endeavors to succeed, we must necessarily dedicate ourselves to this crucial endeavor: the proper cultivation of the holy name—chanting the names of God while earnestly striving to become free from offenses. In this way, we must join together under your shelter, through *vāṇī-seva,* serving you by strictly following your instructions and directions. This is our *saṅkīrtana*.

Kṛṣṇa has also promised that if we concentrate our minds exclusively upon Him, properly worshiping Him always with fixed devotion, He will supply us with all needed resources, tangible and intangible:

*ananyāś cintayanto māṁ ye janāḥ paryupāsate*  
*teṣāṁ nityābhiyuktānāṁ yoga-kṣemaṁ vahāmy aham*  
*(Bhagavad-gītā 9.22)*

This is my understanding of how we must execute your mission. I beg to be part of it.

I pray to you now, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to please give me your mercy. I am wholly unfit to be your servant, but even so you have picked me up. You have begun to reform me to your ends, but I am still a work in progress. You have not finished with me yet. Now, please, help me to become what you want me to be—

*Your eternal humble servant,*  
Ravīndra Svarūpa dāsa

## Romapāda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

My dearly beloved spiritual master Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble and prostrated obeisances unto your divine lotus feet. All glories unto Your Divine Grace!

The very special opportunity offered on your Vyāsa-pūjā day to unrestrictedly express your glories to the entire *saṅgha* of Vaiṣṇavas in this world who are taking shelter of your lotus feet brings great pleasure to the heart!

The most precious gifts of *vaiṣṇava-saṅga* and *nāma-saṅkīrtana,* these most essential *aṅgas* of *bhakti,* which in and of themselves can deliver one to the platform of ecstatic love of Godhead, even for a neophyte who has some slight attachment for them, are your most valued gifts to us.

*sādhu-saṅga, nāma-kīrtana, bhāgavata-śravaṇa*  
*mathurā-vāsa, śrī-mūrtira śraddhāya sevana*  
*sakala-sādhana-śreṣṭha ei pañca aṅga*  
*kṛṣṇa-prema janmāya ei pāṅcera alpa saṅga*

**“One should associate with devotees, chant the holy name of the Lord, hear** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,*** **reside at Mathurā, and worship the Deity with faith and veneration. These five limbs of devotional service are the best of all. Even a slight performance of these five awakens love for Kṛṣṇa.” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya*** **22.128–29)**

As our eternal loving spiritual father, you provided us with each of these most powerful *aṅgas* of *bhakti,* personally packaged with a ribbon and bow strung around them—even with our names attached to the ribbon! We had no idea what your gift was; we only knew that you loved us like nobody else ever had. Only over time did we come to understand more deeply what gift you had actually given us. You adopted us as your children, even though we were lost and forlorn, wandering around in some forsaken place in the darkness. How can we estimate your compassion? Just by being in your company and in your service, we discovered a level of joy in our lives that we had not previously known even existed! How can we ever repay you?

As Sārvabhauma Bhaṭṭācārya states in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* (*Madhya* 10.144), “The order of the spiritual master is very strong and cannot be disobeyed. That is the injunction of the *śāstras,* the revealed scriptures.”

In 1975 you gave your order to your disciples to establish a model Vedic city in Māyāpur, centered on a magnificent temple, an *adbhuta-mandira,* where Śrī ŚrīPañca-tattva would be worshiped by millions of persons from around the world for centuries to come. You had a vision, you imparted it to your followers, and ever-so-slowly that vision has been unfolding.

This year, on the 108th anniversary of your appearance, your disciples, under your order, arranged to have the deity forms of Śrī Śrī Pañca-tattva make their appearance in Śrīdhām Māyāpur. What an experience! The installation ceremony of the Pañca-tattva was like being in your physical presence once again! It was ecstatic!

I openly declare that the likeness of experiencing your personal presence in Māyāpur and the experience of the Pañca-tattva installation allowed me to realize in a deeper way than ever before what an intimate associate of the Pañca-tattva you actually are!

Now great service lies ahead of us—to build the temple you wished us to build. The massive waves of mercy now emanating from Śrī Śrī Pañca-tattva will provide us with all the added strength we need to carry out your orders. We just need to unite ourselves and harness their incredible mercy!

Challenges in fulfilling your orders are being provided from many sides. I can see very clearly that our unity as well as our purity of purpose are being offered both great opportunity for growth and great tests at the same time. Tests make those pure in purpose more pure in heart.

In meeting our challenges, and in furthering the fulfillment of your orders, I find an intense level of spiritual strength coming into my life through opportunities for the association with your surrendered devotees, both among my Godbrothers and among the many greatly surrendered devotees of the second generation. Again, this is your divine gift! You have given your orders how you would like us to serve you, and those who have no other purpose in their life but to make those orders manifest in today’s world are the pillars who uphold and glorify your wonderful ISKCON Society. Powerful and loving association of devotees is abundant for those who seek to make your orders and your mission manifest. All the resources we need are mined within those unique loving relationships. Again, thus is your divine gift!

Please count me as one who wishes to be in such association, giving every ounce of my strength and every breath of life to fulfill your mission until the very end.

I pray for you to fulfill this one request: please keep me forever in your service, in the association of your fully dedicated and surrendered loving devotees, protected from the pitfalls of deviations due to personal motivations and neglect of your divine instructions.

Wishing to be eternally in your service,  
Romapāda Swami

## Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

I think of Śrīla Prabhupāda as far away, but I always think of the distance with pain. I think of closing the gap as my highest priority, to come up to his standards. In the *Gurv-aṣṭaka*, Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura sings that the spiritual master should be treated as good as God because he is the intimate servant of God. And in the last verse of the *Gurv-aṣṭaka* he states, “Unless you please the spiritual master you cannot please Kṛṣṇa.”

I think of our spiritual master in a relationship of *acintya-bhedābheda-tattva* (simultaneously, inconceivably one and different). He is as close to us as our jugular vein. (Śrīla Prabhupāda once said this of the self and Kṛṣṇa. Another time, a new disciple stopped coming to classes and said he felt distant from Prabhupāda and Kṛṣṇa. Prabhupāda said the son is always close to the father. If the son says he is distant he is a rascal. And then he gave the *acintya-bhedābheda-tattva* example, in reference to both the guru and Kṛṣṇa.) If I write of myself, I write very close to that person, Prabhupāda. I don’t, I can’t live without him. An anecdote about myself is one about him or leading to him or pleasing him. I can’t separate myself from him. He is myself, in a sense, my master, my only. And I am his *celā*. I have written and spoken dozens of books about his specific pastimes and adventures and have repeated almost word for word his lectures as mine. If now I tell how “I” feel in processing him and his teachings, how I size up, adore, stumble in his path as *an individual,* that’s not “writing too much about myself.” I’m not a clone of Prabhupāda, nor are any of the *pāriṣadas* of *caitanya*- or *kṛṣṇa-līlā* carbon copies of the Lord. He enjoys their varieties of offerings.

At my present stage, Prabhupāda and Kṛṣṇa seem to enjoy putting me through ultimate tests. They are putting my iron through the fire to purify me into gold. It’s possible, just as Prabhupāda gave me this mantra in 1968 at the first ISKCON *brāhmaṇa* initiation ceremony, a verse by Sanātana Gosvāmī: “As bell metal is turned to gold when mixed with mercury in an alchemical process, so one who is properly trained and initiated by a bona fide spiritual master immediately becomes a *brāhmaṇa*.” (*Hari-bhakti-vilāsa*, quoted in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 15.108)

You can talk about yourself, even with false ego involved, if you sincerely are praising Prabhupāda in between the lines and on the line itself. A poem that builds to a climax, as long as Prabhupāda is in the final words that grab the reader by the back of the neck, may be better than a pabulum recitation full of “Prabhupāda, Prabhupāda, All glories to Prabhupāda”“Prabhupāda Prabhupāda Prabhupāda Kī Jai”without any pain of self. A confessional poem, however, delivers the shock of how much we needed him, how much we need him now, and how much more we will need, to attain his pleasing glance again.

I am crying for him,  
recalling him  
and working for him—  
that great innovator,  
that great aggressor  
who came to America  
and conquered many hippies  
like me. All glories to Swamijī eternally.

Satsvarūpa dāsa Goswami

## Śivarāma Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most respectful obeisances.

I write this offering as a heartfelt submission at your lotus feet—an offering of love—much preferable to a *lettre-de-rigueur.*The lines that follow are my humble attempt to glorify you as the inspirational example behind two important aspects of ISKCON’s mission. Śrīla Prabhupāda! I appeal to you for the empowerment to realize these aspects of your mission in my service to you.

I begin with your own words:

In late October of 1977 you said, “Anyway, do things very carefully. I am already dead. But still, I am giving you instruction as far as I can.”

Gopāla Kṛṣṇa Mahārāja answered, “You are still the inspiration for everything we do.”

To which you replied, “Yes. That I shall go on till the last breathing.”

Nearly twenty-seven years after pronouncing these words, you are still the inspiration for what we do—a sign that you have not yet taken your last breath. I shall now attempt to elucidate two of the many ways you continue to inspire your followers: as the perfect example of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and as the perfect preacher.

The grossly envious, or the deeply ignorant, may fail to recognize your perfect Kṛṣṇa consciousness, but sane men, truly enlightened men, cannot. Anyone who was in your physical presence can testify to the force of the pure devotion that emanates from your heart, a force that transformed thousands into devotees by its mere touch. Then there are your teachings—Kṛṣṇa’s teachings. Those who hear or read them can understand and feel the depth of your realization, realization that can come only from being in direct touch with Kṛṣṇa.

You were the personification of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

And your preaching record—still unequaled by sibling rivals—speaks for itself. Lord Caitanya’s preaching potency thundered in your being. Thus, you spoke constantly to a wide spectrum of listeners, while enthusing your followers to maintain and develop the preaching institution that you established. And that ISKCON lives today as the only institution with a constitution, a foundation, based exclusively on your instructions.

You were the ideal preacher of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

These two examples were two of the many perfect ones you set—examples that continue to inspire your sincere followers, and will for generations to come.

Yet you did not want your followers to deify your pure devotion or your achievements and then rest upon your laurels. You expected your followers to express their adulation of you not merely by words, but also by deeds. You wanted your followers to do as you had done, for what you had done was the practice of all realized devotees.

Devotees who realize that their eternal nature is to be His \[Kṛṣṇa’s\] servants practice and preach the eternal process of devotion.

Thus, you expected us to follow your example. You wanted your followers to be pure devotees and Sndor, you critiqued those who preached enthusiastically without a clear grasp of what they were preaching about. And you were even sterner with those who cultivated their own Kṛṣṇa consciousness and neglected the preaching.

Unless all of my students become very much fixed up in the philosophy and activities of Kṛṣṇa consciousness what is the use of so many programs for expansion?

Don’t be a *bhajanānandī*. As soon as you become a *bhajanānandī* you’ll be spoiled.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you wanted a balance of both *ācāra,* personal example, and *pracāra,* preaching. That balance was the sign of the perfect devotee, and the real guru.

Sanātana Gosvāmī clearly defines herein the bona fide spiritual master of the world. The qualifications expressed in this connection are that one must act according to the scriptural injunctions and at the same time preach.

And how did you define devotional practice and preaching?

In your view, to practice Kṛṣṇa consciousness was to know the philosophy of *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa without offense, to develop the qualities of a Vaiṣṇava, and to adopt the cultural habits of Vaikuṇṭha. Preaching meant to liberate the nondevotees by distributing books, publicly chanting the holy names, distributing *prasādam,* opening temples to the public, and so on.

And you wanted these two things *done.* You did not want them to be simply the objects of eloquent lip service.

What was the value of devotees lecturing about preaching and not actually meeting the public? And what was the value of preaching Kṛṣṇa consciousness without actually displaying such consciousness in one’s practical life? An institution that was obsessed by its internal affairs, that promoted a spiritual experience not followed by its practitioners, that relegated preaching to the realm of theory, would not stand the test of time.

You were emphatic:

You are doing your duty very nicely. That’s all right. But by doing your duty, if you do not develop your Kṛṣṇa consciousness, then you are simply wasting your time.

If you yourself remain always pure, then your preaching will have effect. As soon as there is little impurity, the whole thing will deteriorate and go to hell.

In emphasizing practice and preaching, you defined ISKCON as that society in which your pure devotee followers preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness. It was simple—for the simple.

By your example and by your mission you revealed your greatness. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you were, and continue to be, the greatest.

But the reality of that greatness will sink into the mire of empty words and staid celebrations if successive generations of your devotee followers do not also become great.

“Is it fitting to become great in Prabhupāda’s shadow? If so, how?” asks a follower.

I reply, “It is your duty to Prabhupāda to become great! And you become great by becoming a humble Vaiṣṇava who does ‘wonderful’ things to preach.”

To this I add, “And as the number of such great followers increases, so will the world’s recognition of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s supreme greatness.”

Śrīla Prabhupāda! You will measure the glorification of your greatness by the responsibilities your followers take to realize a permanent legacy of pure devotee preachers within ISKCON!

It was for such a legacy that you inspired us and for such an ISKCON that you breathed. So long as your followers continue to follow your instructions, you will continue to breathe and ISKCON will continue to live. If ISKCON were to stop following your instructions, then your “last breathing” would come, and ISKCON too would breathe its last.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I conclude with a prayer at your lotus feet: As Gaṅgā-devī provides her worshipers with water to worship her, please give me the ability to be your true follower, a practitioner and preacher who, always inspired by your example, measures up to your standards and meets your expectations. Only then will the words I have written and spoken in this offering and elsewhere really be worthy of glorifying you.

Your servant eternally,  
Śivarāma Swami

## Suhotra Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at the shelter of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

You have translated *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.10.12 thus:

*viśvaṁ vai brahma-tan-mātraṁ saṁsthitaṁ viṣṇu-māyayā*  
*īśvareṇa paricchinnaṁ kālenāvyakta-mūrtinā*

**“This cosmic manifestation is separated from the Supreme Lord as material energy by means of** ***kāla,*** **which is the unmanifested, impersonal feature of the Lord. It is situated as the objective manifestation of the Lord under the influence of the same material energy of Viṣṇu.”**

What we perceive as the universe (*viśva*) is the Lord Himself; and none other than the Lord Himself wills that this universe, which is never a thing different from Him, be yet separate from Him. In your Bhaktivedanta Purport to this verse you explain:

It is something like the tape-recorded voice of a person who is now separated from the voice. As the tape recording is situated on the tape, so the whole cosmic manifestation is situated on the material energy and appears separate by means of *kāla*.

*Kāla* means “time.” Lord Kṛṣṇa reveals Himself to be time in the *Bhagavad-gītā*. Thus the Personality of Godhead, His objective manifestation as the universe, and the time factor that distinguishes these two are all one . . . and yet different. This subject matter constitutes all that is essential for a human being to know.

I shall now declare unto you in full this knowledge, both phenomenal and numinous. This being known, nothing further shall remain for you to know. (*Bhagavad-gītā* 7.2)

Phenomenal knowledge (*jñāna*) reveals the universe to be the Lord in truth; numinous knowledge (*vijñāna*) reveals the transcendental Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa in truth. (The word *numinous* is rooted in the Latin *numen,* “nod,” which is said to suggest that phenomena go on by the approval of the divinity at remove, by His mere nod.) Lord Kṛṣṇa mercifully assumed the role of Arjuna’s spiritual master to establish the standard of education in phenomenal and numinous knowledge. Since this knowledge is originally revealed by the Lord, a bona fide spiritual master coming in disciplic succession with the mission to teach the same knowledge is to be considered a form of the Lord as His authorized representative. Only by receiving an education from Śrī Kṛṣṇa can a fallen soul rectify the mistake that keeps him bound to the cycle of birth and death.

*bhayaṁ dvitīyābhiniveśataḥ syād*  
*īśād apetasya viparyayo 'smṛtiḥ*  
*tan-māyayāto budha ābhajet taṁ*  
*bhaktyaikayeśaṁ guru-devatātmā*

**“When the living entity is attracted by the material energy, which is separate from Kṛṣṇa, he is overpowered by fear. Because he is separated from the Supreme Personality of Godhead by the material energy, his conception of life is reversed. In other words, instead of being the eternal servant of Kṛṣṇa, he becomes Kṛṣṇa’s competitor. This is called** ***viparyayo ’smṛtiḥ*****. To nullify this mistake, one who is actually learned and advanced worships the Supreme Personality of Godhead as his spiritual master, worshipful Deity, and source of life. He thus worships the Lord by the process of unalloyed devotional service.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **11.2.37)**

*Kṛṣṇa se tomāra,* sings Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura about the pure devotee—“Kṛṣṇa is yours and you have the power to give Him to me.”Śrīla Prabhupāda, Kṛṣṇa is so much yours that through Your Divine Grace He has sent forth a flood of mercy that covers the whole earth. Your power is such that not only are you giving Kṛṣṇa to every human being on this planet, but you are removing the ignorance that divides us from Kṛṣṇa. Just as Śrī Sudarśana Cakra with his twelve spokes sets afire the domains of the *asuras,* so you, in twelve tours around the globe between 1965 and 1977, set afire the worldwide edifice of modern godless science and philosophy. That fire continues to blaze to this very day. Flood and fire are what you’ve brought to the world, Śrīla Prabhupāda—a flood of mercy in the form of the cooling, thirst-quenching waves of *bhagavat-tattva-vijñāna,* and an unquenchable, roasting conflagration that consumes all opposing speculations.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on October 5, 1975, you gave a *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class in Mauritius. Afterward an Indian gentleman asked you if it is possible for one who has learned and understood this education to depart a few minutes later “in a very ridiculous way” from the teachings he received from God in person. Your reply to this gentleman was that his question was ridiculous, since only an uneducated person acts ridiculously. You clarified for him that a spiritually educated man like Arjuna might be disturbed by a material calamity such as the loss of his son (“that is temporary,” you said), but because he is educated he will never depart from the order of the Lord (“that is final,” you said, “that is his victory”).

Śrīla Prabhupāda, my prayer on this most auspicious day of your appearance is that before the end of this life I may be counted by you as one of your educated disciples. I pray that, even though I may be disturbed by some material reversal, I may never depart your lotus feet, that I may be convinced to act according to your order, and that I may be victorious by your divine grace.

Your humble servant,  
Suhotra Swami

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from Sannyāsīs

## Bhakti Balabh Purī Goswami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I offer my respectful obeisances at your lotus feet again and again. All glories to you and the many great Vaiṣṇavas you have made by your preaching and loving care.

Sometimes I have heard the statement “Let’s put Prabhupāda back in the center.” When I hear this, I feel a little sad, because for me and I’m sure many others *you are always the center.* There is no other center. You are the hub that the great wheel of ISKCON revolves around—the hub, the axle, which drives Kṛṣṇa consciousness throughout the entire planet, through the hearts and minds of all living entities. Without that hub, that axle, the wheel would not be connected and would therefore be unable to move or function. That great wheel of ISKCON has many spokes, and many wonderful decorations. But all are useless unless connected to that great driving axle giving all the power to the wheel, power coming from that great *saṅkīrtana* engine, the Pañca-tattva, and the great driver, Śrī Gaurāṅga Mahāprabhu.

You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, are always the center and always will be. Śrī Gaurāṅga put you in the center. You are the spiritual hub of the universe. You are the center, and no one can ever remove you; they can only become off-center themselves.

May the big wheel of ISKCON turn faster and faster and go further and further until it has traveled everywhere and spread the holy name and devotional service everywhere.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, *the eternal center.* May I always remain attached to that great wheel of ISKCON, of which you are the hub and driving axle.

*Your insignificant servant, the worthless*  
Bhakti Balabh Purī Goswami

## Bhakti Rāghava Swami

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

**“I was born in the darkness of ignorance, and my spiritual master opened my eyes with the torchlight of knowledge. I offer my respectful obeisances unto him.”**

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

**“By the mercy of the guru, even a dumb man can become the greatest orator, and even a lame man can cross mountains.”**

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances on this, the glorious 108thanniversary of your appearance day. May your glories continue to be spread far and wide all over the three worlds.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have set an unparalleled pace for introducing and pushing on the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya. By your relentless traveling, lecturing, writing, translating, guiding, advising, and correcting, you have awakened, inspired, and nurtured thousands of conditioned souls to take up the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Some of your most stalwart disciples and grand-disciples are following in your footsteps, accomplishing many wonderful feats, reconnecting countless spirit souls to the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa. Many wonderful preaching activities are taking place every single day, all due to your causeless mercy, as you continue to inspire all of us in pushing on the *saṅkīrtana* mission of Lord Caitanya. But in looking at myself, I feel personally very much lagging, slow, and despondent in assisting you in your *saṅkīrtana* mission . . . and time is ticking away.

I would like to do much more to serve you and your society, but in looking closely within my heart I sense a lack of strong commitment to pursue steadfastly your desire for establishing one of your most cherished projects: *varṇāśrama* communities and *varṇāśrama* colleges. You had such a clear vision about how this should be established, you spoke with such conviction on this most important topic, and you constantly reminded us, again and again, of the urgency of implementing it. Yet I am like a timid swimmer hesitant to jump into the water, and I feel unsatisfied at not getting my feet wet. Although you taught us that *varṇāśrama* was not the primary goal of our lives, you did clearly explain that it was a necessary means for the vast majority of our devotees to attain the real goal of life, pure devotional service unto Lord Kṛṣṇa.

I am somewhat at a crossroads, and you know my present situation. I would like very much to fully dedicate myself to this one main mission of helping promote and realize this great desire of yours. You knew very well that our present so-called modern civilization would soon perish due to not protecting the principles of dharma. Both America and Europe will perish, you explained, due to the single most sinful activity of not protecting one of the foremost pillars of dharma, Kṛṣṇa’s dearest devotees, the cows and bulls.

I have but a faint understanding of why the cows and bulls are more dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa than the *brāhmaṇas*. I can only surmise that they are present on this planet only to be engaged in pure devotional activities by providing liquid religiosity in the form of milk and all its precious byproducts, such as butter, yogurt, ghee, *barfī, sandeśa, kṣīra, rabrī,* and countless other preparations meant to be offered to Lord Kṛṣṇa and His devotees. The bull is Dharma himself, for he helps till the land, which can then produce the various foodstuffs meant to be turned into delicious dishes offered to the Lord. So both the cows and the bulls are meant to be engaged in such important direct devotional service for the pleasure of the Lord and His devotees. What to speak of the cows’ milk, which should be offered daily to the Lord, even their dung and urine should be used daily to cleanse our homes, wash our dishes, clean our teeth, and bathe our bodies, since these substances contain all kinds of wonderful medicinal and antibiotic qualities.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you wanted so much that your ISKCON society provide models for such a lifestyle so that the people in general can follow such pure examples of simple and pure living. Alas, we have little to offer in this respect, and both the cows and the bulls remain mostly unprotected. We should not feel that only those cows and bulls owned by devotees are our responsibility. In the same way that all living entities entangled in the modes of material nature are the concern of all Vaiṣṇavas, similarly, all the living entities in the bodies of cows and bulls should likewise be the concern of all Vaiṣṇavas.

I pray that I may serve as a menial instrument in helping you realize this dream of yours, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please forgive my frailties and shortcomings. Kindly give me courage to help you in your mission. I wish to one day become your fully dedicated and fully engaged servant, ever satisfied in carrying out your every instruction and desire. May you continue to inspire all your devotees on the path of pure devotional service.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your continued mercy.

Your servant,  
Bhakti Rāghava Swami

## Bhakti Viśrambha Mādhava Svāmī

*vṛndāvane ramaṇa-reti-prasiddha-bhūmau*  
*tatrāpi kṛṣṇa-balarāma-supāda-mule*  
*jñānaṁ paraṁ parama-kṛṣṇa-sudharmīty uktaṁ*  
*dantas tu deva prabhupāda namo namas te*

**“Again and again I offer my obeisances to Śrīla Prabhupāda, who is preaching the glories of Kṛṣṇa at the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa and Balarāma in the place known as Ramaṇa Reti, Vṛndāvana.”**

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

By your personal example you showed the world that only by satisfying the spiritual master through steadfastly preaching the message of Lord Kṛṣṇa while living in this world of names can one hope to please Kṛṣṇa and be promoted to the spiritual world.

You worked tirelessly to establish and spread this movement, proving this to be the genuine and proper path to attain one’s aspiration to serve once again in the company of Kṛṣṇa and His eternal associates in Goloka Vṛndāvana. You’ve mentioned and have demonstrated that this aspiration is not like ordinary, worldly aspirations. One must be prepared to purify oneself for many, many lifetimes to become qualified to attain such fortune. That platform is transcendental to the material modes of nature; thus that perfection is rarely achieved and is not to be confused with the temporary aspirations and so-called achievements in this *jaḍa* world.

In the *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu* Śrīla Rḹpa Gosvāmī gives a list of sixty-four items of *sādhana*. Regarding *sakhya* (48) and *ātma-nivedana* (49*)* he declares at 1.2.198:

*duṣkaratvena virale dve sakhyātmanivedane*  
*keṣāñcid eva dhīrāṇāṁ labhate sādhanārhatām*

**“Devotional service in fraternal love and devotional service in total self-surrender are two processes that are very rarely seen due to the difficulty involved in their realization. These are attained only by a few sober and exalted souls who have earned the right to act on such levels of** ***sādhana.*****”**

You write in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.12.5: “The Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is meant to train one how to transfer oneself from the *jaḍa* to the Brahman, because human life is meant for this purpose.”

You loved to quote the phrase *kṛta-puṇya-puñjāḥ* from *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.12.11, which you defined in the word-by-word meanings there as “all these boys, who had accumulated the results of life after life of pious activities.” In your purport to this verse you’ve nicely clarified matters:

*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is the easiest way of directly approaching Kṛṣṇa. *Īśvaraḥ sadyo hṛdy avarudhyate ’tra kṛtibhiḥśuśrūṣubhis tat-kṣaṇāt* (SB 1.1.2). Diverting even a little of one’s attention toward Kṛṣṇa and activities in Kṛṣṇa consciousness immediately enables one to achieve the highest perfection of life. This is the purpose of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. *Lokasyājānato vidvāṁś cakre sātvata-saṁhitām* (SB 1.7.6). The secret of success is unknown to people in general, and therefore Śrīla Vyāsadeva, being compassionate toward the poor souls in this material world, especially in this Age of Kali, has given us the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam. Śrīmad-bhāgavataṁ purāṇam amalaṁ yad vaiṣṇavānāṁ priyam* (SB 12.13.18). For Vaiṣṇavas who are somewhat advanced, or who are fully aware of the glories and potencies of the Lord, *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is a beloved Vedic literature. After all, we have to change this body (*tathā dehāntara-prāptiḥ*). If we do not care about *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* we do not know what the next body will be. But if one adheres to these two books—*Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*—one is sure to obtain the association of Kṛṣṇa in the next life (*tyaktvā dehaṁ punar janma naiti mām eti so ’rjuna*). Therefore, distribution of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* all over the world is a great welfare activity for theologians, philosophers, transcendentalists, and *yogīs* (*yoginām api sarveṣām*), as well as for people in general. *Janma-lābhaḥ paraḥ puṁsām ante nārāyaṇa-smṛtiḥ* (SB 2.1.6): if we can somehow or other remember Kṛṣṇa, Nārāyaṇa, at the end of life, our life will be successful.

Earlier this year I was blessed by one of your dear *sannyāsī* disciples, Bhakti Vikāśa Swami, who kindly sent me the purport cited above. *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 10.12.11 is one of my favorite *ślokas*. You show that one must not immaturely hanker for a perfection that is not manifest now. We must strive to follow your instructions to please Kṛṣṇa in the mood of the genuine *sādhakas,* who after pleasing Kṛṣṇa and guru become *sādhana-siddhas* worthy of rejoining the eternal service of Kṛṣṇa in the spiritual world.

In your purport to that verse you clearly mention the crucial criterion for success: attachment to proper hearing and chanting of such literatures as *Śrīmad Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*.

You translated that verse late in 1977. Your purport was a warning to all of us to prepare ourselves to take our final examination as gloriously as you taught us by your personal example.

But in showing us the way, you said to Haṁsadūta dāsa at that time: “Don’t think this \[departure\] will not happen to you.”

In your purport to *Bhagavad-gītā* 9.27 you clarify: “Thus, it is the duty of everyone to mold his life in such a way that he will not forget Kṛṣṇa in any circumstance.” Here you remind us all to remain vigilant throughout our lives that we are taking advantage of the knowledge contained in the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* in order to prepare ourselves to rejoin you in service to Kṛṣṇa in such nice ways in the spiritual world.

As of this writing, 220 of your direct disciples have been called back to your lotus feet to engage with you in the service of our Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Why didn’t their departure affect my stone heart?

Great servant generals of yours such as Śrīla Gaura Govinda Swami, Śrīla Tamāl Krishna Goswami, Śrīla Śrīdhara Swami, and Śrīmān Tribhuvanātha dāsa, among others, have already rejoined you. Why did their departure not effect in me a permanent, verifiably substantial devotional change? My heart must be steel-framed.

Now without them I must lament that I got either precious little or no opportunity of serving in their exalted company. Thus somehow or other I was robbed of getting their mercy and instructions at the end of their lives.

Thus in some ways these souls have remained distant from me, and I foolishly have remained aloof from effecting tangible change in both my devotional outlook and my behavior.

In the *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu* (3.2.25) we find this verse:

*kāmādīnāṁ kati na katidhā pālitā durnideśās*  
*teṣāṁ jātā mayi na karuṇā na trapā nopaśāntiḥ*

**“O my Lord, there is no limit to the unwanted orders of lusty desires. Although I have rendered these desires so much service, they have not shown any mercy to me. I have not been ashamed to serve them, nor have I even desired to give them up.”**

Each and every departure of every devotee in your movement, and especially those great souls you personally made your dear servants, should awaken in us powerful devotional feelings of commitment.

But alas, that did not happen to my lazy, foolish self in the past few years.

Lord Kṛṣṇa is directly the form of the powerful time factor, who takes away all. But you have always stressed that we must be careful not to misuse our minute independence.

I must make a substantial *saṅkalpa* to you this year, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I beg at your lotus feet for the strength to again pick up my life, which I originally offered to you at initiation on July 9, 1974, and July 10, 1975, by the grace of Viṣṇujana Swami and Tamāl Krishna Goswami.

I promise to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, upon the memory of all your departed servant generals and in turn to all the devotees in your movement:

(1) As a first step, I acknowledge my foolish mistakes. I apologize to you that I have wasted so much time during the last thirty-two years that should have been better utilized for you. I beg for your mercy so I may get the strength to improve.

(2) I will undertake a radical review of each day’s twenty-four hours, all of which belong to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, with a view to verifiably make each day more efficiently engaged in suitable Kṛṣṇa conscious ways. I will more thoroughly learn and follow the teachings of *Śrīmad Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* in order to better serve your movement and its servitors.

(3) I will strive to make and improve daily and weekly schedules that work and are efficient.

(4) I will strive more and more to be loving and sensitive.

(5) Because devotees are rare souls who belong to Kṛṣṇa, I will try to help them with their health concerns while at all times respecting their own judgments on these matters.

(6) I will take a radical look at my own health and make time-bound healing efforts.

(7)Realizing that my body belongs to you and your movement, I will maintain my health with the view of increasing my ability to to please you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and your devotees around the globe.

I must now surrender (as you stated you did in your 1936 Vyāsa-pūjā offering to your Guru Mahārāja) whatever minute independence I have at Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet by approaching your lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for you eternally sit at Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma’s lotus feet, giving us instructions how to serve Them.

*utsṛjyaitān atha yadu-pate sāmprataṁ labdha-buddhis*  
*tvām āyātaḥśaraṇam abhayaṁ māṁ niyuṅkṣvātma-dāsye*

**“O my Lord, O head of the Yadu dynasty, recently, however, my intelligence has been awakened, and now I am giving up my lusty desires. Due to transcendental intelligence, I now refuse to obey the unwanted orders of these desires, and I now come to You to surrender myself at Your fearless lotus feet. Kindly engage me in Your personal service and save me.”**

In his *Āmnāya-sūtra* Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura describes two kinds of disciples. One is *antar-mukha,* “internally facing,” i.e., devotionally contemplative, and the other is *bahir-mukha,* “externally facing,'” i.e., entangled in worldly matters and unserious.

The fortunate, *antar-mukha* disciples must all happily fall at the lotus feet of the Lord voluntarily through *paribhāvitā-bhakti* (SB 3.9.11), taking the blissful opportunity to step over Death upon leaving the body, as Dhruva Mahārāja did:

*yayottānapādaḥ putro muninā gītayārbhakaḥ*  
*mṛtyoḥ kṛtvaiva mūrdhny aṅghrim āruroha hareḥ pādam*

**“By hearing these topics from the sage \[Nārada\], the son of King Uttānapāda \[Dhruva\] was enlightened regarding the Personality of Godhead, and he ascended to the abode of the Lord, placing his feet over the head of death.” (SB 3.14.6)**

*tadottānapādaḥ putro dadarśāntakam āgatam*  
*mṛtyor mūrdhni pādaṁ dattvāārurohādbhutaṁ gṛham*

**“When Dhruva Mahārāja was attempting to get on the transcendental plane, he saw death personified approach him. Not caring for death, however, he took advantage of the opportunity to put his feet on the head of death, and thus he got up on the airplane, which was as big as a house.” (SB 4.12.30)**

But if *bahir-mukha śiṣyas* like me don’t change our foolish ways, the Lord may speak to us as Kṛṣṇa spoke to Nārada in his previous life:

*hantāsmiñ janmani bhavān mā māṁ draṣṭum ihārhati*  
*avipakva-kaṣāyāṇāṁ durdarśo ’haṁ kuyoginām*

**“O Nārada, I regret that during this lifetime you will not be able to see Me anymore. Those who are incomplete in service and who are not completely free from all material taints can hardly see Me.” (SB 1.6.21)**

Only by suitable application of the principles of devotional service in our lives will we hanker to taste the nectar of the *Bhāgavatam* in the company of great souls.

In *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya* 22.131, Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī quotes *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu* 1.2.90 as follows:

*śrīmad-bhāgavatārthānām āsvādo rasikaiḥ saha*  
*sajātīyāśaye snigdhe sādhau saṅgaḥ svato vare*

**“One should taste the meaning of** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **in the association of pure devotees, and one should associate with the devotees who are more advanced than oneself and who are endowed with a similar type of affection for the Lord.’”**

*Your struggling sevaka,*  
Bhakti Viśrambha Mādhava Swami

## Candramauli Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

This year during the Pañca-tattva installation festival in Śrīdhām Māyāpur, I received the good fortune of attending a talk given by your senior disciple Bhavānanda Prabhu. For many hours we were splashed and bathed with the nectarean flow of your amazing pastimes. In the intimacy of his association with you, Bhavānanda Prabhu described many sweet and rare moments.

He recounted one such incident in which your were reflecting on an exchange with Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa prior to leaving the spiritual world and descending among us rebellious souls.

You said, “This material world is a H-O-R-R-I-B-L-E place.”

Kṛṣṇa said, “You go and write some books.”

“It is a H-O-R-R-I-B-L-E place,” you said again.

Kṛṣṇa said, “You go and write some books and I will protect you.”

Out of your pure love for Kṛṣṇa and compassion for the wayward souls fallen into Durgā-dhāma you came to this h-o-r-r-i-b-l-e place. You were imbued with the full mercy and compassion of Lord Nityānanda Prabhu just to rescue us, who actually did not care for what you were offering.

In old age, with failing health, no money, no support, alone in a foreign land, you came, carrying the message of Lord Caitanya. With the order of Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī deeply planted in your heart of compassion, you persevered amidst the degradation of Western materialistic society.

Traveling around the globe fourteen times, you underwent unbelievable hardships—hardships that even younger persons would find unbearable. You showed us how a pure soul who is fixed on the order of the spiritual master can turn even the most apparently hopeless situation into a successful preaching event.

Whenever I enter into the temple and see you sitting on your *vyāsāsana,* gazing lovingly at Śrī Śrī Kiśora-Kiśorī, a feeling of renewed enthusiasm and conviction to persevere in my service engulfs me.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for saving me and for giving me the opportunity to take part in the grand mission of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. I beg to develop some tiny bit of your immense ocean of compassion, which is the embodiment of your being.

Your very fallen but hopeful disciple,  
Candramauli Swami

## Candraśekhara Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

As always, I need your help in all things. You know my daily struggles and my projects. You know my weaknesses. Kṛṣṇa consciousness is about many things, but it is especially about self-improvement and humble service. It is not so much about what others somewhere else did or did not do. It is about what I am doing in an effort to improve, what I am doing to push on the preaching. Of what use is it to me if others go nicely back to Godhead and I remain staunchly rooted in material existence? Who will gain if I remain lost to myself and useless to others? Kṛṣṇa consciousness is about praying to receive your mercy. Your mercy is all that I am made of.

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ’pi*  
*dhyāyan stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ*  
*vande guroḥśrī-caraṇāravindam*

\* \* \* \* \*

This year, in this Vyāsa-pūjā homage, I want to boldly ask you for your mercy and help in my main projects, prayer intentions, and struggles. Please help me upgrade my chanting of *japa*. Lately, my *japa* has been much better than in the last few years, but there is still such a long way to go. Now I am praying to you, the previous *ācāryas,* and the collective Vaiṣṇavas to give me their mercy and make me fortunate. You have said that, of all the things we do, *japa* is the most essential for purification. I am not pure, but chanting can make me pure. I do believe it, but struggling to chant attentively is a daily commitment. Most mornings I chant at your lotus feet, before the *vyāsāsana,* trying to remember to leave my mind and all its wanderings behind. I preface the chanting with obeisances to you, always trying to connect deeply with the meaning.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale . . .*

We recite the *Śikṣāṣṭaka* every morning, and I try to meditate on the instructions of Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Definitely, I have no taste, I am unfortunate, but I am committed to the effort. The ten offenses are my real challenge. When I look back on my life, I see that I have made many offenses. In some cases I cannot even figure out where those I have offended presently reside, so how can I offer an apology? Please help me. Give me the benediction of mentally participating in the daily activities of devotional life. What use is dry ritual? What use is absent-minded service?

\* \* \* \* \*

The North American temple presidents and the GBC body have asked me to help revitalize book distribution in North America.

*kali-kālera dharma—kṛṣṇa-nāma-saṅkīrtana*  
*kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana*

What I need is an abundance of your mercy to make this project successful. It is your will and it is Kṛṣṇa’s will. How can it fail to come to pass? Please make me an instrument.

\* \* \* \* \*

New Vṛndāvana is a huge project. What will it take to develop New Vṛndāvana? I am not sure I know. I have some ideas. The devotees that manage New Vṛndāvana are working so hard to make it stable and financially secure. I do want to help; let me understand how. At least my prayer intentions for those in New Vṛndāvana go out daily.

\* \* \* \* \*

I know so many devotees in need. They are in need of emotional support, they are in financial difficulty, going through life changes, and they have health problems. For me to make a significant difference in their lives will also take your mercy. Please give me the determination to consistently beg for that mercy.

\* \* \* \* \*

The world is full of people who have never heard of Kṛṣṇa. Please let me desire and find effective means to reach them. I think that preaching could be more than it is now, both from the perspective of my own efforts and from the perspective of our collective efforts.

\* \* \* \* \*

Please help me keep our philosophy in focus and my presentation of it free from mistakes and cheating. Let me help preserve the tradition and work to remove traditionalisms that seek to obliterate the real essence. Please help me strive to protect myself and others from the constant onslaught of concocted ideas, whether ill-intended or honestly mistaken. Please help me focus on real issues and not be concerned with trivialities. Moreover, please help me be an example of a servant embodying the sacred principles that you have taught.

\* \* \* \* \*

Please help me in my music projects. They are intended to express my prayers to you and Kṛṣṇa. They are established for the purpose of promoting the preaching work and culturing the inner expression of my heart. These projects are an area of my life where I need your inspiration and guidance. Please let them be successful if you want, or let me recognize that I need to redirect my effort in another direction if that is really what you want.

\* \* \* \* \*

Please let me recognize that my own life is slipping away, day by day. Do I really want to stay here in this place? If you want me to preach here, I will be content to stay. But let me not want to stay because I really want something here. Have I not made that mistake enough times?

\* \* \* \* \*

*Kṛṣṇa-kathā* is the main part of our interchange with others. Let me be determined to upgrade my knowledge and willingness to speak of Kṛṣṇa. Again I commit to spending more time reading your books and those of other pure Vaiṣṇavas.

\* \* \* \* \*

Please let me be yours in all things, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Let me be a servant.

Your servant,  
Candraśekhara Swami

## Dānavīr Goswami

To His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya 108 Śrī Śrīmad Abhaya Caraṇāravinda Bhaktivedānta Svāmī Mahārāja Prabhupāda on the 108th anniversary of his appearance day in this world.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Kindly accept my eternal obeisances at your divine lotus feet.

The degradation of Kali-yuga continues, but your books escalate in relevancy, addressing the needs of modern human society. As the true *bhakti-vedānta,* you gave the original text and full *paramparā* explanation for the *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. Thus you established the law books for mankind, books that will create “a revolution in the impious lives of this world’s misdirected civilization.”

Some appreciate your emphasis on understanding the difference between matter and spirit. Others marvel at how you transplanted pristine Vedic culture worldwide. Still others find your presentation of the Supreme Personality of Godhead as Śrī Kṛṣṇa to be the ultimate explanation of the divine.

In addition to explaining these, you took the risk of being labeled “unscientific” by siding with Śrīla Śukadeva Gosvāmīs version of cosmology over that of modern astronomers and scientists. Similarly, although you knew that some might consider it proselytizing, you included the entire *mahā-mantra*—Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma, Hare Hare—twenty eight times in your as it is *Bhagavad-gītā* to emphasize the only means of success for the Age of Kali.

Although far ahead of your time, you declared the attempts to reach the moon and Mars by spaceship to be lunacy. Despite its unpopularity, you repeatedly stressed the importance of organizing society according to *varṇāśrama* principles. To the chagrin of religionists and academics, you continuously reminded all that the Vedic literature was compiled five thousand years ago by Śrīla Vyāsadeva. And in the face of a world of decadence, you never slackened on your insistence of sexual abstinence for serious spiritual aspirants.

Disseminating your books and teachings obliges sincere followers to truly understand, explain, and sometimes defend them. Your books need not be softened, smoothed, trimmed, or embellished to gain popularity. Gold is gold, and one who knows the value will appreciate. It is the responsibility of the leaders of ISKCON to uphold this reality.

Your unique brand of *bhakti*—what ISKCON is made of—has proven effective and shall continue to be so.

With the permission of the devotees from the Veda Trust (aka Prabhupāda Rasamrita Trust), who distributed the books, we present the following small selection of recent appreciations of your *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* from American university libraries.

You have secured for yourself a tremendous historical legacy at our library. Hopefully, students will find your works to be interesting and helpful. I will use many of them in preparation for class discussion.

—Central Missouri State University

Our Religious Studies department has a strong Asian religions component, and the members of that department seem quite excited. . . . One Professor in particular plans on using some of these volumes in the upcoming semester.

—Grinnell College

We have changed our Religion and Ethics requirement to a course called Sacred Texts. Our chaplain, assistant chaplain, and members of the department will certainly want to use the books as part of their unit on religions of Asia.

—Groton School

An excellent collection on the culture of India. These volumes, including Srimad-Bhagavatam, are valuable additions to the religious, philosophical, and cultural collections of the Humanities, Social Science, and Education Library. Libraries at Purdue will benefit. . . . We will be more capable of achieving our mission, and serving the students and faculty of the Purdue community.

—Purdue University

These unique volumes will be great assets to the Boston Library Consortium, of which Boston University is a part.

—Boston University (Mugar Library)

These volumes are truly remarkable—beautifully bound and illustrated. I know that our students, faculty, and other scholars and researchers will treasure these volumes for many years to come.

—University of Cincinnati

The 18-volume set, SRIMAD-BHAGAVATAM, by A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, is most comprehensive, as you indicated. We are pleased to add this to our collection.
