—Georgetown University (Lauinger Library)

These materials on the Culture of India, featuring traditional Vedic literature, enrich our collection. We are in the process of adding these to our collection, from which they will be available to users locally, statewide, nationally, and internationally.

—Ohio University

Our students and faculty will value these new translations and commentaries \[the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*\]. They will become the centerpiece of our collection on Indian philosophy and religion.

—Colby College

The books \[Śrīla Prabhupāda’s collection, including the eighteen volumes of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*\] are very beautifully produced, and will be of interest to students in many academic departments. The faculty member who specializes in Vedic philosophy is particularly grateful. . . . Now he can direct students to a broader range of primary Vedic literature, which until now has been represented in our collection by excerpts and commentaries. We look forward to seeing the books well used by generations of students and faculty.

—St. Mary’s College of Maryland

This is a very scholarly edition of Srimad-Bhagavatam which does an excellent job of elucidating the difficult text of Bhagavad-gita As It Is. It should be valuable to Western scholars. We are delighted to have the opportunity to add A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda’s Works to our collection.

—Louisiana State University

These works on the Culture of Vedic India are a welcome addition to our collection. We have an active Department of Religious Studies here at the University and our students will make very good use of the materials.

—University of Kansas

I want to take this opportunity to thank you on behalf of the students, faculty and the staff of the Texas Woman’s University.

—Texas Woman’s University

*Your eternally grateful servant,*  
Dānavīr Goswami

## Dhanvantari Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble and respectful obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

You descended to this world as a *śakty-āveśa avatāra,* without depending upon this world to build your happiness and your intimate relationship with the Lord.

Upon arriving in the West, you were completely prepared to distribute the *bhāgavata* message, being fully surrendered to Kṛṣṇa with your heart, words, and body (*hṛd-vāg-vapurbhiḥ*). You opened your heart to understand the specific problems of the conditioned souls of this place, preached about the Absolute Truth, and showed the perfect example of a devotion to Kṛṣṇa both flawless and suitable for the place, time, and circumstances you found yourself in.

Among your many instructions, I would like to comment on one now to celebrate your Vyāsa-pūjā. And that instruction is that your followers must systematically study your books.

This instruction reveals your authentic nature as a spiritual master, as described in the *Prema-bhakti-candrikā: divya-jñāna hṛde prakāśita.* “The spiritual master reveals transcendental knowledge in the heart.”

Although I feel myself related to the functional reality of this material world, by your causeless mercy I have been able to understand the significance of the knowledge you have given us in terms of regaining inner satisfaction—an issue that is so controversial and much discussed among intelligent and learned people nowadays.

*na hi jñānena sadṛśaṁ pavitram iha vidyate*  
*tat svayaṁ yoga-saṁsiddhaḥ kālenātmani vindati*

**“In this world, there is nothing so sublime and pure as transcendental knowledge. Such knowledge is the mature fruit of all mysticism. And one who has become accomplished in the practice of devotional service enjoys this knowledge within himself in due course of time.” (*****Bhagavad-gītā*** **4.38)**

In a general way, ignorance makes everybody believe that sense satisfaction may bring them their desired happiness.

The most confidential knowledge revealed in the *Bhagavad-gītā* by Śrī Bhagavān is that we must always occupy our mind in thinking about Kṛṣṇa and undertake a proactive attitude to become His devotee.

The sincere souls who approach your movement are seriously interested in this, but they still do not know exactly how to be happy. Considering that happiness arises in the heart of those who attain transcendental knowledge, your instruction to systematically read your books will lead your followers “to enjoy this knowledge within themselves,” that is, it will bring them their desired happiness.

Trying to obey your specific instructions on studying your books and paving the way for the devotees in their quest for true happiness, I have gathered a few youths (male and female) and taught them how to study your books in a systematic, academic way. The results have been outstanding. Many of them now show by their very faces that they are attaining an inner happiness.

Please accept my service as a humble attempt to appreciate and obey your wise instructions.

Your eternal servant,  
Dhanvantari Swami

## Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Big Faults—Little Faults**

In deference to the old saying “We only confess our little faults to persuade people that we have no large ones,” I could offer my annual contrite confession. But you, who are as good as the all-pervading Supersoul, already know my “large” ones. From you there can be nothing hidden. So for whose benefit shall I perpetrate the mistaken image of an honest, humble gentleman by itemizing the “little” ones? Then, shall I shock the virtuous readers with a parade of psycho-pathology for all to marvel at? Alas, they would probably consider it yet another expression of modesty! Let me therefore spare your valuable time (and theirs) by not engaging in such matters of self-indulgence. Instead, I have compiled a short poetic offering in appreciation of Your Divine Grace.

**Thoughts of Hell**

When thoughts of hell came newly to me,  
All that was sacred, embraced in ecstasy,  
Forever perished, these ears no more to hear  
Prabhupāda’s sweet singing—so dear, so dear.

Luxurious *bhajanas,* affluent in majesty,  
Petitions Gaura-pahou, “O Master of mercy.”  
I’ve listened to him sing it thousands of times;  
Turns hades to heaven, whatever the clime.

His eyes closed, thoughts mysterious and free,  
So sober and grave—true yogic *samādhi*.  
Peace amidst turmoil—Himalayan mind,  
Heavy in countenance, yet of golden kind.

“Kṛṣṇa and sex life—just two choices,” he taught.  
Like Yāmunācārya—learn to “spite” at the thought.  
Māyā’s last stand, the Illusion’s grand test.  
Prabhupāda brought Kṛṣṇa to God-forsaken West.

Life’s greatest attachment—small family of two.  
Hearing *Gurvaṣṭakam*—his devotion to Guru,  
Blessed voice pulled me up, high above those modes,  
Like being strapped to a ’copter—broken all the codes.

I know that Govinda I would never have chose,  
Except that I met him, I was just born to lose.  
He gave me the mantra—“Lord Kṛṣṇa and Śrī,”  
Come hell or high water—just chant eternally!

And now how do I prove appreciation of he  
Who sacrificed, like Jesus, life’s blood and energy?  
Though freely given, said, “Now share what you’ve got.”  
And though I can’t repay him, trying not, in hell I’ll rot.

So many relationships this life I have destroyed,  
Debts and obligations, countless beings annoyed.  
No time to turn around, too late to back the clock,  
My only hope’s to get his books amongst the student flock.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I only pray that you please overlook all my “big” and “little” faults, and allow me some credit for this one service, insignificant though it may be, that I repeatedly attempt to offer you.

*Your somewhat reluctant servant,*  
Gaṇapati dāsa Swami

## Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Recently one of my Godbrothers sent me a photo I had never seen before. In it Your Divine Grace is touring the BBT warehouse in Los Angeles, and some devotees and I are accompanying you. I am walking very near to you, visibly relishing every second. I can imagine how fortunate I must have felt, for I had always wanted to get as close as possible whenever I was in your association. When I was by your side I never felt any fear. I was secure, faithful, convinced, detached, and filled with enthusiasm. You were my eternal father, and after so many lifetimes I relished so much that I was finally in your association.

That photo reminded me of the opportunities I had to accompany you on your morning walks. I always experienced a great excitement when chosen to go on one of these special excursions, especially when I envisioned how glorious it would be if I somehow wound up walking by your side, watching your every step, hearing your every word, and seeing the wonderful expressions on your face. I think often of your walks in Los Angeles. At 6 a.m. all the devotees would wait in great anticipation for you, chanting outside next to the temple, at the foot of the stairs leading up to your rooms. Then suddenly the door would open and you would appear with a big smile on your face, the smile that always lit up the world and brightened our day thousands of times over. We would all loudly cheer, “Jaya Śrīla Prabhupāda!” as you walked down the stairs. Then we would form a line on either side of you and offer flowers and obeisances at your lotus feet as you proceeded to the car.

I distinctly remember one very memorable walk on Venice Beach. That day I had an especially intense desire to get very near you and drink in all the nectar of your association. There were many devotees present, however, and all of your more senior disciples had taken their places on either side of you. I thus had to settle for tagging along on the outskirts of the party, far to the side. It seemed as if I were miles away, and my transcendental anxiety was increasing at every moment. But then I got an idea! Nobody was walking directly behind you! “Now is my chance,” I thought, and I literally jumped at the opportunity and positioned myself just inches behind you. How privileged I felt to be walking there! Now with merely a little straining and stretching forward, I could hear every word you were saying. Finally I was where I always wanted to be, drinking in all the ambrosia of your association. But just then something happened that I will never forget. As I was trying my hardest to absorb every drop of nectar, I carelessly stepped on the back of your shoe, which, as you began to take your next step, came off your foot. You stopped in your tracks and bent to put it back on as I stood there motionless, in a state of shock. Then you straightened up, looked at me, waved your hand as if to push me away, and said, “Walk further back!”

One often learns one’s lessons the hard way, and unfortunately this lesson was at your expense. How much have I had to suffer as a result of my self-centered attitude that day, and how many countless times have your words, “Walk further back, walk further back,” echoed in my ears.

Over the last few weeks I have often looked at that photo of your visit to the L.A. BBT warehouse and become absorbed in the reverie of those very special moments I spent with you there. Invariably a type of sadness sets in as I lament how it is no longer possible to sit at your feet, walk by your side, or even see you at a distance. Yes, by your mercy I have clearly understood that no matter how physically close a disciple is to his spiritual master, if he is offensive or negligent, as I was that day on the beach, his relationship will remain distant. But even at that distance, your association was so powerful and sweet that I still long for it always. And I can’t even imagine how life-giving your personal presence would be if I had a truly intimate connection with you through humble, dedicated, selfless, and unswerving service. Thus I pray for the strength to kick away my constant companions of the desire for adoration, distinction, and personal gain and accept you instead as my only friend. I pray that you continue to correct me for my foolish behavior. And I fervently pray that one day I can endear myself to you so much that you cast your transcendental glance on me just as you did that morning on Venice Beach, only this time saying “Come closer, come closer!”

Your aspiring servant,  
Guṇagrāhi dāsa Goswami

## Kadamba Kānana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

I am a disciple of your disciple, and your mercy continues to live in my spiritual master. How can I say that I never met you in person, when no one in the entire world influenced my life as much as you did?

Who can understand the glories of your blessings in the past, how much we depend on you in the present, and what more you will do in the future? We worship you every day, but we have no idea who you really are! You continue to affect the hearts of many around the world; some might have just met you somewhere, and others became your dedicated disciples and followers, who would do anything on your order. It is through people that Kṛṣṇa consciousness will spread all over the world, far into the future.

Toward the end of last year I met a visitor in Māyāpur, a man in his sixties from America. He was an artist and dressed the part, with a beret and a Salvador Dali walking stick. Every day he could be found in the temple, from early morning until evening. One day I was talking with him and he explained that he was trying to spend as much time as he could in the temple. He would complete seventy-two rounds of *japa* by the noon *ārati* and would stay in the temple for all the *āratis* throughout the day. While walking in the temple and meeting different Vaiṣṇavas, he would fold his hands and offer respects.

So I was wondering where he got the strength for all of this, and one day I asked him, “Who are you? Are you a demigod or something?” He simply gave one of those secretive smiles and said “Maybe!”an answer that still didn’t unravel the mystery.

Then not too long ago I received an e-mail from him:

I remember the scene in the east village  
back in the 60’s . . . , the acid was flowing  
we used to go to happenings  
impromptu frolicking . . . ,  
anyway, saw this beautiful old guy . . . , saw  
his people, saw how much he changed them  
I knew that he was dangerous . . . ,  
only to be viewed from afar . . .

*Vaiṣṇavera kriyā-mudrā vijñeha nā bujhaya.* Who can understand what blessings you gave him in a moment’s association? (*Lava-mātra sādhu-saṅge sarva-siddhi haya.*) Here was a hippie from the Village in New York, and later San Francisco, who saw you but did not surrender. He tried to keep a safe distance, but you captured him anyway. He saw how you preached, and as a result he developed strong faith. He nonetheless chose Māyā! But that faith never left him, and eventually it matured and brought him to Māyāpur at an advanced age.

How fortunate this devotee was to have seen you back in the sixties, what to speak of the devotees who saw you then and actually *did* surrender their lives to you at that time. You took those boys and girls from 26 2nd Avenue and San Francisco, lifted them up, and engaged them in pioneering work to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness all over the world. And whenever we meet them, they can only talk about you. Their love for you is above anything else in their lives, and by their association we feel more and more drawn to you, even more than we already were. By the time I came to Kṛṣṇa consciousness so much was already there—so many had joined, and we took advantage of their service. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I bow down to you unlimited times, and I bow down to all those who surrendered to you and dedicated their lives to fulfilling your desire.

Finally, I am noticing the effect of all the devotional service that I’ve been allowed to do. It’s been a lot of struggle from “Day One,” what with distributing books on the street and then working on big projects in austere India—all the heat and hard work. But little by little I am learning the most difficult thing: how to establish and maintain loving dealings with people. Through that service I can feel how your mercy is chiseling away at my stonelike heart, and I am beginning to realize more and more that it is all about pure love for Your Divine Grace and Kṛṣṇa.

Please allow me to always remain in your service.  
Kadamba Kānana Swami

## Keśava Bhāratī dāsa Goswami

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most respectful obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet, which to this day are giving shelter to the whole world.

Those of us who were present for the installation of the Pañca-tattva Deities in Māyāpur this year were fortunate enough to see, in an extremely wonderful way, your unique potency to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

As devotees hovered around those five gigantic golden forms, performing various services, like bumblebees savoring the nectar of lotus flowers, and as the *kīrtana* of thousands of devotees, led by empowered ISKCON *kīrtanīyās,* one after the other, escalated into thunderous waves that rolled through Māyāpur and around the world, the thought kept flooding into my mind: How wonderful it is that you were able single-handedly to adjust things, just as the Pañca-tattva did, in such a way that anyone, from any national, religious, economic, or cultural background, could taste the sweetness of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

The Pañca-tattva broke open the storehouse of love of God, plundered it, and distributed it freely to everyone, without discrimination. No one was ineligible to receive and taste *kṛṣṇa-prema.* And that’s just what you did, Śrīla Prabhupada. I was seeing it before my eyes, twenty-seven years after your so-called departure from this world—devotees from all over the world tasting the ecstasy of serving together the most wonderful forms of the Pañca-tattva ever installed on the earth. Someone would have to have been blind not to have seen who you are on that day, Śrīla Prabhupāda. It was too obvious, even to such a fallen soul as me.

Gone were any political differences, philosophical differences, economic differences, bodily differences, or past differences—and among one of the most diverse groups of devotees I had ever seen. It was truly an amazing spiritual experience, to see the power of *harināma-saṅkīrtana* in action in such a dramatic way.

We will never be the same after that experience, as we will never be the same having personally witnessed the mystical way you single-handedly spread the pure chanting of the Holy Names throughout the world. You were proud of the fact that your movement was not known by your own name. You were proud that people knew your movement as the Hare Kṛṣṇa movement, because you always pushed Kṛṣṇa forward, not yourself. That was your humility. Let others try to imitate you and claim exclusive rights to your legacy or to advanced levels of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in their own names. Your legacy was there for all to see—the day the Pañca-tattva appeared in that most wonderful form—in the faces of everyone present. Tears flowed unrestrictedly, devotees danced madly, embracing one another in ecstatic happiness. It was as if you were personally there with us, Śrīla Prabhupāda, lifting your hand ever-so-slightly, to indicate that we should chant louder and louder and dance higher and higher, lifting your head high in that charming way in which only you could express the deepest humility and gratitude.

So now, as you always did when you were with us physically, you have turned up the heat. Who knows what will be the long-term effects on our movement and the rest of the world as the results of that installation and the *kīrtanas* that accompanied it gradually sweep over the world, inundating it with renewed love for chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa.

We can declare it boldly, then, that all of us, especially from the Western countries, practicing Kṛṣṇa consciousness were delivered and still are being delivered only by your potency and loving mercy. I pray for the day when all will see that and openly glorify you as the uniquely empowered representative of the Pañca-tattva, the only one who could spread the Holy Name in such an unrestricted way.

We pray to stay directly in front of the tidal wave of love of God you initiated, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We pray to be inundated in that wave and to be empowered to do whatever is required to help you continue to deliver the world in its darkest hour.

Hare Kṛṣṇa.

Begging for an eternal place in the shade of your lotus feet, I remain, as always,  
*Your insignificant servant,*  
Keśava Bhāratī dāsa Goswami

## Krsna dasa Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to you, the holder of the torchlight of knowledge, on this, your Vyāsa-pūjā day!

In the *Bhagavad-gītā* Lord Kṛṣṇa describes how rare is a person who actually knows Him:

*manuṣyāṇāṁ sahasreṣu kaścid yatati siddhaye*  
*yatatām api siddhānāṁ kaścin māṁ vetti tattvataḥ*

**“Out of many thousands among men, one may endeavor for perfection, and of those who have achieved perfection, hardly one knows me in truth.”**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are that rare personality who has not only achieved perfection but who also knows Śrī Kṛṣṇa in truth. But you are even more than that: not being content with knowing Kṛṣṇa just by yourself, you made that knowledge available to one and all through your very potent books. Through your books, people all over the world have been given the opportunity to also achieve perfection and to also know Kṛṣṇa in truth.

The ability to do this on the scale that you have done it is not an ordinary event. Your success shows that you have truly received Śrīla Vyāsadeva’s blessings. The need for such mercy is confirmed by Caitanya Mahāprabhu in His conversation with Rāmānanda Rāya:

*prabhu kahe,—‘sādhya-vastura avadhi’ ei haya*  
*tomāra prasāde ihā jāniluṅ niścaya*  
*‘sādhya-vastu’ ‘sādhana’ vinu keha nāhi pāya*  
*kṛpā kari’ kaha, rāya, pābāra upāya*

**“Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu said: ‘This is the limit of the goal of human life. Only by your mercy have I come to understand it conclusively. The goal of life cannot be achieved unless one practices the process. Now, being merciful upon Me, please explain that means by which this goal can be attained.’” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya*** **8.196–197)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given everyone the opportunity to understand and reach the ultimate goal of life, to go back home, back to Godhead, and render loving devotional service to Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa.

Preaching is becoming more and more difficult as the Age of Kali advances day by day, and so the mercy of a pure devotee, the mercy of Your Divine Grace, is needed now more than ever before. Therefore, please continue to shower your mercy upon all of us, so that we may know who Lord Kṛṣṇa is, what the ultimate goal of life is, and how we may achieve that ultimate goal of life.

I remain your humble servant,  
Krishna dāsa Swami

## Mahāviṣṇu Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, I am so fortunate to have a spiritual master like you who have done such unprecedented preaching service for Kṛṣṇa. How dear you must be to Kṛṣṇa! I am only just some flotsam pulled along in the wake of your preaching. Please excuse me for offering your own words back to you. I hope it will please you on this occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā day.

In a *sannyāsa* initiation lecture in Bombay on November 18, 1975, you spoke these sobering but elating words:

So you are taking this pledge for serving in front of Kṛṣṇa, Vaiṣṇava, *guru,* and fire. So you shall be very much cautious not to forget your duty. You have got good opportunity. You are going to Africa to deliver \[the fallen persons there\]. . . .

But everyone can be delivered. *Kirāta- hūṇāndhra- pulinda- pulkaśāābhīra- śumbhā yavanāḥ khasādayaḥ ye ’nye ca pāpā.* These are known (as) sinful life. But Śukadeva Gosvāmī says, “There may be others which is not mentioned here.”*Ye ’nye ca pāpā yad-apāśrayāśrayāḥ:* “If they take shelter of a Vaiṣṇava, ”*śudhyanti,* “they become purified. ” So you have to become very rigid Vaiṣṇava; then you will be able to deliver them. *Śudhyanti.* How they can be purified without taking another birth? Yes. *Prabhaviṣṇave namaḥ.* Because Vaiṣṇava is going to deliver them, by the power of Viṣṇu they become empowered. So practically we have seen last time when I went to Nairobi, so many, these Africans, they are making progress very nicely. They are making nice questions. They are following rules and regulation. So African people, they are not so much sophisticated or so-called civilized to forget God. But if you work sincerely and if you can deliver one person only by your endeavor, then immediately you become recognized by Kṛṣṇa. *Na ca tasmād manuṣyeṣu kaścid me priya-kṛttamaḥ.* This is the quickest way to become recognized by Kṛṣṇa, by preaching work.

So be seriously . . . You take. This business is very nice, and there is good prospect. Prospect is there for everywhere. It is not that . . . *Pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi-grāma.* Caitanya Mahāprabhu said that as there are as many villages and as many towns on the surface of the globe. So His word is not a trifle thing. Yes. There is potency in all the parts of the world to become Vaiṣṇava.

*pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi-grāma*  
*sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma*  
*\[CB Antya-khaṇḍa 4.126\]*

So with Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s blessing and spiritual strength and with the good wishes of Vaiṣṇavas, just proceed and preach and always think of Kṛṣṇa. He will help. *Buddhi-yogaṁ dadāmi tam*. If you sincerely work . . . Kṛṣṇa is within yourself. He will give you strength, instruction. Simply you have to become . . . But we should always remember that *sannyāsa* means material activities finished. This is *sannyāsa:* no more material activities. Only for the service of Kṛṣṇa to live, that’s all. (aside:) Give the *daṇḍa*. So this *daṇḍa,* there are four *daṇḍas*. *Daṇḍas* mean “rods.” One rod is body, one rod is mind, and one rod is speaking. *Kāya māna vākya*. And the fourth rod is the self. So you should always remember that taking this *daṇḍa* means that “My mind, my body, and my words—everything is dedicated to Kṛṣṇa. It will be used only for Kṛṣṇa and for no other purpose.” This is called *tridaṇḍa-sannyāsa*. Come on.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, in one short sentence here you show you understood the African people exactly. “So African people, they are not so much sophisticated or so-called civilized to forget God.” But you were also strategically minded like Hanumān in Laṅkā. Unaffected by sentimentality and illusion, you pinpointed your main world target. On September 17, 1965, you stole into New York City, the very heart of Kali’s “sophisticated”“so-called civilized” nation. As the bold commander-in-chief-devotee from Lord Gaurāṅga’s army, you dropped your transcendental-life–giving–book-bombs with deadly accuracy right into our laps. Just see, Śrīla Prabhupāda, how you hit us big, big *narādhamas* so hard on your morning walk in Māyāpur on April 1, 1975:

Why they struggle? Why do you fight? Because you are all thieves—you want to steal God’s gold—therefore you fight. Why do you fight? Why you declare war from one nation to another? Because you are all thieves and you want to steal God’s gold. Therefore there is fight. Why do you claim, “This is America, my land”? Is it your land? Have your forefathers created this? Why you falsely claim like that? What is the answer? You have stolen from another party, Red Indians, and you are now claiming, “We are the proprietor.” You are thieves. You are not even gentlemen. What do you think?

There in Māyāpur, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you had us all squirming with guilt. But still with good-humored affection you maintained your friendly relation with us: “What do you think?” Because you were the genuine servant of *patita-pāvana* Lord Nityānanda, you could love and glorify even us big, big thieves:

I am very pleased that already you have held fifty festivals in the colleges. *Param vijayate sri-krsna-sankirtana.* And *param vijayate* American boys. Yes, you are doing very nicely. So many Vaisnavas have taken birth in America. Now America is going to be glorified. So many people are afraid of the American CIA. But now they will welcome Americans as American Vaisnavas. Now maintain your status quo. You are in a very good position. To manage a sankirtana party nicely is more precious than all other activities. That is the verdict of Bhagavad-gita and the mission of Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahaprabhu. \[Letter to Tamāl Krishna Goswami, 13 November 1975\]

But on September 14th, 1977, you returned to India from your last mercy tour to the UK. Your tour was cut short by ill health. It was clear you were now our ailing father. This was your last international tour. By destiny I was there in the Juhu guesthouse hallway when you arrived and took the lift. I stood alone by the lift and you looked straight at me as you struggled passed. I was shocked at your skeletal features. Then our eyes met very intensely for a moment. It was as though you had a special message for me alone. My sinful eyes directly met and felt your powerful gaze for the last and most personal time. Your last *darśana* seemed to say:

My life is ending soon. I sacrificed everything to give Kṛṣṇa to all of you. I tried my best for my Guru Mahārāja, and by Kṛṣṇa’s grace I have started something here for Lord Gaurāṅga. The ball is rolling, so now keep it moving on. But now my body is collapsing. Lord Kṛṣṇa, as inevitable time, is taking me away from you very soon. You are my disciple and son. You are still young. It’s up to all of you now. The mission is yours. How much more can I do? Take responsibility and just go on pushing this preaching work as I have taught you. Do your best for me now; do not spoil it with self-interest. In this way you will please Kṛṣṇa. This is the way of success. Carefully take this legacy from me now and come back to me. I am waiting for you with Kṛṣṇa.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, can you remember that time you looked at me? Am I right? Śrīla Prabhupāda, my real father, please forgive me though I am unfit and the lowest of the low. I still want to be recognized by Kṛṣṇa somehow. Please give me the spiritual strength and intelligence to try again to serve you by preaching well before my time is up. Please guide me how to please you wherever my *sannyāsa* duty takes me—especially in Africa, and also in the countries of the biggest *narādhamas,* “the lowest of mankind,” where I was born.

*Your most fallen disciple,*  
Mahāvishnu Swami

## Prahlādānanda Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

If we present things as they are, it will be accepted. Without any adulteration. Sometimes, you know, people say that I have done miracles. They say everywhere. But I do not know anything, miracles or magic. If there is any miracle, that miracle is that we present things as they are. That’s all. Without any adulteration. So that should be the principle. Present as it is. It will be accepted. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* lecture, Vṛndāvana, 22 October 1972\]

What is the difficulty in presenting things as they are? For one thing, a conditioned soul has no direct experience of reality. How can someone perfectly present things as they are when he or she is not fully situated on the transcendental platform and therefore does not truly know how things really are? The illusionary energy, consisting of the three modes of material nature, covers the conditioned soul’s perception of existence. When the soul acts according to the modes of material nature, the illusionary energy creates a new conception of life for him. Only one who is always acting under the influence of the internal energy of Lord Kṛṣṇa can be fixed in a transcendental conception of life and thus be capable of perfectly presenting reality to others. Aspiring devotees may repeat the words of authority according to their realization, but until they become mature in the process, their perceptive experience of reality remains incomplete and their revelations partial.

There are two kinds of instructing spiritual masters. One is the liberated person fully absorbed in meditation in devotional service, and the other is he who invokes the disciple’s spiritual consciousness by means of relevant instructions. Thus the instructions in the science of devotion are differentiated in terms of the objective and subjective ways of understanding. The *ācārya* in the true sense of the term, who is authorized to deliver Kṛṣṇa, enriches the disciple with full spiritual knowledge and thus awakens him to the activities of devotional service. \[*Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Ādi* 1.47\]

Hearing from a transcendental person about the truth and the process by which this truth is revealed is the beginning of spiritual enlightenment. If we try to understand such teachings and instructions and put them into practice under proper guidance, surely the Lord will be pleased, and He will give us spiritual intelligence (*dadāmi buddhi-yogaṁ tam*). If we honestly repeat what a self-realized soul such as Śrīla Prabhupāda has taught, in proportion to our purity our preaching will have effect. However, an aspiring devotee should not imitate a devotee on the absolute platform; otherwise there will be problems.

When a person realizes himself to be an eternal servitor of Kṛṣṇa, he loses interest in everything but Kṛṣṇa's service. Always thinking of Kṛṣṇa, devising means by which to spread the holy name of Kṛṣṇa, he understands that his only business is in spreading the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement all over the world. Such a person is to be recognized as an *uttama-adhikārī,* and his association should be immediately accepted according to the six processes (*dadāti pratigṛhṇāti,* etc.). Indeed, the advanced *uttama-adhikārī*Vaiṣṇava devotee should be accepted as a spiritual master. Everything one possesses should be offered to him, for it is enjoined that one should deliver whatever he has to the spiritual master. The *brahmacārī*in particular is supposed to beg alms from others and offer them to the spiritual master. However, one should not imitate the behavior of an advanced devotee or *mahā-bhāgavata* without being self-realized, for by such imitation one will eventually become degraded. \[*Nectar of Instruction* 5\]

Sometimes a less advanced devotee may take up the role of a spiritual guide for others, but Śrīla Prabhupāda writes in the same purport:

One should not become a spiritual master unless he has attained the platform of *uttama-adhikārī*. A neophyte Vaiṣṇava or a Vaiṣṇava situated on the intermediate platform can also accept disciples, but such disciples must be on the same platform, and it should be understood that they cannot advance very well toward the ultimate goal of life under his insufficient guidance.

Taking the role of a spiritual master without an honest and proper understanding is dangerous. A person who is not self-realized may become bewildered by the praise and service which are expected to be offered to someone who is truly an advanced devotee. Śrīla Prabhupāda uses the example of a big fire and a small fire:

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** Yes. A devotee must work under the direction of a bona fide spiritual master, not imitate the best devotee.

**Bob:** Excuse me?

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** One should not imitate the best devotee.

**Bob:** Imitate. Oh. I see.

**Śyāmasundara:** One time you said that sometimes you feel sickness or pain due to the sinful activities of your devotees. Can sometimes disease be due to that? Caused by that?

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** You see, Kṛṣṇa says, *ahaṁ tvāṁ sarva-pāpebhyo mokṣayiṣyāmi māśucaḥ:* “I will deliver you from all sinful reaction. Do not fear.” So Kṛṣṇa is so powerful that He can immediately take up all the sins of others and immediately make them right. But when a living entity plays the part on behalf of Kṛṣṇa, he also takes the responsibility for the sinful activities of his devotees. Therefore to become a guru is not an easy task. You see? He has to take all the poisons and absorb them. So sometimes—because he is not Kṛṣṇa—sometimes there is some trouble. Therefore Caitanya Mahāprabhu has forbidden, “Don’t make many *śiṣyas,* many disciples.” But for preaching work we have to accept many disciples—for expanding preaching—even if we suffer. That’s a fact. The spiritual master has to take the responsibility for all the sinful activities of his disciples. Therefore to make many disciples is a risky job unless one is able to assimilate all the sins.

*vāñchā-kalpa-tarubhyaś ca kṛpā-sindhubhya eva ca*  
*patitānāṁ pāvanebhyo vaiṣṇavebhyo namo namaḥ*

**“I offer my respectful obeisances unto all the Vaiṣṇava devotees of the Lord. They can fulfill the desires of everyone, just like desire trees, and they are full of compassion for the fallen conditioned souls.”**

He takes responsibility for all the fallen souls. That idea is also in the Bible. Jesus Christ took all the sinful reactions of the people and sacrificed his life. That is the responsibility of a spiritual master. Because Kṛṣṇa is Kṛṣṇa, He is *apāpa-viddha*—He cannot be attacked by sinful reactions. But a living entity is sometimes subjected to their influence because he is so small. Big fire, small fire. If you put some big thing in a small fire, the fire itself may be extinguished. But in a big fire, whatever you put in is all right. The big fire can consume anything. \[*Perfect Questions, Perfect Answers*\]

To advance the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, people have to surrender to a spiritual authority. However, if the spiritual authority assumes a position that he or she is unworthy or incapable of maintaining, there will be difficulty.

However, Lord Caitanya’s causeless mercy is such that He advised all bona fide spiritual masters to speak about Kṛṣṇa consciousness everywhere. Therefore, in the line of Lord Caitanya even the *sannyāsīs* can speak about Kṛṣṇa consciousness everywhere, and if someone is seriously inclined to become a disciple, the *sannyāsī* always accepts him. The one point is that without increasing the number of disciples, there is no propagation of the cult of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Therefore, sometimes even at a risk, a *sannyāsī* in the line of Caitanya Mahāprabhu may accept even a person who is not thoroughly fit to become a disciple. Later on, by the mercy of such a bona fide spiritual master, the disciple is gradually elevated. However, if one increases the number of disciples simply for some prestige or false honor, he will surely fall down in the matter of executing Kṛṣṇa consciousness. \[*Nectar of Devotion,* Chapter 7\]

For preachers in ISKCON to honestly present things without adulteration, they first of all have to objectively know what level of spiritual achievement they have attained. The GBC body has the duty to see that leading devotees especially are not acting or preaching inappropriately, beyond their platform of actual realization. Then the teachings of the *ācāryas* that are coming down through our founder-*ācārya* will not be distorted by ignorance or selfish motives. Śrīla Prabhupāda, speaking about unqualified gurus, said to Tamāl Krishna Mahārāja:

Stick to our principle, and see our GBC is very alert. Then everything will go on, even I am not present. Do that. That is my request. Whatever little I have taught you, follow that, and nobody will be aggrieved. No *māyā* will touch you. Now Kṛṣṇa has given us, and there will be no scarcity of money. You print book and sell. So everything is there. We have got good shelter all over the world. We have got income. You stick to our principles, follow the . . . Even if I die suddenly, you’ll be able to manage. That’s all. That I want. Manage nicely and let the movement go forward. Now arrange. Don’t go backward. Be careful. *Āpani ācari prabhu jīvere śikhāya*. \[Room Conversation, Bombay, 22 April 1977\]

In order for the preachers of ISKCON to be effective they must present without adulteration the teachings of the *ācāryas* coming down through Śrīla Prabhupāda. Job descriptions and assessments are perhaps the doorways to dutiful behavior and purified actions, which can lead to detachment and transcendental knowledge, which can lead to pure devotional service and full Kṛṣṇa consciousness. In this way the preaching will become ever more pure and effective.

Prahlādānanda Swami

## Puruṣatraya Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this glorious day when we celebrate the one hundred-eighth anniversary of your appearance, it is my ardent wish to offer you an homage, an homage which is worthy of the immeasurable spiritual dimension of your divine personality. On the auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, all your sincere followers—disciples and disciples of your disciples—as well as all who appreciate your qualities and acts, become touched and enthralled by recalling your extraordinary deeds, exceptional wisdom, and incomparable love toward God and all creatures. Many, many lives have been transformed by your mercy. Mine included.

My homage is, indeed, very simple. This is due to my smallness and fragility. I will try, however, to make an effort to be sincere. I intend to extract from my heart the deepest feelings and search my intellect for words that express the greatness of your personality. At this moment I pray to the Supreme Lord Kṛṣṇa to enlighten my intelligence and vivify my heart so that I can translate into suitable words the pure sentiments that a sincere and faithful disciple must cultivate toward his eternal master. I know, nevertheless, that I am still far from the perfection of being able to make you feel at least a simple glimpse of transcendental pride at this insignificant disciple of yours, even though I try to glorify you. I promise, however, to do my best to satisfy you more.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is impossible to deceive you. Selected and well-elaborated words will not impress and satisfy you. Formalities, regardless how pompous they be, will not touch you either. You have already transcended the plane of illusion and hypocrisy. How could we cheat and flatter divine beings situated on the plane of omniscience and perfection? Your present reality is the plane of eternal consciousness and blissfulness. You are with Kṛṣṇa, while we continue being lashed by Māyā.

During your stay in this world, the supreme transcendental reality, embodied in your personality, was manifested among us ordinary humans. Your presence in this world spread the rays of pure devotion in all directions. Your stay on this planet left a deep impression of *bhakti,* which now constitutes your legacy for us. Those elements of *bhakti* are the most precious thing in this dirty world. They are valuable jewels amid heaps of garbage and excrement. They are like crystal-clear and fresh springs amid a scalding desert. They are like a savior light-house amid a thunderstorm with threatening waves, terrifying darkness, sinister cliffs, and killer sharks. Through your divine grace we are able to traverse this gigantic ocean of material existence, which is impossible to cross by ourselves. The Deities, your *mūrti* on the *vyāsāsana* of the temples, your Bhaktivedanta purports, the *mahā-mantra,* the association with your faithful disciples and followers—in short, everything we can consider “pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness” is your legacy of *bhakti* to those who love you.

O Prabhupāda, my dear master, you are the incarnation of Lord Caitanya’s mercy. The Vaiṣṇava scene spread immensely in this planet due to your sacrifice. You gave yourself utterly—thoughts, words, and acts—to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s *saṅkīrtana* movement. Your performance in this chapter of Gaura-*līlā* was perfect and superb. Your surrender and service to the lotus feet of Kṛṣṇa equal those of the most exalted and pure devotees, such as Ambarīṣa Mahārāja.

O Prabhupāda, as a consequence of such sacrifice, your departure from this world was undoubtedly advanced. This was a heavy blow to us. We lost you prematurely, but, truly, your departure was worthy culmination of your life of sacrifice. Through this sacrifice, millions and millions of deluded souls, myself included, could and will be able to attain the most valuable good of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

O Prabhupāda, your faith in the potency of the holy names can only be equated with the faith that the *ācārya* Haridāsa Ṭhākura embodied. It is a faith that “moves mountains,” according to the Bible. Through your faith in the *mahā-mantra,* and through your eagerness to spread it, the Hare Krsna mantra has reached the global media and changed hearts all around the world. Your mercy, like that of Vāsudeva Datta and many other pure devotees, has no limits. Your love for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa, is deep and sublime, like that of all devotees who have Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi settled in their hearts.

O Śrīla Prabhupāda, beloved master, on this one-hundred-eighth Vyāsa-pūjā, I pray for at least a single drop of your infinite devotion, surrender, faith, compassion, purity, renunciation, and detachment. I promise to dedicate my life to your mission. Please, always grant me the opportunity to serve you and always keep me under the shelter of your lotus feet. Let this be the core of my life. This is my only hope and everything I have.

Puruṣatraya Swami

## Rasānanda Swami

My dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to your divine appearance. On this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, I bow before you with great awe and reverence.

It is said that your *mūrti* is not different from yourself.  
I have heard that you are living through your teachings.  
Some people have a living, dreaming experience with you.

Why don’t you speak with me?  
Why don’t I feel your presence in my life?  
Why don’t I see you in my dreams?

I wish I could hear you speaking to me.  
I long for the day that I will feel your presence in my life.  
I want so much to see you in my dreams.

What is wrong with me? So, I cry in desperation.  
When will be that day that I will be able to be in your presence?  
Fortunate are the souls who experienced, touched, saw you. So, they should  
not take for granted this blessing, calling them to serve you with all their  
hearts. I am longing for the day that I will be with you.

*From your most fallen servant,*  
Rasānanda Swami

## Śacīnandana Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Today on your glorious appearance day, let me try to glorify you in my simple way:

You are simply wonderful.  
You teach by example—and you have awakened me, a sleeping soul, totally in the grips of Māyā.  
You are definitely the most effective distributor in the transcendental garden of love, owned by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu.  
You display the most exalted qualities of *mukunda-preṣṭha*—being very dear to Lord Mukunda for always putting His pleasure first.  
You maintain an amazing degree of enthusiasm, which comes from your deep relish of your relationship with your own spiritual master and the Divine Couple.  
You are the most expert presenter of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and show humanity a relevant, joyful, and honest path to Kṛṣṇa.  
You truly embody the quality of full surrender to your own spiritual master and in this way speak directly to my own uncommitted intelligence and heart.  
You have absolute faith in the Holy Name, and thereby have become chosen to be His most effective instrument.  
For me you are the greatest example of *lobha,* transcendental greediness to obtain the mercy of the Lord.  
Although the greatest renunciant, you have given birth to many worthy sons and daughters.  
You embody the deepest possible commitment—by tolerating a disciple like me, who must have involuntarily disappointed you many times.

May you be glorified on this most auspicious day by the great *ṛṣis,* by the powerful rulers of the universe, and by endless numbers of wonderful Vaiṣṇavas, whose presence is making this world fortunate.

Thank you . . .

—for having saved me and never given up on me despite my innumerable mistakes.  
—for sharing so generously your own deep realizations of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.  
—for pointing out all the confidential secrets of *bhakti*.  
—for being such an expert guide, who always know where I’m at and how to motivate me to go forward.  
—for your extreme tolerance and patience with such a slow disciple.  
—for extending your warm embrace time and time again.  
—for being such a stern but also kind father (I need both).  
—for giving us the highest nectar in all existence, the service of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa in Vṛndāvana.  
—and lastly, for revealing to us the means and the goal of Kṛṣṇa consciousness: the Holy Name.

**A Prayer**

I have been bound to you since I was very young—but like a lenient father you have often given me the “long rope.” Now my body is getting old—the strength of youth is leaving me. Please, now pull me closer to you—because only in close proximity to you will I get the strength I require for your service and the long road ahead of me.

Thank you very much.

*Your humble servant,*  
Śacīnandana Swami

## Śubhag Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at your lotus feet on this auspicious occasion of your appearance day.

A pure Vaiṣṇava is rarely seen. Kṛṣṇa says in the *Bhagavad-gītā,*

*mac-cittā mad-gata-prāṇā bodhayantaḥ parasparam*  
*kathayantaś ca māṁ nityaṁ tuṣyanti ca ramanti ca*

**“The thoughts of my pure devotees dwell in me, their lives are fully devoted to My service, and they derive great satisfaction and bliss from always enlightening one another and conversing about Me.”**

From your childhood you were raised in a Vaiṣṇava family and brought up in the Vaiṣṇava tradition. Then, by the arrangement of providence, you came in touch with your eternal spiritual master, His Divine Grace Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Aṣṭottaraśata Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja Prabhupāda. He was a perfect Vaiṣṇava and a perfect spiritual master. Whatever you learned from him you implemented in your life, showing the example of the perfect disciple of the perfect master.

Both of you came from the spiritual world with a genuine service attitude to guru and surrendered without any reservation. You taught us by example, giving us your entire life as a lesson. You are decorated with all the transcendental divine Vaiṣṇava qualities. That is why you sincerely and seriously took the instruction of your master to heart and implemented it in your life. You tried to fulfill the mission of your guru and fulfill the prophecy of Caitanya Mahāprabhu, that in every town and village throughout the world people would chant the holy name of the Lord. And you did it.

As you followed your guru’s path and instructions, your disciples are following yours, trying to fulfill your desire and the desire of the previous *ācāryas*. That is the key to success in spiritual life. *Tāṅdera caraṇa sevi bhakta-sane vāsa/ janame janame haya, ei abhilāṣa.* If our desire life after life is to fulfill the desires of the Vaiṣṇavas, then everything will be provided.

Very recently the Pañca-tattva Deities were installed in Māyāpur *dhāma*. This is a step toward fulfilling the desire of Lord Nityānanda, of establishing one magnificent temple where Lord Caitanya will be served eternally. So many devotees came from different parts of the world to see the arrival of the Deities. Everyone was jubilant with ecstasy. There is no doubt that the Lord has descended in the form of the Pañca-tattva to shower mercy upon all fallen souls of the Kali-yuga. You said the periodic flooding of Māyāpur indicates that in the future Māyāpur *dhāma* will flood the whole world with love of Godhead.

I am a very insignificant servant of you and the Vaiṣṇavas. I have no qualification save and except your causeless mercy, your teachings and instructions, and your personal guidance. Please inspire me and bless me so that I can do my very best to fulfill what you expect from me.

We are looking forward to seeing the moon of Māyāpur-candra rise and beam cooling rays upon the whole world. Thus the whole world will be chanting the nectarean names of the Lord, and people will remember you eternally, for you are the pioneer of this movement.

May we always remain your humble servant.

Your unworthy servant,  
Subhāg Swami

## Trivikrama Swami

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

You have given us the greatest challenge: how to lead our life in such a way that we will be always acting for the pleasure of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. On one hand it seems like an impossible task. How can we, who have been addicted to serving our false ego since time immemorial, ever hope to overcome our “natural” desire to put ourselves in the center? Habits die hard, and this one is very deeply rooted.

However, you have given us some very potent weapons to defeat this apparently insurmountable addiction. First and foremost, you have very kindly given us your most valuable association. Without that we would be hopeless. As you mention in this conversation with two of your disciples:

**Puṣṭa Kṛṣṇa:** I personally . . . I don’t think that even Americans or Westerners would have accepted your teachings, as clear as they are, without having your personal association and seeing your example. I think people would have thought that it’s totally impossible to do such a thing.

**Hari-śauri:**It would have remained theoretical. But because you came and showed practical example, then everything has become very easy.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** Yes. *Āpani ācārī prabhu jīvera śikṣāya*. That is the way of teaching. Caitanya Mahāprabhu used to do.

I have recently been reading the book *Memories: Anecdotes of a Modern-Day Saint*. There we can see how your association touched and transformed the lives and hearts of so many. What compassion you showed in your willingness to mix with such fallen souls as us! Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We can never repay you for that kindness.

A second *astra* you have given us to overcome our weaknesses of heart is your instructions. So many jewellike words of advice to protect us from the onslaught of Māyā and our material habits: “Do as I am doing.” . . . “Your love for me will be shown by how you cooperate among yourselves after I have gone.” . . . “Little thing . . . strictly follow.” . . . “Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and be happy.” . . . “You only have to give up the poison.” . . . “First of all deserve, then desire.” . . . “Don’t try to see God, but act in such a way that God will want to see you.”

The list goes on and on, each one full of wisdom and available through your books, lectures, letters, etc., to all your sincere followers for the next ten thousand years. How Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī must be proud of you!

Praying to be of some help to you in your mission,  
Your lowly servant,  
Trivikrama Swami

## Umapati Swami

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

It was 1968, and I was in Los Angeles sitting across from Śrīla Prabhupāda. While we were talking I decided to ask about a thought I had had. “Is the world a reflection of scripture?” I said.

“Why are you thinking like that?”Śrīla Prabhupāda asked.

“Is it wrong?” I asked.

“Why are you thinking like that?”Śrīla Prabhupāda asked again.

I was surprised. This was hardly the yes or no I had expected. “Is it wrong?” I asked again.

“It is not wrong,” said Śrīla Prabhupāda. “The world is a reflection of scripture, but scripture is not a reflection of the world. You should not think like that for sense gratification.”

“Oh, no,” I said. “When I see somebody doing something wrong, like eating meat, I think, ‘My spiritual master said this is bad.’”

“Ah!” said Śrīla Prabhupāda. “When you think like that, your life at once becomes sublime.”

That’s amazing, isn’t it? How easy it is to make our lives sublime! If Śrīla Prabhupāda says it’s bad, then it’s bad; if Śrīla Prabhupāda says it’s good, then it’s good. Just accept this principle and then study Śrīla Prabhupāda’s books to see what is good and what is bad. And your life at once becomes sublime. What’s more, everyone who has tried it says it works.

And even more amazing, if you think about it, is that someone as great as Śrīla Prabhupāda could come to this world just to make people’s lives sublime. He did not have to come to help his own life. It was already perfect. He came to help the fallen souls, and he spared no effort to give us all a life beyond anything we could imagine.

Recently, I was in Māyāpur, and some of us were telling about our experiences with Śrīla Prabhupāda—how he would make us laugh, make us tremble, make us see our own foolishness, make us run here and there to get something done. But Śrīla Prabhupāda always had the same goal: to make our lives sublime.

We often say that Śrīla Prabhupāda built a house the whole world can live in. But this does not mean that wherever you may be living in the world, you are living in Śrīla Prabhupāda’s house. More likely, you are living in Māyās prison.

How can we tell? After all, sometimes Śrīla Prabhupāda tells that something is bad but we think it is sublime. Maybe our favorite material enjoyment still seems sublime to us. And Śrīla Prabhupāda told us not to go outside of ISKCON, but maybe some smooth preacher in India makes us feel so good we think we have attained something spiritually sublime.

We have to be careful. Lord Kṛṣṇa warns us in the *Bhagavad-gītā* that what seems to be nectar may turn out to be poison. We have to let Śrīla Prabhupāda tell us what is truly sublime. The bricks of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s house are his teachings, and unless you are living within these teachings, you are not living in the house where life becomes sublime. If you think you know more than Śrīla Prabhupāda about social issues or even about the moon, then you are living in Māyās prison even though you may be feeling some pleasure.

But, you may object, it is impossible to get out of Māyās prison. There is a way, though, and Śrīla Prabhupāda has made it easy. Just remember this: If Śrīla Prabhupāda says it’s good, then it’s good; if Śrīla Prabhupāda says it’s bad, then it’s bad. What could be easier? And we have Śrīla Prabhupāda’s guarantee. I heard him say it.

“Oh, you’re always harping on this,” you tell me. “Every time you give class, it’s the same thing. ‘Listen to Prabhupāda. Listen to Prabhupāda.’ You’re just a fundamentalist.”

Well, I’m not sure what that big word means, but if it means someone who takes everything that Śrīla Prabhupāda says into his heart, then that’s what I am trying to become. What else does it mean to be a disciple?

And why not? I want my life to be sublime.

How about you?

Umāpati Swami

## Varṣāṇā Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances. All glories to you, who are even better than a desire tree, fulfilling ever deeper longings in our hearts by bringing to earth the priceless and eternal treasure house of Goloka and distributing its contents, the Holy Names of Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

Reflecting back upon those jubilant years when you physically walked and talked with us, my vision begins to adjust to the darkness that followed upon your return to Goloka.

With eyes adjusting to the darkness, and vision gradually returning guided by your words, illumined by the torchlight of knowledge, I see now that your *vapu,* precious as it was, is still not the last word in our relationship.

Indeed, in time, physical proximity may even become an obstacle to an ever-deepening eternal relationship because it is lacking the space needed for reflection.

From the external perspective, darkness and despair, disappointment and death, are the normal companions we would expect to encounter after the *līlā* of your manifest presence, which dispelled the darkness of Kali-yuga for awhile.

As the last chapter closed on your physical pastimes here on earth, it took some time to open the next chapter of our service to you, almost paradoxically entitled “The highest ecstasy of love-in-separation.”

Within the pages of that unfolding chapter, we catch a glimpse of a whole new ever-fresh realm of your pastimes, playing behind the scenes of your *līlā* as we previously understood and experienced them.

In the landscape of this unfolding dimension, a community of believers is growing strong in their faith by your grace. It is not so much a physical community, as we had previously envisioned from your instructions.

I refer here to more than any physical body of believers, but rather to a spiritual lineage, growing and blossoming from the seeds you planted in the dust of this earth.

With fresh new growth emerging from the ashes of bygone dreams, your mission continues to evolve in ways of its own, with a spirit which cannot be constrained, but remains ever free.

As our inner vision adjusts to the external darkness, we see more and more with eyes anointed with the salve of your love. And as you told us to see mainly with our ears, we listen more deeply now to your inspiring instructions as they continue to speak from your books and resound within our hearts.

As we listen more carefully to the inner voice, our hopes and dreams experience rebirth in more real and substantial ways than they ever knew before.

The guidance of the Supersoul is your continuing counsel, inspiring all true believers to rejoice in the truth so that even death is no obstruction to devotional relationships.

You continue to echo the words of the *paramparā,* carrying the message of Śrī Kṛṣṇa on down to the present day.

Inspired by your words that we “die to live,” we eagerly persevere in our service to you, entering more and more into uncharted realms where death does not have the last word.

Truly, Vaiṣṇavas “die to live” and live together forever and ever in the transcendental service of distributing the treasure of Goloka, the Holy Name, who fulfills all the deepest desires of everyone.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for engaging us in the most munificent welfare activities of your Golden Lord. All glories to Śrī Guru and Śrī Gaurāṅga!

Your servant in separation,  
Varṣāṇā Swami

## Vedavyāsapriya Swami

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
Please accept my humble obeisances.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories to the divine grace you have so kindly bestowed upon me, your unworthy disciple. On this auspicious day of your appearance, even though it is very difficult for me, with my poor fund of knowledge, to glorify you in choicest words, yet I am duty-bound to perform the task, and so I am making a humble attempt. I hope my prayers to you remain meaningful.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the more I try to serve you in your movement, the more I realize that it was indeed very, very difficult for you to go through so many more inconveniences and difficulties than what I have encountered so far. For example, what a struggle you had to undergo to establish ISKCON in New York! And then, after eleven years of your cent per cent sacrifice to give the whole world the sublime process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness in written, practical, verbal, and exemplary forms, you departed from this world completely undisturbed, in an exalted transcendental mood. In this way you showed us the perfect way to depart from your Society, your manifested perfections, without attachment! These I consider miracles Your Divine Grace performed in my time.

But poor me! I have been struggling for the last twenty-eight years doing various odd services without accomplishment. Having been tossed off by some difficult situations, I feel discouraged in my seniority and am not having a smooth ride. I feel like retiring untimely because I do not yet know a lot of the intricacies of the movement you left us. I was told once by a senior Godbrother, “Don’t be so naive, otherwise you will be sucked up.” Whenever I feel reverses, these words ring in my mind. Despite all these feelings, I try to overcome them by remembering you and your transcendental accomplishments, which are my only solace and encouragement to keep serving. I pray that it is enough to be your obedient disciple.

Your worthless servant,  
Vedavyāsapriya Swami

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homages from ISKCON Centers

## Abidjan

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Please accept our most humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Dear Prabhupāda, on this glorious day we pray with all humility to Your Divine Grace and your dearmost Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara to make your dearmost wish come true, that all the entities in Africa and beyond will explode overnight in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, in pure eternal love of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara.

No one within the three worlds is sufficiently powerful to deliver us from this land of great ignorance called Africa. It is your good self alone who are empowered to deliver the most fallen, O Patita-pāvana.

Some preach but cannot give a good example, while others give a good example but cannot preach. But you are simultaneously a good preacher and an *ācārya*. This is your special quality that makes your appearance most special and your activities most glorious. It is only a glorious being of your caliber who can save such fallen souls as ourselves.

Dear Prabhupāda, you dine with heads of state, preach to kings, and advise United Nations diplomats. Yet you chant and dance in the street. How humble, how compassionate, how merciful!

Beloved Prabhupāda, what should bind us to your lotus feet? It’s love. How should that love be displayed? Through service. And how can we constantly quantify and qualify the service? By the Holy Name.

So with all humility we pray for three droplets of your divine causeless mercy:

1\) Please bless our tongue to always chant the Holy Name—offenselessly and in pure love.

2\) Please nectarize our ears to always hear the Holy Names—offenselessly and in pure love.

3\) Please mercify our whole being to always spread the Holy Names—offenselessly and in pure love.

In this way kindly chain us to your lotus feet and drag us along wherever you go.

All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Your most unworthy grandchildren at the ISKCON temple in Abidjan, Ivory Coast, West Africa.  
(written by Kuśa dāsa)

## Adelaide

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

Also, Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this most auspicious occasion of your Vyāsa-pūjā, please allow us to appreciate the mercy you so painstakingly delivered to this world of fallen souls.

We exist only by that mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, which in your humility you say is not your mercy but the mercy of Lord Caitanya. But we, as your disciples, cannot see the difference. Just as the traveler in the desert without a guide to find water is as good as dead, we would be similarly lost and dying without your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda. For us lost souls, Śrīla Prabhupāda, your mercy is as good as the Lord’s.

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto ’pi*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are our *param guru,* our guide through this desert of material life. By your instructions and by your example you are present today to help us along this difficult path. This is your mercy, which we feel at every step. It is not by our own power that we can advance along the razor’s edge. The only reason we have made it this far is by your guidance. Left to ourselves we certainly wouldn’t be where we are today, having the opportunity to taste the bliss of devotional service. Rather, we would most definitely be suffering in hellish life like those all around us who have not accepted your mercy. It is your mercy alone, Śrīla Prabhupāda, which corrected the course of our misdirected lives. Of this we have no doubt.

But for those who do doubt the influence of your mercy, Lord Kṛṣṇa says “there is happiness neither in this world nor the next.” And as you have stated in your Bhaktivedanta purport to *Bhagavad-gītā* 4.40:

\[T\]hose who have no faith and are always doubtful make no progress at all. . . . One should therefore follow in the footsteps of great *ācāryas* who are in the disciplic succession and thereby attain success.

In the blindness of our neophyte condition, we can only stumble along at your lotus feet. Please forgive us for our deviations. Amidst the bewildering mirages of Māyās attractions, we are sincerely trying to stay on track.

May we never confuse our own mishaps for your negligence, thinking the fault is not ours but yours. If you were ever to neglect us, that would also be a facet of your mercy, designed to bring us back on track.

Conversely, allow us never to confuse any success as being a result of our own work. You are a perfect devotee, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and we are full of faults. If we accept your guidance faithfully, we will never be lost, and if we can somehow or other please you by our service, success will automatically come.

This is our success formula here in Adelaide, and although our progress has been tentative until now, we hope that by your guidance (and by our submissive following), we can continue to improve.

Please, therefore, allow us to remain fixed in devotional service. Our cherished desire is to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We wish that many more conditioned souls might experience the sweetness of your mercy in their lives.

All glories to Śrī Guru and Śrī Gaurāṅga!

Your servants in Adelaide, Australia.

## Ahmedabad

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept many thousands of millions of *daṇḍavat praṇāmas* at your feet, which are worshiped by great demigods and sages.

In *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* (5.19.21) we find this description of how the demigods pray:

*etad eva hi devā gāyanti—*  
*aho amīṣāṁ kim akāri śobhanaṁ*  
*prasanna eṣāṁ svid uta svayaṁ hariḥ*  
*yair janma labdhaṁ nṛṣu bhāratājire*  
*mukunda-sevaupayikaṁ spṛhā hi naḥ*

**“\[Since the human form of life is the sublime position for spiritual realization, all the demigods in heaven speak in this way:\] How wonderful it is for these human beings to have been born in the land of Bhārata-varṣa! They must have executed pious acts of austerity in the past, or the Supreme Personality of Godhead Himself must have been pleased with them. Otherwise, how could they engage in devotional service in so many ways? We demigods can only aspire to achieve human births in Bhārata-varṣa to execute devotional service, but these human beings are already engaged there.”**

The demigods certainly had persons like yourself in mind when they sang such glories of those born in the Bhārata-varṣa. You have made not only India but the whole earth glorious by your appearance. Without your appearance, the earth would have remained bereft of *bhakti*. You have demonstrated the power of *bhakti* and the power of scriptures and sages that glorify *bhakti*. You have given new life and meaning to the entire process of spiritual life.

The dust of your feet has inconceivable power to bestow pure devotion. In fact, that is the only thing in this material world that can bestow pure devotion on suffering materialistic human beings:

*naiṣāṁ matis tāvad urukramāṅghriṁ*  
*spṛśaty anarthāpagamo yad-arthaḥ*  
*mahīyasāṁ pāda-rajo-'bhiṣekaṁ*  
*niṣkiñcanānāṁ na vṛṇīta yāvat*

**“Unless they smear upon their bodies the dust of the lotus feet of a Vaiṣṇava completely freed from material contamination, persons very much inclined toward materialistic life cannot be attached to the lotus feet of the Lord, who is glorified for His uncommon activities. Only by becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious and taking shelter at the lotus feet of the Lord in this way can one be freed from material contamination.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **7.5.32)**

Unfortunately, because of overwhelming material desires, in spite of your intimate association and personal blessings, our hearts still remain inundated with *anarthas*. Otherwise, upon your disappearance how could we have remained alive after having relished your association and mercy? We would have simply died of separation.

*‘duḥkha-madhye kona duḥkha haya gurutara?’*  
*‘kṛṣṇa-bhakta-viraha vinā duḥkha nāhi dekhi para’*

**“\[Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu asked,\] ‘Of all kinds of distress, what is the most painful?’**  
**“\[Śrī Rāmānanda Rāya replied,\] ‘Apart from separation from the devotee of Kṛṣṇa, I know of no unbearable unhappiness.’” (*****Caitanya-caritāmṛta, Madhya*** **8.248)**

You made spiritual life so easy and accessible, we can see the Supreme Personality of Godhead like the back of our hand, though He is inaccessible even to the greatest demigods and sages (*deva-munīndra-guhyam*).

In our blinded condition we did not even have the idea of searching after the Absolute Truth or spiritual life. Then by your association you not only gave us the perfect knowledge of God, but you created the desire in us to know God.

In the old days, the kings gave in charity to the *brāhmaṇas* not only a cow but also a barn to house the cow and land to feed the cow. In the same way, you gave us the vision and then showed us the Supreme Lord. Once when we said, “Oh, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are so fortunate that we met you and learned about Kṛṣṇa,” you rightly corrected, “I created your good fortune.” Otherwise, we were dashing into the big dark hellish condition of sinful material life. Having been saved by your from such a condition, where is the justification for us to think of anything other than your service? Every moment of our life we have to remember this—that you and only you are the cause of our becoming aware of the goal of human life. We were just like animals, *dvipada-paśus*.

You made us human beings. Without your mercy we would have remained animals, unable even to think of the Absolute Truth or spiritual life. So as soon as we stop remembering you and recognizing your absolute mercy, we immediately become a mouse again (*punar mūṣika bhava*).

Therefore, even though the Supreme Lord Nṛsiṁhadeva was personally present in front of him, Prahlāda Mahārāja prayed for the service of Nārada Muni, his spiritual master:

*evaṁ janaṁ nipatitaṁ prabhavāhi-kūpe*  
*āmābhikāmam anu yaḥ prapatan prasaṅgāt*  
*kṛtvātmasāt surarṣiṇā bhagavan gṛhītaḥ*  
*so ’haṁ kathaṁ nu visṛje tava bhṛtya-sevām*

**“My dear Lord, O Supreme Personality of Godhead, because of my association with material desires, one after another, I was gradually falling into a blind well full of snakes, following the general populace. But Your servant Nārada Muni kindly accepted me as his disciple and instructed me how to achieve this transcendental position. Therefore, my first duty is to serve him. How could I leave his service?” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **7.9.28)**

The words in this verse are not only Prahlāda Mahārāja’s. These are the words of Lord Nṛsiṁhadeva Himself, because he empowered Prahlāda to speak. Thus these words are absolute truth.

For our life there is no good fortune or destination without your complete satisfaction:

*yasya prasādād bhagavat-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto 'pi*

**Once in the early days when you became sick and a devotee suggested that maybe one of your Godbrothers could replace you, you had tears in your eyes and said, in effect, “My Guru Mahārāja is very merciful. I would never think of ever replacing my Guru Mahārāja.”**

So it is clear that Kṛṣṇa consciousness in not available by scholarship, austerity, penance, great oratory skill, or any such seemingly spiritual but inherently material quality.

*nāyam ātmā pravacanena labhyo*  
*na medhayā na bahunāśrutena*  
*yam evaiṣa vṛṇute tena labhyas*  
*tasyaiṣa ātmā vivṛṇute tanūṁ svām*

**“The Supreme Lord is not obtained by means of expert explanations, vast intelligence, or even much hearing. He is obtained only by one whom He Himself chooses. To such a person, He manifests His own form.” (*****Kaṭha Upaniṣad*** **1.2.23)**

Only one who is chosen by Kṛṣṇa, blessed by Kṛṣṇa, can receive spiritual understanding, and such blessings come only through the medium of an absolutely transparent pure *niṣkiñcana* devotee such as yourself.

On this auspicious 108th appearance day of Your Divine Grace, may we always remember the pure devotional mood you demonstrated on board the *Jaladuta* while writing the poem *Mārkine Bhāgavata-dharma.*

The total surrender, the grave *saṅkalpa,* the firm determination, and utmost humility and helplessness you showed before setting foot on American soil is the ideal mood of the servant of the Lord. You prayed,

How will I make them understand this message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness? I am very unfortunate, unqualified, and the most fallen. Therefore I am seeking Your benediction so that I can convince them, for I am powerless to do so on my own.

Somehow or other, O Lord, You have brought me here to speak about You. Now, my Lord, it is up to You to make me a success or failure as You like. O spiritual master of all the worlds! I can simply repeat Your message, so if You like You can make my power of speaking suitable for their understanding.

Only by Your causeless mercy will my words become pure. I am sure that when this transcendental message penetrates their hearts they will certainly feel engladdened and thus become liberated from all unhappy conditions of life. O Lord, I am just like a puppet in Your hands. So if You have brought me here to dance, then make me dance, make me dance. O Lord, make me dance as You like.
