I have no devotion, nor do I have any knowledge, but I have strong faith in the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. I have been designated as Bhaktivedanta, and now, if You like, You can fulfill the real purport of Bhaktivedanta.

Signed—the most unfortunate, insignificant beggar  
A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami

Your mood was very similar to that of Prahlāda Mahārāja, who pacified extremely angry Lord Nṛsiṁhadeva with his humble words. The great demigods could not pacify the Lord; even the goddess of fortune could not. But the humble words of Prahlāda immediately pacified Him.

It is also remarkable that Prahlāda Mahārāja, because he was transparent and fully dependent on the Lord’s mercy, was not afraid of the Lord’s ferocious form. Prahlāda had no other support or shelter, such as the great austerity, scholarship, power, prestige, and status of the demigods. His sole shelter was Lord Nṛsiṁhadeva’s lotus feet. In the same way, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you had no false shelter or support of a wealthy institution or many followers or anything else. Like Prahlāda Mahārāja, you were all alone, ready to be a cent percent instrument in the hands of Kṛṣṇa.

If some foolish person compares Your Divine Grace with any other person who dresses like you, speaks like you, or appears like you, he is very unfortunate because it is not at all the external appearance that caused the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. It was your transcendental total surrender, your totally pure transparent surrender, that made you an instrument in the hands of Kṛṣṇa. Once we walked into a room and you were reading *Kṛṣṇa* book. We asked you, “Oh, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are reading your own book.” You simply smiled jubilantly and said, “I cannot imagine that I have written this book. I can never write like this. Kṛṣṇa must have written this.”

That, we think, is indeed the most crucial realization. Whatever you said or did was not done by you but by Kṛṣṇa. Both Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī and Vṛndāvana dāsa Ṭhākura said many times, in effect, “We are simply writers, but actually Caitanya Mahāprabhu Himself is speaking. We are simply taking the dictation.” How can a human being write such wonderful transcendental poetry as *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* or *Caitanya-bhāgavata*? It is so extremely sweet and nectarean and natural. The very composition of both these literatures proves that it can be written only by the Supreme Lord. There is no such literature among human beings or demigods. In the same way, there is no character or person or sage or saint or prophet in human history who has transmitted the entire spiritual life, *bhāgavata-dharma,* in such an absolutely pure and complete way as you have. That means it has been done by Lord Kṛṣṇa and Lord Caitanya, and you are Their instrument (*kṛṣṇa-śakti vinā nahe tāra pravartana*).

When Sanātana Gosvāmī was diseased and Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu would embrace him, Sanātana would feel very guilty that his diseased body, which was wet due to infected sores, was touched by the Lord. So he decided to end his life by throwing himself under lord Jagannātha’s cart during the Ratha-yātrā. Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, being the omniscient Lord, rebuked Sanātana, who said,

“I am lowborn. Indeed, I am the lowest. I am condemned, for I have all the characteristics of a sinful man. If You keep me alive, what will be the profit?”

Lord Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu said, “Your body is My property. You have already surrendered unto Me. Therefore you no longer have any claim to your body. Why should you want to destroy another’s property? Can’t you consider what is right and wrong? Your body is My principal instrument for executing many necessary functions. By your body I shall carry out many tasks. You shall have to ascertain the basic principles of a devotee, devotional service, love of Godhead, Vaiṣṇava duties, and Vaiṣṇava characteristics. You will also have to explain Kṛṣṇa’s devotional service, establish centers for cultivation of love of Kṛṣṇa, excavate lost places of pilgrimage, and teach people how to adopt the renounced order.

“Mathurā-Vṛndāvana is My own very dear abode. I want to do many things there to preach Kṛṣṇa consciousness. By the order of My mother I am sitting here in Jagannātha Purī; therefore, I cannot go to Mathurā-Vṛndāvana to teach people how to live there according to religious principles. I have to do all this work through your body, but you want to give it up. How can I tolerate this?”

At that time Sanātana Gosvāmī said to Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, “I offer my respectful obeisances unto You. No one can understand the deep ideas You plan within Your heart. A wooden doll chants and dances according to the direction of a magician but does not know how he is dancing and singing. My dear Lord, as You cause one to dance, he dances accordingly, but how he dances and who is causing him to dance he does not know.”

Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu then said to Haridāsa Ṭhākura, “My dear Haridāsa, please hear Me. This gentleman wants to destroy another’s property. One who is entrusted with another’s property does not distribute it or use it for his own purposes. Therefore, tell him not to do such an unlawful thing.”

Haridāsa Ṭhākura replied, “We are falsely proud of our capabilities. Actually we cannot understand Your deep intentions. Unless You inform us, we cannot understand what Your purpose is or what You want to do through whom. My dear Sir, since You, a great personality, have accepted Sanātana Gosvāmī, he is greatly fortunate; no one can be as fortunate as he.”

Thus Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu embraced both Haridāsa Ṭhākura and Sanātana Gosvāmī and then got up and left to perform His noon duties.

In the same way, dear Śrīla prabhupāda, by dint of your total surrender, your body was also the property of Kṛṣṇa, and he engaged it in the welfare of the whole human society.

We pray on this auspicious day that we may always remember this fact: that you were not one of the many pure devotees or great saints or sages. You are a unique and very special person with special ability to cure the most wretched, the most fallen, sinful persons like ourselves. Therefore life after life we beg to remain specially connected to you and, through you, to the whole disciplic succession and Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Begging to always remain your humble servants,  
The ISKCON devotees in Ahmedabad, Gujarat, India.  
(written by Yaśomatīnandana dāsa)

## Aravade

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to your fearless lotus feet!

Actually, we are supposed to glorify you every day and every moment, but due to material bondage we are unable to do so. Due to your kind mercy, this attempt is made possible now.

This year is your very auspicious 108th birth anniversary. Everyone is trying to become “Prabhupāda conscious” by offering you many things related to Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

In this small center, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Gopāla Deities are worshiped. From this year onward we are going to improve the Deity worship standards by your mercy.

As Lord Caitanya predicted, the holy name is being preached all over the world, in every town and village. You preached all over the world. We, as your instruments, are trying to follow in your footsteps by increasing *harināma, prasādam* distribution, and book distribution in the villages around our center during this yearlong celebration of your 108th birth anniversary. We will also try to complete “Krishna-tattva Darshan,” a diorama exhibit.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are trying to improve our *sādhana* standards. Please empower us to fulfill your expectations so that we can please you forever.

Again, we are hoping for special mercy from you.

The servants of your servants, the devotees at ISKCON Aravade, India.

## Arizona

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

When death approaches  
Am I going to be ready,  
Will your potent instructions  
Make my mind steady?  
Or will I give in to  
Distractions that haunt,  
Be filled with desires  
That leave me to want?  
You taught us how  
To bridle the mind,  
Perform *japa* and *kīrtan*  
And with Kṛṣṇa be aligned,  
Associate with devotees,  
Spread the holy name,  
Serve your holy mission,  
Shy away from false fame.  
You’ve given us the means  
To pass the greatest test,  
To go home to Kṛṣṇa  
And not settle for less.  
Other kinds of liberation  
May attract just a few,  
But I only want to go  
Where I can be with you.  
Many disciples have left us;  
The list is getting longer.  
Half a century has passed;  
I’m not getting any younger.  
Leave frivolity behind.  
Let’s step up to the plate  
And get free from Māyā  
Before it’s too late.  
How fortunate are those  
Who have gone before,  
To enter your *līlā*  
And be with you once more.  
I dare not expect  
To be given that treasure;  
Their devotion to you  
I can’t even measure.  
But try I must,  
Despite all apparent odds.  
I hope my attempt  
Will not be seen as a fraud.  
And when it is time  
For what we refer to as death,  
I’ll be chanting Kṛṣṇa’s glories  
With my final breath.  
We’ll see you, our master,  
Again at the helm,  
In your eternal form  
In the spiritual realm.  
What can’t we sacrifice  
To have that divine vision?  
Let’s give our heart and soul  
To your glorious mission.

*Your servants in Arizona, USA.*  
(written by Sandāminī-devī dāsī)

## Auckland (New Varsana)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances on this most blessed anniversary of your transcendental appearance.

Just a month before your departure from this mortal world, Śrīla Prabhupāda, in your beloved Śrī Vṛndāvana-dhāma, you bestowed your causeless mercy upon this fallen soul. Remembering that day when you spontaneously demonstrated all the saintly qualities of a *paramahaṁsa sādhu* strengthens me and deepens my appreciation and love for you.

With your kind permission, Śrīla Prabhupāda, I would like to share those special moments with the assembled devotees, hoping that they too will enjoy transcendental pleasure from hearing of your unlimited compassion.

Near the beginning of His teachings to His mother, Devahūti, Lord Kapila describes the qualities of a *sādhu* as follows:

*titikṣavaḥ kāruṇikāḥ suhṛdaḥ sarva-dehinām*  
*ajāta-śatravaḥśāntāḥ sādhavaḥ sādhu-bhūṣaṇāḥ*

**“The \[seven\] symptoms of a** ***sādhu*** **are that he is tolerant, merciful, and friendly to all living entities, he has no enemies, he is peaceful, he abides by the scriptures, and all his characteristics are sublime.”**

**Tolerant**

With only a month or so to go before your final departure, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the *kavirāja* explained that you must have been experiencing excruciating pain. There was no flesh left on your body to cushion the nerves from pressing directly onto the bones. An unbearable condition. Personally, when we are even a little sick our tendency is to avoid anything that may cause inconvenience or difficulty. But when a young disciple came to see you in your final days, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you didn’t consider your own discomfort: you tolerated all bodily pains just to fan this disciple’s tiny spark of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

**Merciful**

You instructed your servant to “bring this young boy to my room.” Why? What could you possibly have to gain from speaking to a young neophyte disciple? You already knew that you would be leaving us very soon, so you weren’t interested in utilizing the few dollars of *guru-dakṣiṇā* that had been offered, or in the possibility of receiving some bodily service: you already had so many personal servants. What was your motivation for calling me? The obvious answer is “your causeless mercy.”

You cared so much for your disciples (and all conditioned souls) that your very life-breath was dedicated to creating our good fortune. You asked me what service I was doing and seemed pleased that I was enthusiastic to bring ghee to India. It was obvious that you were physically exhausted, but you sat and listened and then instructed me to continue making money so that it could be used for book distribution. Your final act of mercy before lying down on your bed was to offer me the huge *tulasī*garland you were wearing (a leaf of which I still keep in my “Prabhupāda locket’). You had absolutely no obligation to communicate with me at all. You weren’t seeing anyone. But you couldn’t help yourself; you called me to your lotus feet and bestowed your causeless mercy upon me. I can never repay you.

**Friendly to all living entities**

Kṛṣṇa is known as *suhṛdaṁ sarva-bhūtānāṁ,* the friend of all living entities. However, in your *Bhagavad-gītā* purports you write that the Lord’s pure devotees pursue His purpose of delivering the conditioned souls even more eagerly than the Lord Himself.

Your actions clearly demonstrate that you are the real friend who knows our real self-interest. During your last days with us, when most *sādhus* are absorbed in thoughts of their own personal (or impersonal) salvation, your continued meditation was on how to extend yourself, to be “a friend of all living entities.” Thank you a million times, Śrīla Prabhupāda, my dearmost friend and eternal spiritual master.

**He has no enemies**

You never considered yourself anyone’s enemy. You tirelessly worked for the spiritual benefit of all others. And even when there was a need to chastise, your words were aimed at raising the recipient to the spiritual platform. O faultless father, although I was a bumbling young fool, you didn’t see me as an “enemy” who had come to disturb you. Rather, you saw me as your spiritual infant and took the time to encourage me.

**He is peaceful**

At the morning Deity *darśana*, when I daringly stood between you and Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma to take a Polaroid snapshot of you, you sat peacefully on your palanquin, waiting for the picture to develop. Then, completely at ease with your surroundings, you smiled beautifully as you watched it gradually manifest.

The final test was at hand: your body was diminishing rapidly, there was no turning back. But you weren’t disturbed. “Stop all the medicine,” you said, “just perform *kīrtana;* that is real medicine.” You were completely satisfied with the simple chanting of your disciples.

Whatever the circumstances, Śrīla Prabhupāda, you always remained dignified and beautiful—transcendentally peaceful.

**He abides by all the śāstras**

Up to your last breath you translated and gave us your *Bhāgavatam* purports. In your final days you absorbed yourself in hearing *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and followed its final instruction to always be absorbed in *hari-kīrtana*. The basis of your life was *Bhagavad-Gita,* and your only solace in times of difficulty was *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta*. You often explained that only by seeing through the eyes of *śāstra* (*śāstra-cakṣus*), can one return to the lotus feet of the Lord. As the great *ācārya* for the modern age, you taught us by your example of always living according to the *śāstras*. You are the person *Bhāgavatam.*

**All his characteristics are sublime**

Your thinking, feeling, and willing, your spiritual intelligence, your aristocratic demeanor, your compassionate nature, your beautiful smile—in fact, your every gesture, Śrīla Prabhupāda, was always sublime. When you called me to your side in Śrī Vṛndāvana-dhāma back in October 1977, I had the direct experience of being smiled upon by someone whose characteristics are always sublime.

Your mercy is all that I am made of, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Although I’m not doing much to spread your mission, I humbly beg you not to give up on me. There may still be a chance that I can do something. Please don’t kick me away.

Your devotees at the New Varṣāṇā rural community in Auckland, New Zealand.  
(written by Jayaśīla dāsa)

## Austin

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

I am begging your mercy that your wretched, insignificant, so-called disciple may offer you his obeisances in the dust of your feet.

In spite of my hopeless contamination, you have so kindly given me shelter from the dark, cold, miserable Age of Kali in which I was so painfully suffering. What unparalleled love you manifested, kindly capturing and shackling me in the network of your boundless compassion, keeping me safely protected within your International Society for Krishna Consciousness, the incarnation of Kṛṣṇa for the present age.

I never dreamed that I would or could become a Hare Kṛṣṇa devotee, but one of your sweetest, kindest servants, His Holiness Viṣṇujana Swami, came to Austin in 1971 and introduced me to the sublime nectar of lovingly serving your lotus feet. The words emanating from your lotus mouth have for the last thirty-three years been frying the sinful desire seeds in my heart. Consequently, in spite of my stubborn weedlike monster *anarthas,* I am finally starting to get a little taste and appreciation for just what it is that you are giving me.

You are the true savior of the world. By your divine potency all living entities throughout the universe are becoming delivered from their hellish, nightmarish entanglement in the cycle of birth and death. You have ordered me to assist you in your divine mission by becoming an *ācārya* for delivering the fallen souls back to Godhead. This is a service which I am hopelessly unqualified to execute, but execute it I must on your order, completely dependent on your mercy.

I can only be successful by purely connecting my students with you. If there is any tinge of an enjoying spirit in my heart, I will ruin my own spiritual life and that of my disciples. Therefore I am begging you that I may be always situated 100% in the mood of pure devotional service to you, the most glorious of all gurus in the history of the universe. I request the boon that you kindly give my disciples your special blessings and protection as they struggle with their senses in their sincere endeavor to become your pure followers. You said that the grandfather is more merciful than the father. So please be especially kind upon these tender souls, your grandchildren, that Kṛṣṇa has sent me to engage in your service.

For spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness I have gotten the inspiration to start an E-course for introducing the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness on the Internet. By your inconceivable mercy I have gotten results far, far beyond anything I could have ever imagined. So far over 10,000 spiritual seekers from all over the world have joined the course. We have named our sign-up web page www.backtohome.com, after your famous slogan. Please bless me, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that this humble attempt to purely pass on your teachings may become unlimitedly successful for pushing forward Kṛṣṇa consciousness and sending the personality of Kali into an early retirement.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, when all is said and done all I can do is simply fall down at your lotus feet and take the sacred dust of those feet on my head as absolutely my only shelter.

I beg that you never kick me away.  
*Your insignificant servants in Austin, Texas, USA.*  
(written by Saṅkarṣaṇa dāsa)

## Baltimore

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble prostrated obeisances in the cooling dust of your lotus feet.

This year marks the most auspicious 108thanniversary of your divine appearance in this world. We are eternally grateful that you have come here to save us from this rotten material existence. While your glories are unlimited, let us reflect on just 108 of them during your Appearance Day celebration.

*You are:*

1\. Compassionate to the fallen souls

2\. Surrendered to the order of your spiritual master

3\. Fixed in devotion to Kṛṣṇa

4\. The most dear servant of the Lord

5\. Conqueror of the impersonalists and voidists

6\. Deliverer of the pure message of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu

7\. A pure, unalloyed devotee of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa

8\. Always distributing Kṛṣṇa’s mercy in the form of *prasādam*  
(you once called Kṛṣṇa consciousness “the kitchen religion”)

9\. The greatest scholar of Vaiṣṇava *siddhānta*

10\. The representative of Śrīla Vyāsadeva

11\. The distributor of the holy names of the Lord

12\. An eternal resident of Kṛṣṇa-loka

13\. Immersed in love of God

14\. Transcendental to the material modes of nature

15\. Equal in both distress and happiness

16\. A perfect gentleman

17\. Mad after Kṛṣṇa (this you considered the most important quality of a guru)

18\. A selfless, unmotivated servant

19\. A jewel amongst Vaiṣṇavas

20\. The most wonderful singer of *bhajanas*

21\. The best *mṛdaṅga* player

22\. More tolerant than a tree

23\. Equally disposed to every living entity

24\. The benefactor of the entire world

25\. *Bhakti* personified

26\. Bestower of *kṛṣṇa-prema*

27\. Overflowing with the twenty-six qualities of a devotee

28\. Exhibiting all the godly qualities of the demigods

29\. Endowed with divine nature

30\. The perfect seer of the Absolute Truth

31\. Worthy of everyone’s surrender

32\. The master at whose feet all other masters sit

33\. Enthusiastic and resolute in your mission

34\. Always chanting the holy names and . . .

35\. Always dancing in ecstasy

36\. Always engladdened by the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Lord Caitanya

37\. Always relishing the mellows of pure devotion

38\. The establisher of the *yuga-dharma* all over the world

39\. Empowered to spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness

40\. Always worshiping the Lord’s *arcā-vigraha*

41\. Expert in assisting the *gopīs* in their service to Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa

42\. The most confidential servitor of the Lord

43\. Purifying the holy places of pilgrimage

44\. Always attentive to the details of devotional service

45\. Uncompromising in presenting Kṛṣṇa consciousness

46\. Intolerant of those who present untruth in the name of truth

47\. The perfect guide for all humanity

48\. The defeater of tyranny in the realm of thought

49\. Taking us all back to home, back to Godhead

50\. The source of transcendental wisdom, *divya-jñāna*

51\. The author of a vast number of transcendental books filled with your Bhaktivedanta purports

52\. *Jagad-guru,* spiritual master of the universe

53\. The greatest distributor of Vedic knowledge in history

54\. The only way by which we can attain to pure devotional service

55\. The one who opens our darkened eyes

56\. Our lord, birth after birth

57\. An ocean of mercy

58\. The Lord and master of the devotees

59\. The most daring and bold preacher

60\. The great devotee whose appearance was foretold by Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura

61\. The establisher of temples in every part of the world

62\. Most tolerant and forgiving of our mistakes and offenses

63\. A kind friend to all living entities; you have no enemies

64\. The emblem of simple living and high thinking

65\. The proponent of real religion, *sanātana-dharma*

66\. Our savior from the miseries of birth, death, old age, and disease

67\. Extinguishing the blazing fire of material existence

68\. Living forever in your books

69\. Living still in sound, through your divine instructions (*vāṇī*)

70\. Always satisfied and engaged in devotional service

71\. The topmost, swanlike devotee (*paramahaṁsa*)

72\. The one who exposed the fallacies of modern science

73\. Our source of strength and encouragement

74\. The representative of Śrī Nityānanda Rāma, the original spiritual master

75\. Fearless and brave in all circumstances

76\. The uncompromising teacher of *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*

77\. Always joyful

78\. Gentle and magnanimous

79\. Humbler than a blade of grass

80\. Famous throughout the three worlds

81\. Always offering four kinds of palatable foodstuffs to the Lord

82\. Always satisfied to see your disciples take *bhāgavata prasāda*

83\. Unhappy to see the suffering of others

84\. One without a second

85\. The spiritual physician for the ailing souls of this age

86\. The guardian of the truths of the *Vedas*

87\. The most expert preacher

88\. Peaceful and always surrendered to Kṛṣṇa

89\. Victorious over the six material qualities

90\. An *uttama-adhikārī* devotee

91\. Enjoying transcendental bliss

92\. Showing us the path to attain immortality

93\. Showing us how to live happily in this world and the next

94\. Inspiring us by your example of simple living and high thinking

95\. The annihilator of the effects of Kali

96\. Teaching us how to care for cows, women, children, and others needing protection

97\. The best of the *brāhmaṇas*

98\. Always remembering the activities of the Lord

99\. Attached to describing the glories of the Lord

100\. Selfless and able to tolerate inconvenience for the sake of others

101\. Following in the footsteps of the previous *ācāryas*

102\. Giving us the privilege of devotional service

103\. Always accepting our service

104\. Creating our good fortune

105\. Our eternal master

106\. As hard as a thunderbolt

107\. As soft as a rose

108\. Our dearmost friend and ever well-wisher

*Your servants at the ISKCON temple in Baltimore, Maryland, USA.*  
(written by Sunanda das)

## Barcelona

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

We would like to thank you very much for all your mercy. Although we are not worthy at all, by your divine grace we have been engaged in devotional service day by day. Although we are limited, you have encouraged every one of us every day with your inspiring Bhaktivedanta purports, with the reading of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra,* along with the Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi worship. In this way, you have been taking care of us every day. Now we can only cry, being completely conscious that we will never be able to repay you for even a little spark of the causeless love you have shown us. No matter what we are able to do to serve you and Kṛṣṇa, the merit will always be yours, because every bit of knowledge, inspiration, skill, intelligence, and any other good quality we have has become manifest due to your planting the seed of devotion in our hearts and watering it every day. So please, let us keep trying to do more and more service eternally for your transcendental pleasure; please give us your blessings to continue our humble service through the service of your beloved disciples. This year we would like to offer you all the *prasādam* distribution we have done, along with the results of our interfaith preaching, university preaching, and festivals preaching.

Thank you very much, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*śrī-guru karuṇā-sindhu, adhama janāra bandhu,*  
*Prabhupāda lokera jīvana*

Your humble servants at your ISKCON temple in Barcelona, Spain.

## Baroda

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*anarthopaśamaṁ sākṣād bhakti-yogam adhokṣaje*  
*lokasyājānato vidvāṁś cakre sātvata-saṁhitām*

**“The material miseries of the living entity, which are superfluous to him, can be directly mitigated by the linking process of devotional service. But the mass of people do not know this, and therefore the learned Vyāsadeva compiled this Vedic literature, which is in relation to the Supreme Truth.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.7.6)**

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this, your 108th appearance day, we bow to you, who have so kindly blessed the entire world with the teachings of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* so that the misdirected members of humanity can somehow obtain the opportunity to escape from the imprisonment in the material world and the miseries of repeated birth and death.

It is a thankless task—the propagation of the *Bhāgavatam*—and frequent and difficult to overcome are the obstacles on the path of fulfilling your desires, which you revealed in your Bhaktivedanta purports, lectures, correspondence, and conversations.

We pray to your lotus feet on this, the auspicious anniversary of your appearance, to kindly bless our sincere efforts to remain true to your teachings and to kindly protect us from the attractions of both material happiness and philosophical deviation. These pitfalls are great and, in fact, seemingly impossible to avoid falling into.

We can attain success in the endeavor to fulfill your aspirations only by strictly accepting your instructions without change, a task that is admittedly extremely hard to put into practice due to the strong influence of the Lord’s illusory energy in her many manifestations.

We pray that we will always remember your transcendental instructions and never forget them!

Yours servants at ISKCON Baroda, India.

## Belo Horizonte

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories to Your Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Śrīla Prabhupāda! You were determined to spread the *mahā-mantra* throughout the entire world, and you displayed extraordinary courage doing so. With your unflickering faith and unlimited strength you overcame innumerable difficulties and initial setbacks, and your life is a constant source of inspiration in our own struggles. With you as our spiritual foundation, we feel confident in facing all challenges. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are an ocean of mercy, and we always try to remember this.

Belo Horizonte is the capital city of the second most important state in Brazil, Minas Gerais. It is famous for its sierras, caves, mountains, and waterfalls. Also, Minas produces up to 70% of the nation’s milk and milk products. We are certain you would have enjoyed visiting this opulent land.

We consider that Brazil has been graced by Your Divine Grace even though your lotus feet never tread upon this land. You always inspired your devotees to show deep concern for ISKCON’s development here.

The fertile lands of Minas Gerais are favorable for fulfilling Lord Caitanya’s prophecy that His Holy Names would be chanted in every town and village. Therefore, with your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we devotees now have to roll up our sleeves and humbly try to fortify Kṛṣṇa consciousness here.

This year the Belo Horizonte *mandir* celebrated the twentieth anniversary of the installation of the deities of Śrī Jagannātha, Śrī Baladeva, and Śrīmatī Subhadrā-devī. Your Divine Grace has been the biggest source of inspiration to all of us during our difficult moments; therefore, we servants of yours in Belo Horizonte bow down countless times to your lotus feet. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your servants in Belo Horizonte, Brazil.  
(written by Śrī Kṛṣṇa Mūrti dāsa)

## Bhakti-latā Purī

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances to your lotus feet. All glories unto you!

You remain the crown jewel of ISKCON. You are always present, reaching out to beginners in Kṛṣṇa consciousness by humbly accepting their offerings and worship.

Your talent in the creation of the structure and management of ISKCON was unparalleled, but we feel woefully unfit to take over the difficult task of maintaining and expanding this institution.

All of us are willing—we really want to help. Please bless us with whatever potency is needed to keep ISKCON going as you want it to, especially in the matter of taking very nice care of the devotees working in your army. We are sure that just a drop from the ocean of your mercy will empower us to serve you properly.

*Your aspiring servants in Bhakti-latā Purī, ISKCON’s rural community in Argentina.*

## Bhaktivedanta Manor

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Prabhupāda, each year we come  
to hold a candle to your sun,  
with paltry praise that tries to reach  
glories that exceed all speech.

And yet by your unbounded grace  
we find a love so long misplaced—  
fledgling feelings which now, we pray,  
show gratitude on this great day.

Lost in realms of deepest darkness,  
adrift on oceans of distress,  
blind even to our hapless plight,  
our course was set for Death’s black night.

Led by a vain and vacant heart,  
from love and truth we did depart.  
Bound for nothing more than pain,  
we struck out on the mighty main.

Towering waves of misery  
beset our boat incessantly.  
Clouds of grief on storm-force gales  
soon tore apart our flimsy sails.

And so in helpless agony,  
all hopes of great discovery  
were smashed to pieces one by one,  
our dreams of conquest all undone.

With nothing left but shattered pride,  
we cast our eyes from side to side  
in hope that we might somewhere see  
a shelter from that raging sea.

Then of a sudden did espy  
there in the distance clear blue sky,  
wherefrom a stately ship did sail,  
whose crew to us did loudly hail.

“Abandon now your battered boat,”  
they called, as we did barely float.  
And freely giving us a room,  
they sailed us swiftly from the gloom.

And the master of that vessel,  
which sails from heaven into hell,  
is you, dear Prabhupāda, our lord,  
whom we shall evermore applaud.

Commander of a mighty fleet  
that sweeps the seas of blind conceit,  
to search for souls whose search is done  
to be themselves the worshiped one.

With patience and compassion pure,  
you every misery endure,  
till all lost souls are brought aboard  
and carried back to your dear Lord.

And so we shall with thanks and praise,  
on this and all our lasting days,  
engage whatever strength we have  
to show the world your boundless love.

Thus prostrate at your feet we pray,  
please guide us on this sacred day,  
that we may never lose our place  
upon your ship of gentle grace.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to you!

Here at Bhaktivedanta Manor your mission continues to grow. More and more souls are discovering you and the messages you worked so hard to distribute. We owe everything to you. By following the simple process you gave us we have become peaceful and happy. Before that we were dejected, bedraggled fools fighting one another for a few scraps of miserable material enjoyment. By your potency and pure love we have completely turned our lives around. We have gone from despair to delight, from hippy to happy, from misery to majesty, and from loathing to love. Hell-bent on our own total destruction, we engaged in any kind of sinful act, utterly forgetful of our true nature. What unbelievable good fortune was ours that we should meet such a personality as you? How could that happen? It is surely a testimony to causeless mercy, which you perfectly personify. You have taken us by the hand and led us step by faltering step back toward the Lord. And still you lead. Your words remain our life and soul, and your glowing example is our endless inspiration.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, devotees like your good self are verily holy places personified. Because you carry the Personality of Godhead within your heart, you turn all places into places of pilgrimage. Anyone coming into contact with you for even a moment is supremely benefited, whether or not they understand. And you are still present in your books and the hearts of your sincere followers. At Bhaktivedanta Manor we are trying in some small way to spread your sublime teachings. However, we understand that we must first assimilate them ourselves. Only by becoming one with the order of the guru can one deliver it effectively to others. You wanted us to become *brāhmaṇas,* and you taught that the two main brahminical activities are *adhyayana* and *adhyāpana—*studying and teaching Vedic knowledge. Bhaktivedanta Manor is therefore endeavoring to establish a faculty of Vedic education on its premises. In 1974 you said, “Everywhere, wherever we have got our centers, a *varṇāśrama* college should be established to train all divisions.” We are therefore working toward that vision, and we pray for your grace that we might succeed.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we know that without your mercy nothing can be achieved. Our real challenge lies within, in fully purifying our hearts and minds so we can become proper recipients of your mercy. We want to become conduits for your grace, for we know that alone we have no power to do anything. *Śakti-buddhi-hīna, āmi ati dīna, koro’ more ātma-sātha.* Please help us find the humility to recognize our wretched condition and to therefore take more shelter of you. Keep us fixed in *nityaṁ bhāgavata sevayā.* In that way free us from the unwanted and painful impediments that we still hold in our hearts. Then perhaps we may begin to do a little service and actually assist you, which is surely our deepest desire and hope.

With thousands of obeisances at your lotus feet, we remain  
Your aspiring servants at Bhaktivedanta Manor, UK.  
(written by Krishna Dharma dāsa)

## Boise

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you on this most auspicious occasion of Vyāsa-pūjā!

This year marks the 108thanniversary of your appearance in this world. September 1, 1896—few people then realized the significance of the day. The child who took birth under a jackfruit tree in British Calcutta would soon bring about a “revolution in the impious lives of this world’s misdirected civilization.”

Usually the winds of time diminish the magnitude of a man’s accomplishments. Great persons in the past have left their mark on the world, changed the course of history, and departed the world with great flourish. Yet a few years later they become little more than names in history books. The honor and gratitude due them for their labors become relegated to the realm of ceremony, as the next generation tries its best to outshine the past.

But things are different in a community of Vaiṣṇavas. Each generation in the disciplic succession feels increasingly grateful to the preceding *ācāryas,* as the magnitude of their contribution becomes ever more apparent. The followers aspire to live up to the ideals of their spiritual master, as his instructions and example guide and vitalize their activities. Each generation of the *paramparā* is thus present in the succeeding one.

This is especially true in regard to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. As your disciples, grand-disciples, and now great-grand-disciples work to spread your mission further, with every passing year we appreciate more and more the greatness of your accomplishments and sacrifice. Three areas come to mind immediately:

On your disappearance day last year, Śrīla Śrīdhara Swami made special note of your sacrifices in terms of age and health. As many preachers in the movement pass middle age, he said, we are beginning to gain first-hand experience of what it is like to continuously travel, manage, preach, and write when the body is past its prime and encumbered by physical ailments. What austerity you were willing to endure to spread Lord Caitanya’s mission! We are only now beginning to realize.

As we study your books over and over, our appreciation increases for the breadth of your knowledge and the depth of your realization. You were devotee and scholar par-excellence. Among scholars of Sanskrit, it is commonly accepted that one’s Sanskrit ability is put to the test by *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. The *Bhāgavatam’s* language is poetically rich, philosophically deep, and linguistically subtle—all at the same time. That you were able to translate such a difficult text and explain it for the modern world is a tribute to your scholarship and accessibility. Your writings are not casual dictionary translations—they carry the force of the entire *paramparā* behind them. Like an oncoming train, they run over all misconceptions within the heart. Every fresh reading brings out new meaning and philosophical insight. “How did I miss this the first time?” Your books reveal themselves to us as we become ready to hear.

Your accomplishments are not just a memory of the past: they are vitally felt today, in the daily growth of your movement. This year we saw a historic event take place in Śrīdhām Māyāpur—a significant step in the fulfillment of your vision for Lord Caitanya’s birthplace. Ten thousand devotees chanted, danced, and welcomed Śrī Śrī Pañca-tattva. Each one left personally transformed. “I’ve never experienced anything like this in my life. It was beyond words.” The power of your desire and instructions are clearly visible.

So also in Boise we have felt your presence and guidance this last year. Four new initiates committed themselves to a life of service to you. Boise ranked second highest in the USA in the number of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* sets distributed. Devotees regularly distribute your books at the university, downtown, and door to door. New preaching programs have been started at prisons in the area. Lord Jagannātha again rode triumphantly through downtown Boise during the Fourth of July parade. And for the first time this year, every Sunday Feast at the temple was sponsored by members of the congregation. The Friday *Bhagavad-gītā* study group has grown to become like a second Sunday Feast.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, the more we appreciate the magnitude of your accomplishments and sacrifice, the more our endeavors seem small and inadequate. Although we cannot hope to repay our debt to you, please allow us to reciprocate in some way by engaging us in your service. Our lives are yours, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and you can use them as you see fit.

Your grandchildren in New Biharvan, Boise, Idaho, USA.  
(written by Rādhikā Ramaṇa dāsa)

## Boston

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Thirty-nine years ago you sailed forth deep into the heart of Western materialism. We were unfortunate not to greet you then. Today we welcome you at the Boston shore and offer our very selves, upon which you can place your lotus feet. Please bless us to remain before you always as your servants.

You have stated, “This whole world is in chaos,” yet we see it as orderly, and thus we must truly be blind. You are always thinking how to extract the entrapped souls from this scorching fire of material existence, yet we not only see it as normal, we are very eager to remain here for our happiness and satisfaction! Today we pray to Your Divine Grace to pry open our eyes with your mercy so that we may at last see the truth. Please forcibly pull us from this hellfire of material life.

The living entity’s conditioned consciousness is excited by contact with matter and is happy inducing others into mutual sense enjoyment. You, on the other hand, are in pure and excited Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and your happiness is in freeing others from enjoying matter and inducing them to serve the Supreme Lord. Thus you are always enthusiastic and eager to engage in the *saṅkīrtana* movement of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Sweetly and melodiously you sing from the heart the Holy Names of Śrī Kṛṣṇa to give transcendental pleasure to the Supreme Lord and the devotees. Today we pray for your divine mercy so that we may get that very same taste. Please bless us that we may always want to chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mantra* and dance and, like you, compassionately induce others to experience this great mercy. Please, we are your puppets, make us dance to your will!

Everyone here is deeply absorbed in personal and extended sense gratification. The irony of it is that the senses hardly find happiness and in the meantime are the source of all troubles. You have solved the problem and given us *bhāgavata prasādam*. Though we have been born as *mlecchas* and *yavanas,* without a touch of devotion to the Supreme Lord, you have petitioned Kṛṣṇa to accept our humble and insignificant offerings made on your behalf. Today we pray for your compassionate mercy that we desire only to prepare “sweet and delicious, juicy and succulent” foods for the satisfaction of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa. Please bless us that our offerings are made according to your instructions and that the Supreme Lord accepts them. Please bless us to develop the taste to take *prasādam* exclusively so that we have no more hankering for anything material.

We see so many purposes for this human life, and, at best, Kṛṣṇa consciousness is merely an important one. For example, we are very enthusiastic for economic development. You boldly declare, “This economic development is nonsense.” Rather, you see that there is only one purpose of life, one activity, that of serving Kṛṣṇa. Nothing else exists. Today let us pray for your mercy to get the vision of truth and become devoted servants of Your Divine Grace. Please bless us to know the Absolute Truth, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and thus have only one purpose, to engage ourselves and others in pure, unalloyed, unadulterated, unflinching devotional service.

We take education as a mechanism to earn an income, increase our standard of living, and “perfect” our material existence. The general result of education is to gain domination over others and increase one’s arrogance. You, on the contrary, inform us that real education is to know how to end our material existence, once and for all. “The real purpose of education is to learn to become submissive” and humbly to serve the representative of God. The material conception sees life as only a product of matter: there is no soul and, therefore, life is only a transitory state of enjoyment ending in death. Thus we are steeped in impersonalism and voidism, resulting in rampant materialism. In every way you are the supreme personalist, seeing only the soul and its transcendental relationship with Kṛṣṇa. Matter comes from life, and both life and matter come from God. Before meeting Your Divine Grace we were only the walking dead. Today let us pray to Your Divine Grace that we may understand real life and that we may realize that our real life comes from your benediction. Please bless us to understand our indebtedness to you, and thus desire to reciprocate by engaging exclusively in your service.

Conditioned by this material energy, we take this body as all in all. Eager for sense gratification morning, noon, and night, we simply practice decorating this “dead body.” In fact, the advancement of so-called civilization is measured by its preoccupation with the beautification of this body. Through your books, you have so kindly opened a window to the spiritual world and given us knowledge of the unsurpassed beauty of Kṛṣṇa. In the midst of the great impersonalism of the modern world, you informed us that God is the Supreme Person, the most attractive of all, Kṛṣṇa, the supremely attractive! Who is more disqualified than we? Today we pray to Your Divine Grace that we may understand your message and become attracted to the beauty of Kṛṣṇa. Please bless us to desire to engage as your assistants in making beautiful and attractive arrangements for the worship and decoration of Śrī Śrī Rādhā and Kṛṣṇa and become free from the phantasmagoria of the illusory attractiveness for these material bodies.

Everyone here is clamoring to become important, prominent, recognized, or, in other words, the Supreme. With great bravado you informed us, “God is infinitely great, we are infinitesimal. We are not God. We are His servants.” Nay, the aspiration is even greater: to be lesser, i.e., to be the servant of the servant of the servant of God. That is the supreme happiness of Your Divine Grace, that you are the servant of the servant of the servant of your spiritual master. Today we pray for your divine humble mercy that we may learn this infinite and great humility from you. Please bless us to purify our consciousness so we can relish being the servant of the servant of the servant of Your Divine Grace.

How exhilarating to be in your presence! You are indeed *sākṣād-hari.* You are just like Kṛṣṇa in that you are infinitely appreciative and grateful for the tiniest of efforts in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Even a small flower brought to you with love and devotion absorbs you in complete affection toward the devotee. Today we pray to Your Divine Grace that we may be able to begin to understand who you are. The rarest of souls, you are completely absorbed in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. By the force of your purity and devotion to your spiritual master, you are able to deliver the entire universe. Please let us understand who you are so that we may completely surrender to you as the representative of Kṛṣṇa.

A small child is thrilled for her entire life because you made her feel like the most important person in the world, completely accepted by you and wanted and fully loved. How is this? It is because you are completely pure and fully Kṛṣṇa conscious. You see the world as it is. To you, she is naturally very important and you treated her exactly as you felt. Our dear lord and spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda, today please allow us a glimpse of this spiritual vision you have. Please bless us to see only in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, and to see the real importance and great significance of every living being, and their eternal connection with Kṛṣṇa and ourselves.

Our dear spiritual master, you are always enthusiastic for inspiring others in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Not a moment lapses when you are not serving the interests of others in increasing and perfecting their dormant relationship with the Supreme Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa. Once you revealed the inner working of your heart and how you think while preaching. With great enthusiasm, glowing eyes, and a sweep of your hand mimicking the wave of a peacock fan, you said, “Preaching is to see the spiritual spark and fan it!” Today, Your Divine Grace, please bless us to understand this confidential mood in your heart. Please bless us, as your humble and aspiring servants, to think and act only to enthuse others in their Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Please enable us, as your servants, to be the useful servants of all others in their service to Kṛṣṇa.

Your advice that the *vāṇī* is more important than the *vapu* is, of course, so important. But we could say that to us, more important than these separately is the personality whose presence we cherish and whose absence we serve, His Divine Grace 108 Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya Śrīla Abhay Charan Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda. Our Prabhupāda! What a wonderful and amazing person! Intensely personal, by your personality you are personally destroying the nasty impersonalism and voidism of the modern world. You intensely love Kṛṣṇa, and by your love and personalism you are filling that void. Who are you? When will we ever begin to appreciate even one millionth of your qualities? Today we pray that you reveal yourself to us. We pray that we understand well enough who you are so that we may unconditionally surrender unto you.

We see so many valuable things in this world, but you brazenly declare that the value of this entire creation is zero. More materialism is more burden for the soul. Adding zeros to zeros has no net result. You have informed us that in the whole world there is one thing of supreme value, the Holy Name of the Lord. You have done mathematical wizardry by enlightening us that multiples of zeros can indeed be increasingly valuable by simply putting a one in front. Simply adding Kṛṣṇa makes everything material valuable and liberating. Today we pray that we begin to understand that “your mercy is all that we are made of.” Please bless us that we develop the taste for pleasing Kṛṣṇa and that we therefore will desire to use everything purely in His service.

Who could preach this way, like you? In each and every class, each and every *darśana,* you speak so expertly and personally, guiding each and every person to their next steps in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. At the same time, your presentations are perfect and complete. Even if a person met you only once, you gave him the entire message of how to go home, back to Godhead. Every time, you speak of this simple and sublime truth: that we are not our bodies. Basic, yes, sublime, yes, deeper than most of us can imagine. Deeper than first thought. At the same time, as one comes to understand it, you are leaving out nothing. If one could only hear you perfectly, even the first meeting could give someone complete and pure love of Godhead. Today we pray to Your Divine Grace that we realize in full that we are not our bodies. Please bless us to think always as your servants, and to be eager to engage actively in propagating the message of *Bhagavad-gītā.*

You are so kind and merciful that you decided to bless us and make things simple. You have made Kṛṣṇa consciousness “simple for the simple”. All we have to do is simply follow your simple instructions. Śrīla Vyāsadeva was disheartened by the complexity of his Vedic compilations. But following in Vyāsadeva’s footprints, you made it simple: please just chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. Lord Caitanya made it simple, Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī made it simpler, Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura and Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī made it even simpler, and you have transparently presented that and perfected it. Simple living and high thinking. Simply surrender unto Kṛṣṇa. Today we pray for your divine mercy so that we may become truly simple. Please bless us that we may savor the sublime happiness of chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, and that we may discard all unnecessary material activities that distract us from Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Because of your expertise, you made the difficult seem easy. We become busy with the tasks we are given, and often find we have not enough time for *prasādam,* temple programs, baths, walks, and reading the books you have given us. Sometimes we struggle to do just our minimum rounds of *japa.* Often we neglect our health because there is just not enough time. Many people want to meet us, but we do not have enough time to meet them all and to give them the time they need. You would translate an average of twenty-five pages a day, answer all the day’s mail, attend the programs, and chant your rounds, all without fail. Daily you took a leisurely breakfast, lunch, afternoon fruits, and evening snack, savoring and honoring *prasādam* without rush. You personally oversaw each aspect of this huge and quickly developing movement, including three difficult projects in India. Every day you would take a long and brisk walk, take an hour-long massage in the afternoon and a half-hour one at night. The list is long. But amazingly, transcendentally, you always had time to meet everyone and give them all the time they needed. Today we pray to Your Divine Grace that we understand your transcendental position. Please bless us to relish with bliss your transcendental association.

Your books are the saviors of the world, meant to bring about a transcendental revolution. When you prepared the first volumes of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, you said, “I have put in them everything one needs to know to become fully Kṛṣṇa conscious.” This is a fact. Though you made things simple, nothing is left out. Often you would read your own books with rapt attention. Enthused and amazed, you would comment, “I could not have written this book! Kṛṣṇa wrote it.” Today, Your Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray that we have an appreciation of the power of your words. Please bless us to realize that you have given us Kṛṣṇa in full, complete. Please bless us that we develop a desire to distribute your books and their content all over the world.

Though one should be careful about comparing one *ācārya* with another, it is safe to say that you accomplished the revolution that all our predecessor *ācāryas* had planned, i.e., to make the *mahā-mantra* a household word in every town and village of the world. But there is much work still to be done. Today, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray that we may become instruments in your hands. Please bless us to fulfill your plans. Let there be Kṛṣṇa consciousness in every aspect of life throughout the planet. Let tens of millions, hundreds of millions, nay, billions take up the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness, read your books, chant Hare Kṛṣṇa, and be happy. Today we pray to be your instruments. Please bless us to fulfill your desires.

Concerning your desires to please the Supreme Lord, you humbly state:

How will I make them understand this message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness? I am very unfortunate, unqualified, and the most fallen. Therefore I am seeking Your benediction so that I can convince them, for I am powerless to do so on my own. Somehow or other, O Lord, You have brought me here to speak about You. Now, my Lord, it is up to You to make me a success or failure as You like. O spiritual master of all the worlds! I can simply repeat Your message, so if You like You can make my power of speaking suitable for their understanding. Only by Your causeless mercy will my words become pure. I am sure that when this transcendental message penetrates their hearts they will certainly feel engladdened and thus become liberated from all unhappy conditions of life.

O Lord, I am just like a puppet in Your hands. So if You have brought me here to dance, then make me dance, make me dance. O Lord, make me dance as You like. I have no devotion, nor do I have any knowledge, but I have strong faith in the holy name of Kṛṣṇa. I have been designated as Bhaktivedanta, and now, if You like, You can fulfill the real purport of Bhaktivedanta.

These were the words you wrote at Boston’s Commonwealth Pier. Today we pray to Your Divine Grace to please let us understand this humble mood of yours, spoken from the realization of how Kṛṣṇa empowers the pure devotee in His service. Please bless us to become your instruments to further spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

By pleasing you we will become Kṛṣṇa conscious. When can we realize that? When can we understand that we are blind, lame, and dumb? Today we pray that by your divine mercy we may understand what a great gift you have given us. Please bless us to abandon the false ego, which tells us that we may achieve Kṛṣṇa consciousness without your mercy. Please bless us to be able to hear your words as they are, free of mental speculation and mundane interpretation. Please bless us to gain the intelligence to understand your instructions, to follow them, and to surrender ourselves completely.

Begging to become the servant of your servants,  
*The residents and congregation of Nava Guṇḍicā, ISKCON’s temple in Boston, Massachusetts, USA.*

## Brisbane

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**Day by Day**

Today, after chanting, we walked by the bay  
And watched flying fishes at break of day.  
We remembered your morning walk ever day,  
Though you’d flown round the world  
And were constantly moving.  
Whatever we try to do for improving  
Our quality of life and service,  
We find exemplified by you.  
“If someone comes to stay with you,”  
You said, “and after a week  
Wants to live like you,  
That is preaching.”  
Day by day, husband and wife,  
Or *sannyāsa/guru—* living life,  
Remembering your example,  
Trying to tread the path you showed,  
Stumbling, sometimes falling, calling  
For your mercy, struggling to remember,  
To keep the taste, increase the taste,  
The growing understanding as we age  
How much time we waste.  
And for every aspect of our lives  
We have the barometer of your activities,  
Your completely poised walk  
Through this material forest:  
How to be responsible,  
To keep accounts, be free from debt,  
How to support a family,  
And then to extend one’s love  
Beyond the clan and welcome all,  
To be courageous and discerning  
Yet flexible and compassionate,  
To be creative without deviating  
From the highest truth,  
To be a master and simultaneously  
The dearmost friend of all;  
How to eat, exercise, keep the body  
Clean and fit for serving Kṛṣṇa,  
Everything an instrument,  
We see how to use everything.  
Not one of us can say  
We have nothing to offer the Lord,  
For you have shown God-centeredness  
From every angle of vision,  
How to engage in your mission,  
Be we rich men, poor men, managers,  
Artists, speakers, friendly souls . . .  
However we are situated,  
We find encouragement in you.  
Though you teach the highest truth,  
Your vision is broad and encompasses  
The world of the neophyte.  
You taught us the simple steps,  
You performed miracles no other could  
In giving us service to the Divine Couple,  
Just cleaning the dirty stairs from the street  
To their shining abode  
In a first-storey temple room,  
Above busy, hellish Soho.  
We did not know anything but how to serve,  
How to cook, to clean, to invite others  
Into Their Divine Presence  
Within that exquisite inner sanctum  
Fashioned by one young man who loved you.  
How brilliant you were, Śrīla Prabhupāda,  
To engage the whole world  
In Deity worship.  
This was your clear and simple  
(For the simple)  
Yet **matchless** gift  
Of *kṛṣṇa-bhakti*.  
Love was not an esoteric concept  
But a lived reality, day by day.  
Somehow you taught the loftiest principles  
In a feet-on-the-ground way!  
So it is this “lived experience” that I value  
More than anything else in the world.  
Whether it is a health issue,  
A way to work with others,  
A way to converse with nondevotees,  
To see them as Kṛṣṇa’s separated servants,  
Whatever comes before us day by day,  
We have the example of how it can be done  
With Kṛṣṇa at the center.  
You told us “the most secret of all secrets,”  
You gave us “direct perception of the self  
By realization,” and we experienced  
The taste of true joyfulness.  
It’s a constant *sādhana* for re-awakening  
Lost taste for many of us—  
We are so forgetful, we must practice.  
Taking our material anxiety  
Out of the center to remember  
Our harmonious relationship  
With the Lord of the Heart.  
Just the sound of the opening bars  
Of the “Kṛṣṇa Meditations” album—  
*kṛṣṇotkīrtana-gāna-nartana-parau*—  
And the taste floods us again,  
To hear your voice, to see your face,  
To dip in the ocean of Your Divine Grace,  
And again we set the rudder on course.  
This is conditioned life—over and over  
We strive to remember and taste again.  
We were flooded with mercy when the dream came true  
In Māyāpur this year, with the descent of the Pañca-tattva.  
Stunned, we witnessed their beauteous forms  
As they were bathed before the assembled devotees.  
The Holy Name was worshiped by B. B Govinda Mahārāja  
And the floodgates opened—the tidal wave  
Smashing my rocklike heart to bits.  
We bathed Their Lordships with the Hare Kṛṣṇa mantra  
And we were all transformed in the moment,  
It seemed as if forever,  
No past or future—time disappeared,  
Simply we were present in the holy sound  
As if we had attained our eternal *svarūpa*  
And lived with the Lord.  
You have given us so many glimpses  
Of the spiritual realm  
Even from our neophyte world,  
Just as you installed Rādhā-Gokulānanda  
At the Manor and invited us all  
Into Their kingdom to dance around  
Their divine forms while chanting Their Names.  
How can we ever leave you?  
You are ever present every day,  
Though sometimes we are distracted  
And turn away, but you took our hearts,  
Injected the seed of *bhakti*—our true father—  
Broke those hearts when you left us,  
But stayed within our vision every way.  
You are the miracle worker of the *yuga*.  
We saw your son, Śrīdhara Mahārāja,  
On the brink of death,  
Looking like an innocent young boy,  
Hoping with all his heart to meet again  
With his beloved father.  
He opened his warm heart  
To all the assembled devotees.  
You gave us such brothers and sisters  
Whose association we could love and cherish.  
My prayer for ISKCON:  
That we can be each other’s  
Well-wishers and live our lives  
Day by day, in such a way  
That will make you glad  
And proud to say  
“These are my disciples.”

*Your servants in Brisbane, Australia.*  
(written by Sarvamaṅgalā-devī dāsī)

## Budapest

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my respectful obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Another year has passed, and those devotees to whom you mean a lot are taking pen in hand and trying to express your glories and the loving emotions they feel toward you. It is not an easy task, since there is no limit to your glories, but still I am trying.

A Vaiṣṇava is *para-duḥkha-duḥkhī,* that is, though he does not have personal problems, if he sees others in trouble he is very unhappy. This is the problem that a Vaiṣṇava has to face. A Vaiṣṇava finds full shelter at the lotus feet of the Supreme Personality of Godhead; therefore he himself has no problems. But since he feels sorrow for the fallen conditioned souls, he always puzzles over how to rescue them from hellish life, both in this and their next body. In the same way, you were living in Vṛndāvana, in the living place of the Supreme Lord, but instead of staying there and doing your own *bhajan* you took the sea of difficulties upon yourself that preaching involves. You traveled to America and took on the suffering voluntarily. *Tapyante loka-tāpena sādhavaḥ prāyaśo janāḥ:* “It is said that great personalities almost always accept voluntary suffering because of the suffering of people in general.”

The result of this selfless devotion is that now more and more people are starting to realize the singular opportunity offered by this wonderful Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, the movement that owes its existence to you. By founding ISKCON—that is, by translating and commenting on the Vedic scriptures, establishing temples and farms to make *sādhu-saṅga*, *hari-kathā,* and *saṅkīrtana* possible for the devotees, and doing so much more—you gave everyone the opportunity to rise to the spiritual platform. You also mercifully did everything you could to help any person who set out on the path back to Godhead never to turn off it. You said in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 6.36–37:

One who is interested in his own salvation is not as advanced in Kṛṣṇa consciousness as one who feels compassion for others and who therefore propagates the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement. Such an advanced devotee will never fall down, for Kṛṣṇa will give him special protection. That is the sum and substance of the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement.

You graciously afforded us the chance to take part in fulfilling Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s desire. If we simply follow your instructions, we are guaranteed to receive the greatest possible treasure that can be received: pure and unmotivated love for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa. Thank you for everything. All glories to you!

*Your servants at the ISKCON temple in Budapest, Hungary.*  
(written by Kaṁsāri dāsa)

## Canberra

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda

Please accept my most respectful obeisances All glories to you on this occasion of your most auspicious Vyāsa-pūjā!

I have always found that this day is the most spiritual day of all days of the year, since you are the most merciful to all your spiritual children. I know that you are most pleased to see your spiritual children advancing in devotional service to Lord Kṛṣṇa by strictly following the process of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

I know that you are always present in all your temples, guiding us along the path back home, back to Godhead. The very simple and sublime process you have given us is a wonderful treasure.

We have to take that treasure and give it away freely, and as we give it out the treasure increases. What a wonderful treasure you have given! We feel so much appreciation and love for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and by your mercy we hope to develop love for your Lord, Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

I know the only way to safely proceed along the path is to always think of you and pray to you to give us the intelligence to make the right decisions and treat others with humility and respect while trying to be free from offenses.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, please continue to guide your spiritual children and give us the mercy to continue in devotional service.

Your aspiring servants in the ISKCON temple in Canberra, Australia.  
(written by Ādi-puruṣa dāsa)

## Chandigarh

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Most dear and revered spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupādajī,

Kindly accept our insufficient attempt to glorify Your Divine Grace on this day celebrating your 108th Vyāsa-pūjā.

It is said that Śrīla Nārada-deva pervades space, inside and outside, almost like the Paramātmā, sunshine, and air:

*tvaṁ paryaṭann arka iva tri-lokīm*  
*antaś-caro vāyur ivātma-sākṣī*  
*parāvare brahmaṇi dharmato vrataiḥ*  
*snātasya me nyūnam alaṁ vicakṣva*

**“Like the sun, Your Goodness can travel everywhere in the three worlds, and like the air you can penetrate the internal region of everyone. As such, you are as good as the all-pervasive Supersoul. Please, therefore, find out the deficiency in me, despite my being absorbed in transcendence under disciplinary regulations and vows.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.5.7)**

Similarly, Your Divine Grace pervades the ISKCON world, inside and outside. For those who wish to stay connected to you, all means to do so are available: holy *dhāmas,* twenty-four-hour *kīrtanas,* temples, *yātrās,* festivals, *sādhu-saṅga,* books, videos, audios, construction, Deity worship, meetings, courses, *go-seva,* and, not the least, e-mails and the Internet.

And yet some of us, such as myself, manage to forget, bypass, overlook, or just plain disregard *all that connects us to Your Divine Grace and, through you, to the eternal glory,* just to grab at illusion, the *asat*. That is all it takes for us to suffer the insufferable: *a distinct disconnection from Your Divine Grace.*

But *mūḍhas* such as myself are just a few in number compared to the hundreds and thousands of new faces, the numerous temples, Nāma-haṭṭas, ISKCON Youth Federation members, and the multitude of simple common people getting connected every day to your movement. ISKCON, as always, is growing—spreading the holy names around.

Recently in North India a new center opened at Ludhiyana, Punjab. Thousands of people queued up to have *darśana* of Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Subhadrā-devī.

*Nagara-kīrtanas,* book distribution, school preaching, *prasādam* distribution, the Sunday feast, Ratha-yātrā and Janmāṣṭamī festivals, morning and evening programs—everything pure is available for those who do not wish to disconnect from you and your pure movement all over the world. Your devotees at Chandigarh and Ludhiyana are very, very grateful to you and hope to be absorbed in your service eternally. Kindly bless all of us on this important day of your holy appearance. *Oṁ tat sat*.

Your servants at the ISKCON centers in Chandigarh and Ludhiyana, India.  
(written by Bhaktivinoda dāsa)

## Chicago

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

The assembled devotees at Śrī Śrī Kiśora-Kiśorī Mandir, in Chicago, offer you our most humble obeisances and appreciation for all the amazing mercy you have bestowed upon us. Actually, if we look at the history of your appearances in Chicago, we see that they were filled with the most extraordinary pastimes. One such incident occurred just after you had named the Deities Kiśora-Kiśorī. The temple president, Śrī Govinda Prabhu, wrote you a letter. He was curious why everywhere else Rādhārāṇīs name is mentioned first when addressing the Deities—Rādhā-Govinda, Rādhā-Śyāmasundara, Rādhā-Gopīnātha—but in the case of Kiśora-Kiśorī Kṛṣṇa is mentioned first. You responded with the most amazing and delightful answer, which is full of pure *bhakti*. You said that in this case “We have saved the best for last.” Today we are remembering this story with great relish.

While you were traveling from Dallas to Chicago in 1975, you saw a cover of *Time* magazine that said “Crime: Why and What to Do?” Upon arriving and being interviewed by reporters, you boldly stated with clear conviction that you had the solution for the problem of crime. This attracted the attention of the mayor’s office, and the mayor sent a police lieutenant name Mr. Mozee to learn how to stop crime. When he asked you to describe your plan for stopping crime, you said to him, “You should give us the facility to chant the holy name of God and distribute *prasādam*. Then there will be a tremendous change in the population \[and crime will stop\].” Lieutenant Mozee very respectfully kept asking the question over and over, wanting to hear something different, but you repeated that through *harināma* and *prasādam* distribution, crime would be stopped. Lieutenant Mozee developed a genuine appreciation for you, but he could not understand your answers. Perhaps some day in the future society will understand that you have the solution to all problems.

Also, you shook up Chicago’s women’s liberation groups with your statements regarding the actual position of women according to the Vedic understanding. They sent reporter after reporter, many of them women, to question you on this issue. And you boldly stated that women’s liberation is simply a plot by men to exploit women by breaking down social norms. In one such interview a lady reporter, not accepting your explanation of women’s position, left in a very disturbed state of mind. You sent a devotee to accompany her and told him to mention to her that one cannot legislate men becoming pregnant. The press attacked you heavily, but you remained firm in your presentation of the Vedic truths.

Another interesting event happened here in July of 1975. The first and only *bābājī*initiation in ISKCON took place when you awarded Anulomi dāsa the title Anulomi dāsa Bābājī Mahārāja. Unfortunately, he was unable to keep his vows.

There are many other wonderful pastimes that you performed here in Chicago. Most wonderful was when you blessed the Windy City with the caress of your sweet lotus feet as you took part in the 1974 Ratha-yātrā downtown. But the most amazing thing, Śrīla Prabhupāda, (and the most fortunate thing for us) is that you are still with us, encouraging us, by your presence in the temple in the form of your *mūrti,* to push on Lord Caitanya’s *saṅkīrtana* movement as you guided us in the past.

Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, continue to bestow your mercy upon us. Your mercy is our only hope, our real treasure, and the source of our very existence. We humbly offer you our obeisances on this glorious occasion of the 108th anniversary of your appearance in this world.

Your fallen but most grateful servants at the Śrī Śrī Kiśora-Kiśorī Mandir in Chicago, Illinois, USA.

## Coimbatore

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most respectful obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace and the wonderful movement you have launched to take the whole world back home, back to Godhead.

I recently read one of your *līlās* wherein you mentioned that Kṛṣṇa could give ISKCON the whole world in eighteen days but that we were not prepared. We can see that great impediments are posed by our lack of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and our being not ready! Though you are ever ready, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to serve Kṛṣṇa under any circumstance and make volumes of sacrifices because of your love for Him, the expansion of your movement today is closely dependent on our furthering our devotion to Kṛṣṇa. This is a sweet burden of love that you have entangled us with, due to your mercy on us.

Here in Coimbatore and Kerala we are trying to serve you in this spirit under the guidance of your disciples. For your pleasure, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we conduct regular college and university programs, training sessions for the congregation, and programs for the schoolchildren under the educational wing of ISKCON. We can see that we are predominantly an educational institution, and we have so much to say on every subject matter from your books. Your books contain the solutions to all problems in this world !

Devotees are also endeavoring very much to distribute these wonderful books in Kerala and Coimbatore. In Guruvayur we are struggling to move our project ahead, to give the people there the mercy of Lord Caitanya. However, although sometimes the people there work against us, we are determined that with your blessings we will benefit them and embrace them within the folds of your wonderful *saṅkīrtana* movement.

It’s often mentioned that the grandfather is more merciful than the parents. Please forgive us for our laziness, carelessness, unintelligent behavior, and irresponsibility toward our duties in your movement. We, as your grandsons and granddaughters, constantly need your special mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to push on your movement and maintain our Kṛṣṇa consciousness simultaneously. There is so much to do, and at times we are overwhelmed with the services, Śrīla Prabhupāda. You have given us a whole world of services to do for Kṛṣṇa. It’s a new life, and we are happy serving you and being amidst so many devotees you have created for us to blissfully associate with. Simply let us appreciate the wonderful things you have given us and use them properly for Kṛṣṇa’s benefit.

Although our movement is going through challenging times, we have been sticking together to fight the forces arrayed against us, and we are always reminded of the spirit of loyalty to ISKCON you tried to inculcate in us, which you often expressed by quoting Cowper: “England, with all thy faults, I love thee still.” On this most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, please bless us to be stronger to face challenges, and to increase our Kṛṣṇa consciousness while remaining your instruments, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Thank you once again for everything, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

Your servants in the ISKCON centers in Coimbatore and Kerala, India.  
(written by Sarvaiśvarya dāsa)

## Coventry

**Śrīla Prabhupāda**

Words cannot praise such an amazing personality.  
You made Lord Caitanya’s words become reality.  
Without you we would have been suffering in the lap of Māyā,  
Full of ignorance, wanting to satisfy materialistic desire.  
If you had not come to the West to give us wisdom and preach,  
Attaining love of Godhead would be completely out of reach.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda, you’re exceptionally graceful and merciful because  
You enlightened the Western world about who Kṛṣṇa was.  
You spoke the words of Kṛṣṇa, following discipline succession,  
You taught how to control the senses, lust, ego, and aggression,  
You awakened many souls to become conscious of the Lord,  
You built a spiritual ship, welcoming everyone aboard.  
One must become self-realized to escape this world of frustration.  
Only by attachment to Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa can one gain liberation.  
With faith in your divine teachings, we are following your spiritual road,
