He taught her to chant *japa.* He borrowed all your books from the library (because his own had been put into a fire by his father) and gave them to the girl, who accepted them happily. Immediately she started to read these books day and night and chanted sixteen rounds. She told John not to be sad about her death, which would come soon, because we are all eternal spirit souls.

Soon her condition became worse and she had to be taken to the hospital. When she left her body, the *Bhagavad-gītā* was on her chest, and the doctors could hardly take it from her hand. What a fortunate soul she was! We cannot know her exact destination, but we can be sure that she is closer to Kṛṣṇa. And it is a all a result of your mercy, dear Prabhupāda! Your books possess inconceivable potency, which can give perfection even at the last moment of life. I wonder how many stories like this can be found in the world, stories we don’t know about but which tell about the glory of Kṛṣṇa consciousness?

Here we are in Szeged, a few devotees in the parching desert of materialism. Please bless us that we can adequately represent your teachings and lead more and more souls to the path of eternal happiness.

Your devotees in Szeged, Hungary.  
(written by Jayaśrī-devī dāsī)

## Tampa

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!  
Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!  
Empowered servant of the lord,  
Risked your life  
To please your Gurudev.  
You never looked back  
To comfort and enjoyment  
But moved like a rocket  
To change the world,  
Fulfilling the prediction  
Of Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura,  
Engaging the world in *saṅkīrtana,*  
Pushing the envelope,  
Chopping Māyās illusion,  
Unlocking the spiritual world  
With your purports,  
Searching for intelligent life,  
Collecting smart souls  
With your GBC and surrendered soldiers,  
You saved this one fool,  
But why me?  
I grabbed your books  
And never let go.  
Your footprints still mingle  
On this damaged earth,  
Evaporating sins.  
More so are your words  
Lingering in the air and minds  
As your BBT books  
Pass from hand to hand.  
“It is too late”—  
The books are distributed,  
An epidemic of enlightenment.  
Smooth and easy,  
Your books move about  
From printer to person,  
From eyes to mind.  
You started ashrams  
To give shelter  
To those who fully surrender  
Around the world.  
They are doors to the spiritual world;  
We are the caretakers,  
Guiding sincere souls  
Relieving the *karmīs*  
Of the imaginary burden,  
The only hope  
The kindness of Vaiṣṇavas.  
You are very clear, Prabhupāda,  
Emanating pure kindness  
To anyone who can hear.  
*Kaniṣṭha, Madhyama, Uttama.*  
Why be a *kaniṣṭha*?  
Let’s preach,  
Manifest your knowledge.  
“I didn’t say it would be easy.”  
It looks easy,  
Scheming and planning  
To engage all the fools,  
A society of animals,  
Beasts with two legs.  
What does that mean?  
They can’t chant 16.  
Your Gurudev said,  
“Don’t be disappointed  
So many *karmīs*—  
That is the way of this world.”  
We will not be diverted  
Away from your mission.  
To every home  
Your books are delivered.  
“Just fully surrender;  
Don’t waste time.  
Make your lifelong plans  
To serve the Divine.”  
Christians waiting for Jesus,  
Jews looking for the Messiah,  
Muslims crying for Allah.  
Are you all fools?  
Prabhupāda is here!  
If they only knew!  
If they only knew!  
If they only knew!  
Long live Śrīla Prabhupāda!  
You are always  
With your sincere disciples.  
Bless us, Prabhupāda,  
That we may be sincere.

*Your devotees in Tampa, Florida, USA.*  
(written by Vivasvān dāsa)

## Togo

**You Gave Us Faith**

Dear Jagad Guru Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Our prostrated obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. Jagad Guru Param Guru Mahā-patita-pāvana Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!!

*‘śraddhā-śabde—viśvāsa kahe sudṛḍha niścaya*  
*kṛṣṇe bhakti kaile sarva-karma kṛta haya*

Lord Caitanya defines faith in this verse as “the confidence that by rendering transcendental loving service to Kṛṣṇa, one automatically performs all subsidiary activities.”

Almost all those who surrendered to Kṛṣṇa through your mission demonstrated this level of faith. And for those who have unflinching faith in your words and those of the Supreme Lord, we see how the secrets of the *Vedas* became revealed to them. They carried on doggedly despite what was happening around them and despite all the distractions and mishaps.

You gave your disciples faith in Kṛṣṇa, and they in turn continue to share that faith. This mission is catching on because of the fire of faith that you have ignited. The flame is hard to put out, for it is being fed by the fuel of the faith of new generations of devotees.

You gave us faith through your Bhaktivedanta Purports. What can anyone do without your inspired words? Those who understand them are basking in transcendental activities, claiming one wonderful victory after another in the battle against Māyā, while those who do not are still running helter-skelter, looking for shelter.

Your glories and those of your empowered representatives continue to shine, affecting the hearts of many as one after another of your personal projects bears fruit. We saw faith in action when we witnessed the jubilation and spiritual ecstasies experienced by those who witnessed the Pañca-tattva installation in Māyāpur. Even those who were not so fortunate to be at the ceremony became enlivened just by hearing about it, hearing audiotapes of the ceremony, or seeing videos. Those who are already faithful had an upsurge of faith, while those on the borderline had their faith uplifted. Many also seized the occasion of the installation to make a pact with the Lord. From thousands of miles away they all sent prayers inviting the Pañca-tattva to manifest.

When you started this mission in America, you said, “If I make even only one pure devotee, my mission is successful.” Today it is clear to all, and especially the doubtful, that this movement continues to grow almost three decades after your disappearance because you have made many pure devotees. You once told your disciples that they should be surprised not that some devotees “blooped” but that many are able to continue nicely in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Today it is not only the devotees who are surprised at the growing list of your generals going back to Godhead, but I am sure the doubters are put to shame for their minimizing your greatness. Some of your disciples who have left this mortal plane have demonstrated to us the final lesson of how to leave this world remembering Kṛṣṇa. His Holiness Śrīdhara Mahārāja recently left this world, and his demise was like the passing away of Bhīṣmadeva. We see more and more the proof of your greatness as more and more of your disciples are attaining the Supreme goal.

What more do we need to keep our faith steady on the path? You have given us everything. You gave us faith in *nāma-kīrtana* for our purification. You created ISKCON so that we can continue to have *sādhu-saṅga* for continuous inspiration. You gave us *kṛṣṇa-smaraṇa* through your books. You made us fit for Deity *sevā*so that we can engage our senses in the service of the master of the senses, Hṛṣīkeśa. And finally you revealed to us our eternal home by giving us residence in the *dhāma*.

I pray that I may be blessed with a particle of your faith so that I can in turn give faith to many souls and connect them to your transcendental boat, which is ferrying lost souls back home, back to Godhead. Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for what you have given us. This year you have enabled us to realize more about the importance of *sādhu-saṅga* and Deity worship. Please protect us, your grand-disciples, from Vaiṣṇava *aparādha* and give us the necessary focus to be absorbed in the service of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and Rādhā-Govinda-Mādhava.

The servants of your servants in Togo, West Africa.  
(written by Varāha dāsa)

## Tokyo

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

We were lost in this world,  
Not a clue what to do,  
But by Kṛṣṇa’s mercy  
We’re now part of your crew.  
Your message is simple,  
Easy to understand:  
We are not this body  
And not part of this land.  
Give up the false ego,  
Birth, death, old age, and fear.  
Sooner we surrender,  
It will be crystal clear.  
Complete concentration  
On the Lord’s Holy Name,  
Complete meditation,  
Give up this mortal frame.  
Śrīla Prabhupāda  
Saved us from disaster  
With compassion and mercy—  
Our spiritual master.  
Just to see you again,  
So effulgent and bright,  
Speaking *kṛṣṇa-kathā,*  
A transcendental sight.

*From your fallen servants in Tokyo, Japan.*

## Toronto

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

We were floundering in the ocean of material existence. Death was just waiting to take us to our next body, which, under the circumstances, was likely to be more miserable than this one. You plucked us out of this ocean, and you have given us shelter in the shade of your lotus feet. Did we do something to deserve it? No, it is only by your causeless mercy that we are here. How can one thank that person who has saved his life? In the material world one owes his life to his savior, what to speak of a savior who has freed one from unlimited suffering.

So here we are offering our obeisances so many times every day, but when will that day come when we will actually obey you? Our lives belong to you, and yet we still seem to have desires that are not in line with your instructions. This is our most unfortunate situation. We can only become fortunate by rooting out these desires and replacing them with your desire. How can this be done? You have given us the means to accomplish this seemingly impossible feat: *ceto-darpaṇa-mārjanaṁ . . . paraṁ vijayate śrī-kṛṣṇa-saṅkīrtanam.* By our performing the congregational chanting of the Holy Name and giving the opportunity for devotional service to others, our hearts will become cleansed of our own wayward desires. Therefore the means and the end are the same. As the saying goes, if we “just do it,” then our lives will be successful in the endeavor.

What is stopping us? It is the inertia that comes from thinking we are these bodies. By remembering your instruction that our bodies are the source of our misery, we can motivate ourselves to overcome this inertia. Then it is possible we will be able to start pleasing you. You have given so much, but just as children learning to walk need the support of their parents, we need your help in our first steps toward our spiritual goal. Please pray to Kṛṣṇa to allow us to become servants of the family of Vaiṣṇavas. Your mercy is all that we are made of.

We, the devotees of ISKCON Toronto, take this opportunity to renew our pledge to you. May we measure up to your expectations by your mercy and that of Kṛṣṇa

Your servants at New Remuṇā Dhāma, ISKCON’s temple in Toronto, Canada.

## Tyumen

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this auspicious day of your appearance, please accept our humble obeisances unto your lotus feet.

One devotee who had some difficulties in his spiritual life revealed that the only thing that helped him overcome his difficulties was his firm faith in you. His firm faith in you made him continue to follow the process of *bhakti-yoga*. This faith is his great fortune, and it helps him overcome all his difficulties and get your mercy and complete shelter. Senior devotees often remember you and share with us their realizations about your unlimited power to instill faith in Kṛṣṇa. These devotees are very much inspired to serve your mission, and they show sincere attachment to you and love for you. Hearing the senior devotees, we may feel real love for you, for each other, and for all the devotees, and the success of Lord Caitanya’s mission becomes obvious. Thank you for your great mercy.

Not long ago a young devotee who is a talented musician took part in a discussion of Kṛṣṇa consciousness philosophy during the meeting of a *bhakti-vṛkṣa* group. When the devotees were sharing what really inspired them to serve Kṛṣṇa, this young devotee said that he is very much attached to you. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, your mercy is unconceivable! The devotees in Tyumen are trying to help you spread Kṛṣṇa consciousness in their region and city. During the last two years we built the temple of Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, where the devotees may also worship two *mūrtis* of Your Divine Grace. In the city of Nijnevartovsk a new preaching center was opened. In Tyumen three new *bhakti-vṛkṣa* groups were organized and meet every week. There are about forty devotees there. We have a Bhakti-yoga Club for presenting the Kṛṣṇa conscious philosophy to new people, and there is a popular Vaiṣṇava shop called White Lotus, where your books are being distributed. The devotees are also engaged in *prasādam* distribution. We recently began our preaching at the university and other schools of higher education.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are trying to serve you, but we always need your mercy and the mercy of the *paramparā.* On this auspicious day of your appearance we bow down low unto your lotus feet in a hope of getting your complete shelter. Only in connection with you may our activities be successful.

Your eternal servants at Prabhupāda-deśa, the ISKCON center in Tyumen, Russia.

## Udaipur

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances at the holy dust of your lotus feet.

This is a wonderful opportunity to glorify your great personality. By going to the Western world you transformed the course of this dark age. Your shining personality has dispelled the darkness of sinful life in the hearts of millions of conditioned souls.

The good fortune you have given us is impossible to repay. You are so dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa, having made Him famous all over the world. By your purity you have established so many ISKCON *dhāmas* in so many places of the world. You traveled incessantly, always preaching the message of Lord Caitanya. The whole world is grateful to you.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, I pray at your lotus feet that I can continue to serve and become more surrendered to the *saṅkīrtana* mission. Your books are the solace for the suffering conditioned souls. As you said, “They are like gold.” Once we grasp the deep meaning of their message, we will appreciate that they are the most valuable thing. Now their inner meaning is covered by the impurities in my contaminated heart, but by your mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda, please remove these obstacles and reveal the confidential understanding of devotional service, which is the only goal for the soul.

We wish to learn from you how to surrender to Śrī Kṛṣṇa, our most beloved friend and the supreme shelter of everyone. Again and again I offer my obeisances and appreciation for showing us the path of *bhakti-yoga*. You are glorified throughout the three worlds, and you are living eternally in our hearts. Thank you.

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Your faithful servants at the new temple in Udaipur, Tripura, India.  
(written by Durgama dāsa)

## Veracruz

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your divine lotuslike feet.

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

In one of your purports you advise us to approach a *sādhu* as follows:

One should approach a *sādhu* or great sage not to see but to hear him. If one is not prepared to hear the words of a *sādhu,* there is no profit. Śukadeva Gosvāmī was a *sādhu* who could speak on the transcendental activities of the Lord. He did not satisfy the whims of ordinary citizens. He was recognized when he spoke on the subject of *Bhāgavatam,* and he never attempted jugglery like a magician. \[*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.4.6 purport\]

Nowadays there is a fashion to make “*sādhu*” and “guru” by appointment of a group. The disciples of such appointed “*sādhus*” or “gurus” cannot even hear their words because they cannot speak the “*sādhu’s*” or the “guru’s” language. The followers only approach because of the way they look, dance, chant, and so on. Some of these “gurus” and “*sādhus*” even say that your methods of preaching are out of date.

We want to humbly submit a prayer to your divine lotus feet: Please do not let us make the same mistake as the unfortunate followers of these “gurus” and “*sādhus*.” We know deep within our hearts that the only means to be successful in this lifetime is to follow your preaching methods without the smallest change.

Every day before *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* class we chant the following prayer:

*nārāyaṇaṁ namaskṛtya naraṁ caiva narottamam*  
*devīṁ sarasvatīṁ vyāsaṁ tato jayam udīrayet*

**“Before reciting this** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,*** **which is the very means of conquest, one should offer respectful obeisances unto the Personality of Godhead, Nārāyaṇa, unto Nara-nārāyaṇa Ṛṣi, the supermost human being, unto mother Sarasvatī, the goddess of learning, and unto Śrīla Vyāsadeva, the author.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.2.4)**

This verse describes the way the *Bhāgavatam* has come down to us, until you came to the Western world to give us this same transcendental knowledge. Therefore we call this day Vyāsa-pūjā day, because you were empowered by Śrī Kṛṣṇa to deliver this transcendental knowledge, and so you are the modern Vyāsadeva.

Later in the *Bhāgavatam,* it is said:

*idaṁ bhāgavataṁ nāma purāṇaṁ brahma-sammitam*  
*uttama-śloka-caritaṁ cakāra bhagavān ṛṣiḥ*  
*niḥśreyasāya lokasya dhanyaṁ svasty-ayanaṁ mahat*

**“This** ***Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **is the literary incarnation of God, and it is compiled by Śrīla Vyāsadeva, the incarnation of God. It is meant for the ultimate good of all people, and it is all-successful, all-blissful, and all-perfect.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.3.40)**

Śrīla Vyāsadeva is known as a *mahā-muni,* not an ordinary sage but an extraordinary sage who is in fact an incarnation of God. Similarly, without any doubt you were empowered to translate and comment on the spotless *Purāṇa,* and also on the *Bhagavad-gītā*, *Śrī Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* and other books. All the knowledge you left us is meant to help us realize the truths of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Therefore we do not call this day your birthday; it is your appearance day, Vyāsa-pūjā day, because you came with divine grace to distribute transcendental knowledge throughout the world. All that Your Divine Grace has left us is meant for the ultimate good of all people, and it is all-successful, all-blissful, and all-perfect, now and in the future.

Therefore we all fall at Your Divine Grace’s lotus feet every year, every day, every hour, and every minute of our lives. We try to please you by reading every one of your books, lectures, conversations, and letters; in this way we try to understand Kṛṣṇa consciousness as you taught it and preach according to your successful methods.

This so-called servant of Your Divine Grace feels separation from you very strongly. We hope that in the near future we will once again sit before Your Divine Grace, listening to your strong voice glorifying Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Lord.

Your servants in Veracruz, Mexico.

## Villaggio Hare Kṛṣṇa

Dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you, my dear father, spiritual master, and ever well-wisher!

Since the devotees in Rome are preparing a nice festival commemorating your visit in Rome thirty years ago, my mind has gone back to those days, and as I dig into my memory, many sweet remembrances of you are coming to the surface.

You came to Rome in May 1974. Dhanañjaya Prabhu, his wife Bāla Gopāla dāsī, and I had opened the Rome temple only six months before. There were not even enough facilities or devotees to offer you a proper welcome, but you didn’t care: you came anyway because you had promised Dhanañjaya: “Go to Rome, find a house there, and write to me. I will come and stay with you.”

With those magic words of encouragement in our hearts, we came down to Rome and succeeded in our mission. To fulfill your promise you blessed us with your presence for one whole week in our small and very simple place, undisturbed by the discomforts and lack of facilities, always keeping up with your usual schedule of preaching strenuously in the day and translating at night. In this way you gave us a vivid example of how one should carry on with his service in any condition.

Every day, when you went out for your morning walk, I used to come into your room for some cleaning service, and I regularly noticed that your bed was almost intact, as if you didn’t even touch it. In fact, at night I could see from the terrace that the light in your room was always on: in the silence, when everyone was fast asleep, you were tirelessly dictating your translations and purports for the benefit of the whole world.

Such a great soul was living among us, whose actions we were witnessing with our very eyes, but we could hardly understand our inconceivable fortune. Now that your physical presence is no longer with us, I am thinking how many chances I had to render you some personal service, and I missed them. I regret so much! If you were personally present, as you were thirty years ago in Rome, I would serve your lotus feet in so many ways, trying to please you, but now I can do that only in my mind.

Just as Nṛsiṁhānanda Brahmacārī meditated to create a beautiful road full of flowers and precious gems for Lord Caitanya to walk on, I also often find myself meditating on how I would like to offer you a nice scented room full of all kinds of comforts where you could peacefully translate more books. I would also serve you your favorite *prasādam,* and from time to time I would come and bring you nice gifts like a new bead bag, a soft sweater, a warm pair of socks, and slippers for your lotus feet. In this way my mind gets lost in thinking of all the innumerable ways I could personally please you and then, for a moment, enjoy the broad smile that would illuminate your lotus face in reciprocation for my service.

In Paris, while giving me second initiation in June 1973, you looked surprised and pleased when Yogeśvara Prabhu told you that I was translating your books into Italian. “She knows Italian?” you asked him, and Yogeśvara answered, “She is Italian, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and she is translating your books.” “Oh, very nice!” you said, smiling, “she is making a good use of her womanly intelligence!”

Yes, in due course of time I did translate all your books, and now I am hankering so much to see your smile again because your pleasure alone is the purpose of my life. To please you is my only real happiness and the reason for which I live.

If you would descend personally here among us once again, I would also send you a beautiful letter relating all that I’ve done in these long thirty years of separation, and I would invite you to come again to Italy so I could show you all the progress we’ve made—the books we’ve published and distributed, the temples we’ve acquired, the Deities we’ve installed, and the devotees we’ve made—all by your pure desire.

Only three of us came to Rome in the very beginning. Then many more came by your mercy. Some stayed, some left, but those who are still here are faithfully pushing on your mission.

I will end my homage by frankly saying that in all these years I have never actually felt independent of you because I cannot identify myself as anything else but an instrument in your hands. Therefore, Śrīla Prabhupāda, my humble prayer today is that you may continue to use me in your service at your sweet will, and keep this poor servant in the shadow of your lotus feet. I have no other desires.

*The devotees who are faithfully serving Śrīla Prabhupāda’s mission in Villagio Hare Kṛṣṇa, Italy.*  
(written by Āli-Kṛṣṇa-devī dāsī)

## Vṛndāvana

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet, whose shade is as cooling as millions of full moons. All glories to you, our eternal guide and master, and all glories to your instructions, which light the path back to Godhead!

Your servants at Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma Mandir are especially blessed with your perpetual guidance because you gave us many instructions for this project. You called ISKCON Vṛndāvana the “beating heart of ISKCON.” Anatomically the heart has many functions, and similarly ISKCON Vṛndāvana is meant to fulfill those demands for the corpus of the society. The heart is also the seat of the Supersoul; similarly, Vṛndāvana is your eternal residence as a representative of the Supreme Lord. You are guiding us from within and without for the development of Vṛndāvana. Our responsibility through the years has been to carefully carry out your instructions, according to your specific vision for development.

We are studying carefully, trying to understand your wishes for Vṛndāvana. Hundreds of quotations have been collected and condensed into key directions, particularly in the field of education. These directions, these guidelines, have become the fiber of our temple master plan, or you may say in this case, the Spiritual Master Plan, because our aim is to carry out *your* plan for Vṛndāvana.

We have tried to involve an international team of senior devotees to gain insight into fulfilling your desires. For instance, just one sentence you wrote on 6 November 1976 is an example of the cooperation needed for success: “The center will include a Vedic library, theater, prasadam restaurant, gurukula school, an international guesthouse, as well as a temple and *āśrama*.” We are concentrating on developing ISKCON Vṛndāvana to now have all of these facilities.

From as early as 1969, you had a plan for an educational institution in Vṛndāvana:

The idea is that when we open our educational institute, we will require some dedicated monks, just like Christian Fathers, who have no connection with women. In that case, we can start a theological school also, along with a boys’ primary school. Our theological school will regularly teach our published books, such as *Bhagavad-gita As It Is, Śrīmad-Bhagavatam, Teachings of Lord Caitanya, Nectar of Devotion, Brahma Samhita,* and *Krishna*. We wish to hold examinations and award titles such as Bhakti-sastri, Bhakti-vaibhava, and Bhaktivedanta. The whole program will continue for seven years. . . . Actually we require many enlightened students who can go to any part of the world and establish there a center. . . . \[Letter: 4 February 1969\]

Plans are now under way for such an international leadership *āśrama* college. This was your vision for education, as you further described in a letter dated 23 August 1971:

The aim is to make it an international institute for taking perfect spiritual order of life; we shall give titles like bachelor of divinity, master of divinity, doctor of divinity. . . . Our mission is solid. Our philosophy is not utopian. Our men are being trained for exemplary character. So we shall have a unique position all over the world provided we stick to the principles, namely unflinching faith in Spiritual Master and Kṛṣṇa, chanting not less than 16 rounds regularly, and following the regulative principles. Then our men will conquer all over the world.

You have given the framework; we will try to fill it in appropriately. This will be a valuable service for the entire society, for all of mankind—to train spiritual leaders for worldwide preaching. As the heart rejuvenates the body, such a concentrated training program will circulate strong Kṛṣṇa conscious preachers worldwide.

Along with educational endeavors, we are trying to carry out your instructions to distribute *caraṇāmṛta* and *prasādam* to all the temple’s guests, to keep everything very clean, to see that nothing is wasted, and to maintain a high standard of neatness and cleanliness in temple worship. We hope that if we please you, then Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma, Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Śyāmasundara and Lalitā and Viśākhā, and Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi will be pleased, and the world will be infused with Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

On this most auspicious day of Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā, we pray for your guidance, your blessings, and most important, “unflinching faith in Spiritual Master and Kṛṣṇa,” the key principle for conquering the world with Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your servants at ISKCON Vṛndāvana, India.

## Wales

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

There are many great devotees present in our movement, and through the course of history many glorious Vaiṣṇavas have manifested their pastimes of delivering conditioned souls in this world. However, we, as your faithful servants, can proudly say that the service you rendered to mankind cannot be excelled by anyone. It is a fact that any person outside of India who knows anything about pure devotional service, or has the opportunity to render some devotional service, either knowingly or unknowingly, has only been able to do so as a result of your mercy. The Supreme Lord Himself has chosen to leave the task of delivering the Western countries, nay the entire world, to you. This is because he wants you, his most dear and confidential devotee, to be glorified. How wonderfully Kṛṣṇa arranges everything! Rather than be glorified Himself, Kṛṣṇa experiences more pleasure seeing his dear devotee worshiped and praised.

In the endeavor to please you, we have firmly established a small temple and restaurant in Swansea, the second largest city of Wales. Having achieved this goal, we are now beginning our next major project of setting up a preaching center in Cardiff, the capital of Wales. We have been distributing your books in Cardiff for at least eighteen years, and now, by the combined effort of the devotees, we are happy to inform you of the forthcoming opening of ISKCON Cardiff. We pray that you may kindly bless us so that we may be successful in our endeavors to attract conditioned souls to the service of the Lord, and also so that we may receive your mercy and develop love for Kṛṣṇa.

Begging for the eternal service of your servants, we offer you our humble obeisances again and again.

Your servants at ISKCON Wales.

## Warkworth

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your face: never have I watched a face so closely,  
tried to see into every pore—  
those little moles on cheeks and underneath one eye,  
that lightning-bolt blue vein that leaps from ears to your golden brow—  
ever-moistened eyes and never-ceasing play of lips, generous and sculptured;  
never did I see before or since a soul express ten-thousand shades  
of smile—and scowl, and pain and ease and wonder—  
under a crumpled chaddar with 3-days’ growth in an unheated room in Vṛndāvana  
or bright-faced basking in Hawaiian noon with mangoes on your table.  
And oh! Those poses, those noble poses: regally reclined, right forearm resting on top of your head, relishing the day’s events with your little pack of children  
or striding out on public display, chin erect and forward, ready to take on the world.  
And then you speak! The words that answer every question, like opening the door:  
everything so clear and simple, easily in place.  
So sweetly do you grace us with the sacred words, and give us too our work:  
to spread these words around.  
O Swamiji take me with you into those magic days and nights:  
when there is no time but Kṛṣṇa’s time  
where all things happen by His design, laughter rings and  
nothing is impossible—  
To a tiny room by Red Square where you toppled fear and redesigned a nation,  
To the Corrida at Cuernavaca where you dropped fear to its knees with your sword,  
To that rolling field in Hertfordshire where you set your children free . . .  
O Prabhupāda take us with you always into magic days and nights:  
hatching plans to expand your words, grabbing the red-eye for Africa;  
cooking up strategy, reckless and laughing, weighing the pros and cons,  
portioning time into one-way tickets,  
dosing your children with love—  
Gathered together as one mighty force,  
a century and eight years later:  
The secret of secrets is safe in our hands  
for as long as we see  
perfect symmetry:  
the fascinating beauty of your face.

Your servants at ISKCON Warkworth, New Zealand.  
(written by Śyāmasundara dāsa)

## Wiesbaden

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at your lotus feet!

On this very auspicious day of your appearance, we would like to thank you for your causeless mercy and for allowing us to serve Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa and His devotees.

In the spiritual world no one gets sick, therefore there is no need of a doctor. But in this material world you are the doctor who gives the medicine of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and heals us and thousands of other lost, fallen souls every day.

What would the Western world be without you!

Beloved spiritual master, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we thank you wholeheartedly for this.

May you always live in our hearts.

May we be able to enjoy your mercy for all eternity, because without it our lives are simply useless!

Your servants at the ISKCON community in Wiesbaden, Germany.

## Yogyakarta

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories to Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga!

All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Since the installation here on 21 February 2003 of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi and your personal *mūrti,* your transcendental bhajans on the CD “Śrīla Prabhupāda in Kṛṣṇa Meditation” have been repeatedly heard every single day. This is a special feature in this *āśrama*—that from morning till evening, all resident devotees and guests who happen to come get to associate with you through your transcendental sound vibration. All of this is possible because of the special mercy of your representatives, who desire to spread the holy name of Lord Kṛṣṇa and push on the *saṅkīrtana* mission of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu.

Today, O Śrīla Prabhupāda, is the beginning of Śālagrāma and Tulasī Jaladāna. This morning, again and again, I heard your sweet chanting of *jaya rādhe jaya kṛṣṇa, jaya vṛndāvan/ śrī govinda gopīnātha madana-mohana*. I listened to the song very attentively as I performed *ārati* after offering the morning *bhoga* to Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi. I felt so very happy. At that time I thought, “How can this happen—how can I feel so very, very happy?” And then I thought “Oh, when will I be able to again taste such happiness?”

I am very sorry, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that I never felt this way before. I understand that getting this feeling of happiness is possible only by your causeless mercy and that of your Vaiṣṇava representatives, whom you have sent simply to help deliver me. I don’t know how to thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please keep me engaged in your devotional service and never kick me away from my unworthy service.

I take shelter at your lotus feet and those of your servants,

Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!

Your servants in Yogyakarta, Indonesia.  
(written by Garuḍa dāsa)

## Vyāsa-pūjā Homage from the Publishers

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Middle East)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances beneath the dust of your divine lotus feet. All glories to your ever-present mercy, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Another year passes, more and more blessings of service fall upon us, and it becomes more and more clear just how important it is to actually accomplish something for Your Divine Grace. You ask the Lord to give us His eternal devotional service, and then we make the choice whether or not to make a sincere effort to serve. Even though it is for our own eternal benefit, we resist. I resist. Some infinitesimal service is there, but here we are, begging you again for the desire to serve much more seriously, with deep intelligence and great enthusiasm. To bring a broad smile to your lotus face—that is the goal, Śrīla Prabhupāda!

I am happy to report that there are two new translations of your books at the printer as I write this: *The Nectar of Instruction* (Greek) and *The Perfection of Yoga* (Farsi). Gradually we plan to print and distribute many more of your books in these languages, as well as in Arabic and the other languages of our BBT division. But I shamelessly must beg you yet again, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for your mercy to allow this to happen. Or rather, for your mercy to allow us to boldly take the correct actions, both for your service and in our lives. Your sweet, divine and eternal mercy is there for the taking—Lord knows it is time for us to take it!

Your servants at Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Middle East).  
(written by Satyanārāyaṇa dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (North America)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Each year we realize more and more how merciful you are to keep us in the service of your books. They are creating a revolution in this misdirected civilization. The Age of Quarrel is in full swing, Śrīla Prabhupāda, and the only hope is the countervailing influence of the empowered words in your books. When we read them, we are always moved to realize that these aren’t just your words but those of the whole *paramparā* you are delivering to us. All these *mahājanas* in the disciplic succession are our masters, and by hearing your words we also hear theirs, and the timeless message of Kṛṣṇa consciousness enters our hearts.

With these ideas in mind, we were moved to compose a little poem for your pleasure:

As years go by and books go out,  
We see what life is all about:  
To serve the mission of our lords  
By spreading wide their sacred words.

As well they want us all to strive  
To let those words direct our lives.  
This safest path will lead us Home,  
No more in Māyās realm to roam.

Also for your pleasure, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we present this summary of books printed and distributed in 2003 by the North American branch of your BBT:

Last year the BBT printed or helped print 1,191,700 books (some new, some reprints), the most in fifteen years. Most of the twenty-five titles were printed overseas very economically, resulting in a record-low per-book price to temples, with no loss in quality. The books include *Beyond Birth and Death;* the Spanish *Science of Self-Realization; The Reservoir of Pleasure; Śrīla Prabhupāda Līlāmṛta* in two volumes; the deluxe *Kṛṣṇa* book; *Civilization and Transcendence; Coming Back;* the softback *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is;* the commemorative *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*; *Introduction to the Bhagavad-gītā*; *Bhagavad-gītā* verse book; hardback *Kṛṣṇa* book in two volumes; *Hare Kṛṣṇa Vegetarian Cooking; Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 30-volume sets; *Songs of the Vaiṣṇava Ācāryas*; *Bṛhad-Bhāgavatāmṛta,* Part 2, Volume 1; *The Library of Vedic Culture; Caitanya-caritāmṛta* in one volume; Vyāsa-pūjā 2003; the BBT Calendar 2004; the Spanish *Kṛṣṇa consciousness, the Topmost Yoga System;* the Spanish *Easy Journey to Other Planets;* and a Spanish cookbook.

The 47 temples that regularly bought books from the BBT in North America distributed 992,265 pieces of literature in 2003. One interesting fact is that the results reflect the efforts not of just one or two temples, or a particular zone, but of every temple in North America, from Vancouver to Miami, Montreal to San Diego: all pitched in with a very sincere effort to please you through book distribution. All 47 temples in North America did their level best to fulfill your order of that every man and woman get a book!

There were some very bright spots for the year and December distribution: Chicago, Montreal, Toronto, New Tālavan, New York, Tucson, and Atlanta were all big surprises for December. And many temples and parties put in a valiant effort though they were practically one-man *saṅkīrtana* shows. The greatest percentage increases for the year were shown by Los Angeles, Chicago, Tucson, and Puerto Rico.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, our greatest pleasure is to see more and more of your books being distributed into the hands of the conditioned souls. Please let us continue to take part in this glorious *saṅkīrtana* movement, birth after birth.

Your servants at the North American offices of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust.

## Bhaktivedanta Book Trust (Northern Europe)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

You have always been so eager to hear about the production of your books and their distribution. His Holiness Jayādvaita Mahārāja has suggested that this year the BBTs include some book production and distribution facts and figures in our Vyāsa pūjā offerings to you. We think this is an excellent idea and hope that you are pleased by our efforts and the efforts of your dearmost servants, the book distributors. Although a lot of books were distributed, we hear you saying “Double it! Double it!” Please give us the confidence and strength to achieve this goal.

Below is a graph of the books distributed over the last three years in the area served by the North European BBT, which includes the following countries: Albania, Armenia, Austria, Azerbaijan, Bosnia-Herzegovina, Bulgaria, Belarus, Cambodia, French-speaking Canada, Croatia, Czech Republic, Denmark, England, Estonia, Finland, France, Georgia, Germany, Hungary, Iceland, Indonesia, Ireland, Kazakhstan, Kyrgyzstan, Korea, Laos, Latvia, Liechtenstein, Lithuania, Macedonia, Moldova, Mongolia, Northern Ireland, Norway, Poland, Romania, Russia, Scotland, Serbia, Slovakia, Slovenia, Sweden, Switzerland, Tajikistan, Turkmenistan, Ukraine, Uzbekistan, and Wales.

We have also donated 120,000 French books to the French-speaking parts of Africa to encourage large-scale book distribution there. May these books quickly find their way into the hearts of the people there, and may they inspire many people to become devotees and enthusiastic book distributors.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

We offer our loving obeisances unto your lotus feet. And we thank you for your love and faith to your spiritual master.

Nārada Muni instructed Śrīla Vyāsadeva:

The Supreme Lord is unlimited. Only a very expert personality, retired from the activities of material happiness, deserves to understand this knowledge of spiritual values. Therefore those who are not so well situated, due to material attachment, should be shown the ways of transcendental realization, by Your Goodness, through description of the transcendental activities of the Supreme Lord. (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.5.16)

Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī Mahārāja, your Guru Mahārāja, instructed you: *āmar icchā chila kichu bai karāna*. “I had a desire to print some books. If you ever get money, print books.” And in his last letter to you he wrote:

I have every hope that you can turn yourself into a very good English preacher if you serve the mission to inculcate the novel impression of Lord Chaitanya’s teachings in the people in general as well as philosophers and religionists. (*Śrīla Prabhupāda-līlāmṛta*)

Out of love you took up the instructions that were delivered to you in the *guru-paramparā*. You accepted them as your life’s goal. And you have succeeded. You have fulfilled your Guru Mahārāja’s desires.

The two examples mentioned above, in which a disciple gets an instruction from his spiritual master, were historic for mankind.

Śrīla Vyāsadeva compiled the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* in accordance with Nārada Muni’s desire. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, expanded that effort in accordance with Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatīs desires. Your translations into English and your commentaries are ground-breaking. They are wonderful transcendental manifestations of mercy.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have made the spiritual world accessible here on this planet. You have brought us in direct contact with the Lord’s literary incarnation. The spiritual world is hidden from mundane eyes. Yet it exists and is real. The sound vibrations in your literature directly reveal to us the hidden splendor of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. These sound vibrations are now present all over the world.

To date, 400 million books have been distributed, and that is just the beginning. Your works are in libraries, in universities, in schools, in private homes, on the Internet, in the ether, and in purified minds. Your works instantly purify and spiritualize. They give light and guidance in the darkness of the Age of Kali.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have given the greatest gift of love to all human beings. You instill spiritual desire in us. You are planting the seeds of devotion. And you nurture our devotional creepers.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, your love for Kṛṣṇa is as vast as the limitless number of His parts and parcels. Your love is the same magnanimous love that Lord Caitanya has shared. We thank our sweet Lord Kṛṣṇa that he has sent you. And we thank you with immense gratitude for the love you share with us.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are the greatest inspirational power. Please enliven us at your Bhaktivedanta Book Trust “to discover novel ways and means to convert the nondevotees in terms of particular time and circumstances.”

Please bless us with creative inspiration and spiritual strength and intelligence to carry on in our services.

Please show us the way to grow in true and pure love.

Your servants at the North European offices of the Bhaktivedanta Book Trust.

## Vyāsa-Pūjā Homages from Other Sources

## Associazione Ritorno a Krishna

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances unto your lotus feet on this auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

On this joyful occasion we have the opportunity to renovate our love for the teachings that you, out of mercy, gave us. They enliven our inner self and give us peace and hope. I pray to God to grant us, by His grace, the ability to serve you. I offer my respectful obeisances unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who are the authentic representative of Kṛṣṇa.

We deeply thank Lord Nityānanda for manifesting all the qualities of a devotee through your holy person, and we thank you for showing the transcendental pastimes and nectarean exchanges of love with your spiritual children, and for revealing, in this way, how to serve them. I offer my respectful obeisances unto you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because you are an ocean of auspicious qualities.

Out of your unlimited compassion, you appeared in this universe just to bless the entire world and free it from all miseries. I offer my respectful obeisances unto your lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda, because without your mercy there can be no spiritual advancement. Offering ourselves unto the Supreme will of the Lord, we pray to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, to kindly give us the strength to overcome all obstacles on the path of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Your servants at Associazione Ritorno a Krishna, in Rome, Italy.  
(written by Raghubīr dāsa)

## Back to Godhead Magazine

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

This year marks the sixtieth anniversary of your inspired founding of *Back to Godhead*. Those of us who try to carry out your vision for BTG feel truly honored that you allow us to serve in this way. And we acknowledge the weight of our responsibility to preserve the spiritual integrity of the magazine as we search for ways to increase its appeal. “Where there is Godhead there is no nescience.” The nescient world needs BTG, but the world doesn’t know it yet. We beg for your blessings so that we can more widely shine the rays of BTG.

You started *Back to Godhead* because you saw the need for a magazine dedicated to pure Kṛṣṇa consciousness. Today, BTG is still the only widely read magazine that satisfies that need. It remains unique. Unlike so many magazines on the market, it’s not about generic, watered-down spirituality. It focuses now, as it did sixty years ago, on the clear, potent teachings of Lord Caitanya. BTG will always boldly proclaim the essential message that Lord Kṛṣṇa is the Supreme Personality of Godhead and that surrender unto Him is the goal of all genuine spiritual practice. As BTG and ISKCON’s other preaching efforts convince people of the truth of that message, the distribution of BTG will grow and grow.

We want to please you with our humble efforts, but we are powerless without your help. Please give us the Kṛṣṇa conscious intelligence, enthusiasm, and determination to carry out your mission for *Back to Godhead*.

Your humble servants, The Back to Godhead Staff.

## Bhaktivedanta Archives

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories to Your Divine Grace, who have descended to save a world mired in illusion. The brilliance of your message is able to deliver us from the wars, famines, pestilence, and death that surround us. The sun planet, which generates the energies that power life on this earth, is as nothing to the light that shines from your words. The books that you have given us are our salvation from this darkness of Kali-yuga.

In the short time that you were within our earthly vision, your mercy spread the message of Lord Caitanya to distant corners of the world, and also into the hearts and minds of so many suffering conditioned souls. Though it is hard to understand how you accomplished this, you made it look easy. You made the purport of the *Vedas* understandable to ordinary people. This *rāja-vidyā,* king of knowledge, and this *rāja-guhyam,* the most confidential of secrets, have a childlike simplicity to those who read your books and follow your teachings. This is due to your compassion and desire to deliver others. Kṛṣṇa reciprocates with you by guiding the conditioned souls to the understanding necessary for their deliverance.

We pray that we can obtain your mercy by serving your mission. We are not very expert, nor devotional, nor compassionate. Thus our service to you cannot accomplish very much. It is only by developing some sincerity to serve you that we can do things that will have any value. We pray to Kṛṣṇa that your words and example of pure devotion stay in our hearts and minds for the duration of this life, so that at the end we may remember and be saved.

Your humble servants at The Bhaktivedanta Archives.

## Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture (South Africa)

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Loving Grace! All glories to your Śrī Vyāsa-pūjā!

*Nāma-saṅkīrtana* in every town and village is Mahāprabhu’s desire,  
To rescue those trapped within the blazing material fire.  
By your desire and His favor it is coming true,  
As varied races bow to Kṛṣṇa, that boy blue!  
You empowered your followers to distribute your books.  
Now, too, they are being studied in every nook,  
Teaching many how to chant the Holy Name.  
By your mercy so many are being reclaimed.  
Boldly your purports proclaim life’s aim,  
Leading so many to surrender with no personal gain.  
The absolute truth you give in your purports,  
Systematically erasing the student’s doubts.  
Your words dispel illusion and us to reality shock,  
Repeating the teachings of previous *ācāryas,* you never concoct.  
To boil the milk until it’s thick was your desire.  
To become like Śrī Rūpa and Śrī Sanātana you wanted us to aspire.  
Despite our lack of literary worth, we submit this poem, bow and pray  
That we may be of continued service to you day after day.  
May our hearts thus become purified, reaching past *bhakti’s* first rays,  
Fixed in your mood and mission we beg to stay and never stray.

**An Offering at Your Lotus Feet:**

**Preaching Report**

*mūkaṁ karoti vācālaṁ paṅguṁ laṅghayate girim*  
*yat-kṛpā tam ahaṁ vande śrī-guruṁ dīna-tāraṇam*

**By the mercy of the guru, even a dumb man can become the greatest orator, and even a lame man can cross mountains. As advised by Lord Brahmā, one should remember this śāstric injunction if one desires success in his life. \[*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **6.7.23 purport\]**

By your causeless mercy, during 2003 the Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture conducted structured courses in Chandigarh; Chowpatty (Mumbai); Durban; Guyana; Houston; Johannesburg; Juhu; Kanpur; Nairobi; Kisumu, Kenya; Guadalajara (Mexico); Mexico City; Montreal; New Delhi; Pietermaritzburg; Pilani, Rajasthan; Trinidad; Toronto; Vancouver; and Vṛndāvana. Internationally, a total of 8,111 students have graduated over the past fourteen years. We feel that this is so few out of so many conditioned souls. Hence we humbly fall at your lotus feet and pray that by your mercy we may be able to continue and increase our service to your mission despite our limitations.

Your servants at Bhaktivedanta College of Education and Culture, South Africa.  
(offering composed by Mādrī-devī dāsī)

## Bhaktivedanta College (Radhadesh)

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

On this day of your divine appearance, all of us—the students, teachers, and administrators of Bhaktivedanta College, in Rāḍhadesh—submit ourselves happily before you with humble obeisance.

In this second year of the College, we are happy to report near-completion of the process of affiliation with the University of Wales, Lampeter. By this arrangement, this year’s graduates will receive certificates recognized by that institution (and hence throughout Europe); then after completing three years of study at the College, students will receive a B.A. in Vaiṣṇava theology from Lampeter. We all have great hope that these accomplishments are pleasing to Your Divine Grace.

The central core of Bhaktivedanta College’s curriculum is, of course, your own teachings, comprehensively elaborated in your books. The main four of these—*Bhagavad-gītā As It Is, Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam, Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* and *The Nectar of Devotion—*you identified as a unity, to be imbibed carefully and thoroughly by all your students, i.e., your initiated disciples. In the spirit of celebrating your accomplishment and remembering the centrality of this fourfold unity, we humbly offer you the following song. We beg you to overlook its faults:

**Prabhupāder Catur-grantha Gītā**

(can be sung to the tune of “Guru-vandana”)

*arjunera duḥkha haite, aṣṭadaśadhyāya madhye*  
*kṛṣṇa-vākya amṛta-sāgara*  
*sarva-dharma-vidhi-tyāga, kṛṣṇa-vāñcha mukhya aṅga*  
*kṛṣṇa-bhakti ei śreṣṭha upāya*

*vyāsadever grantha-varya, prasiddha śrī-bhāgavata*  
*sarva-śāstra-śiromaṇi sāra*  
*śuniyā ei kṛṣṇa-kathā, prema-bhakti upajaya*  
*hṛdoy bahir rahe śoka-moha*

*kṛṣṇadāser paraṁ kāvya, caitanyera itihāsa*  
*gauḍa-deśa-vaiṣṇaver ākara*  
*gaurāṅgera tattva jāne, dāser anudāsa-bhāve*  
*śuddha-bhakti habe sarva-loke*

*rūp-gosaier rasa-grantha, rasera viveka-panthā*  
*bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu nāma*  
*vidhi, rāga, bhāva, prema, krame sab vistāra-kathā*  
*tabe bhakter hṛdoya ujjvala*

1\. The *Bhagavad-gītā* is an ocean of Lord Kṛṣṇa’s nectarean words, spoken (lit: obtainable) in eighteen chapters to assuage Arjuna’s lamentation. Giving up the strictures of all (other) principles of religion, (one should accept the *Gītā’s*) central principle: (to serve) Kṛṣṇa’s desire. Devotion to Kṛṣṇa is the ultimate means (of spiritual success).

2\. The famous *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is Śrīla Vyāsadeva’s most excellent work, the essence of all topmost scriptures. (In the heart of one who) hears its narrations about Kṛṣṇa, pure loving devotion arises, as lamentation and illusion remain far away from the heart.

3\. Kṛṣṇadāsa Kavirāja’s sublime work of poetry describing the history of Caitanya Mahāprabhu is a mine (of inspiration) for the Vaiṣṇavas of the Gauḍīya tradition. (By reading it) one comes to know the truth of Gaurāṅga and attains the mood of being a servant of the servant; thus pure *bhakti* comes about for everyone.

4\. Rūpa Gosvāmīs relishable work of the name *Bhakti-rasāmṛta-sindhu* shows the way to carefully analyze devotional mellows. It is an expanded progressive explanation of devotion in the stages of regulation, attachment, intense devotion, and pure love. The hearts of the devotees who read it become illuminated.

In last year’s Vyāsa-pūjā offering from Bhaktivedanta College, each of the nine students addressed you as “Śrīla Prabhupāda, my teacher.” It is you, as our actual teacher, who have set the foundational curriculum and clarified the aim of all learning in the pages of these four books. Please continue to bless us individually and collectively to act as your instruments in churning the nectar of these books, such that we can properly imbibe their meaning and purpose ourselves, and properly help to convey their meaning and purpose to others.

Your servants at Bhaktivedanta College, Rāḍhadesh, Belgium.  
(written by Kṛṣṇa Kṣetra dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Cultural and Educational Library

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

We at the Bhaktivedanta Cultural and Educational Library in Rome wish to ask your blessings on this auspicious day that we may never forget all you have given us, and that we may use every facility granted us by the Supreme Lord to serve you.

A special mercy of Śrī Kṛṣṇa was manifest to us four years ago when the Comune of Rome allotted a truly worthy space in which to set up the Bhaktivedanta Library, where the immense wealth of the *bhāgavata* culture is made accessible to everyone. Located in the heart of Rome, the library now offers a reading room and conference hall with over 2,000 volumes, in various languages, on Vaiṣṇavism, Philosophy, Indian Psychology, Āyurveda, Vedic science, interreligious dialogue, and vegetarianism. There is even one room set aside as an Āyurvedic tea room. In addition to the lending library and tea room, in the conference hall conferences and courses are held daily on the traditional arts and sciences of India, such as Vedic astrology, *aṣṭāṅga-yoga, mṛdaṅga* playing, and Sanskrit in collaboration with the Centro Studi Bhaktivedanta. We also function as an archive for researchers on the study of ISKCON in Italy.

Our primary objective now is to obtain recognition by the Library Systems of the Comune of Rome as a specialized local library. This would result in annual financing for the maintenance and development of the library.

Again, we ask your blessings that our efforts in this endeavor may be pleasing to you and all the devotees of the Lord.

Sincerely,  
*Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Cultural and Educational Library, Rome, Italy.*  
(written by Ekādaśīnāth dāsa)

## Bhaktivedanta Gurukula and International School

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

**A Million Times I Can Never Repay**

As I progress along the *bhakti* way  
It becomes as clear to me as the light of day  
That a hundred times I can never repay  
The gift that you have given me today.  
As I stand before Their Lordships and pray,  
Watching Their dance and pose of sway,  
A thousand times I can never repay  
The chance that you have given me today.  
As I read your wonderful books every day  
And feel them penetrate my mind of clay,  
A million times I can never repay  
The knowledge you have given me today.  
As I hear those with loyal hearts clearly say  
“We will stand with Śrīla Prabhupāda come what may,”  
I know a billion times I can never repay  
The love that you have given me today.  
If I could gather the whole universe and say  
“This is an offering of gratitude today,”  
Still that wouldn’t even begin to repay  
The joyous life of service you have given me today.  
I bow down my head and humbly pray  
That you use me in your mission in your own expert way  
To give *bhakti* to others and assure them it’s okay,  
That there’s hope in this dismal world of decay.  
And thus I may hope in some way to say,  
With gratitude and sincerity on this auspicious day,  
“Thank you for the gift of true life and right way,  
And may my dedicated service be an effort to repay.”

*The staff and students of Bhaktivedanta Gurukula & International School, Vṛndāvana, India.*  
(written by Ānanda Vṛndāvaneśvarī-devī dāsī)

## Bhaktivedanta Institute (Kolkata)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances! All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Out of your causeless mercy you have blessed this lowly servant with transcendental instructions on spreading Kṛṣṇa consciousness among the intellectual class of men, and on making Manipur a Kṛṣṇa conscious state. Today, on the occasion of the 108thVyāsa-pūjā celebration of Your Divine Grace, I pray for your unlimited blessings so that I may humbly execute your divine orders to spread the message of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu among the scientists and scholars around the world.

By your divine blessings, I have shifted my activities in the service of the Bhaktivedanta Institute to Kolkata. In Kolkata, I have been able to humbly execute some of the transcendental instructions that Your Divine Grace has mercifully given to me. I am submitting a brief report for the pleasure of Your Divine Grace.

In the year 2000, we published *Thoughts on the Synthesis of Science and Religion,* a compilation of articles presented at the Second International Congress on the Synthesis of Science and Religion (which was organized in 1997 as part of the Centennial celebrations of the appearance of Your Divine Grace). This publication contains thoughts on science and religion by five Nobel Laureates and several other important scholars from around the world. It has attracted the worldwide attention of distinguished scientists and scholars, including that of the President of India, Dr. A. P. J. Abdul Kalam. When I had the opportunity to meet with the President, I was able to discuss with him many issues regarding science and religion. He very much appreciated the efforts of the Bhaktivedanta Institute towards promoting the synthesis of science and spirituality for solving the problems of the world.

The BI in Kolkata has also recently published the first issue of the Journal of the Bhaktivedanta Institute, *Savijñānam: Scientific Exploration for a Spiritual Paradigm*, the brainchild of Your Divine Grace. It has received an overwhelming response and great respect internationally.

The main highlights of the journal are:

(1) At least one interview or dialogue by a Nobel Laureate or scientist of that stature, and;

(2) Scientific commentary on at least one aphorism of the *Vedānta-sūtra,* as instructed to me by Your Divine Grace.

By your unlimited mercy, we are soon planning to publish a journal called *Science and Spirituality for Youth.* We are hopeful that by your unlimited mercy it will attract many intelligent young people to the movement.

We have also recently published a popular book entitled *Seven Nobel Laureates on Science and Spirituality*, which has received a tremendous response both from scholars as well as devotees. We are in the process of translating this book into Bengali. Bengal is an intellectually and spiritually fertile land, and we are hopeful that by your unlimited divine blessings our institute will be able to attract a large number of intellectuals from this region into the movement.

A wonderful team of your grandchildren is involved with the activities of our Institute at Kolkata. In addition to the dedicated and well-qualified *brahmacārīs* at BI Kolkata, we have about fifteen devotees spread out in the reputed universities and institutes all over the world. Five of them are faculty members at the Indian Institutes of Technology (IIT). By your unlimited mercy, the IITs, the premier scientific institutions in India, are becoming strongholds of BI Kolkata. At present we are successfully coordinating presentations of Bhāgavata Science at IIT Kanpur and IIT Bombay; and we are inaugurating such programs at IIT Guwahati and IIT Kharagpur. Your grandchildren who are associated with BI Kolkata are in the process of joining other IITs, either as students or as faculty members, all by your unlimited divine blessings.

The Bhaktivedanta Institute is currently organizing the Second International Conference on Life Comes from Life, to be held in Rome from November 12 to 14, 2004, as part of the 108thappearance day celebrations of Your Divine Grace. The venue for the conference, the prestigious *Sala Protomoteca*, was given free of charge by the Mayor of Rome to the Bhaktivedanta Institute for the purpose of this conference. This beautiful hall, situated right in the center of Rome, is the official meeting place for the annual Nobel Peace Summit in Italy. Having taken the guidance of Your Divine Grace’s instructions at every step in organizing the First International Conference on Life Comes from Life, held in Vṛndāvana in October 1977, we pray that the upcoming conference will be successful in spreading Your Divine Grace’s glories among the community of scholars.

I pray that by your unlimited mercy I will be able to publish the book *Origin of Life*, in addition to several other books. By your divine blessings, our institute has attracted the attention of prestigious organizations such as the Metanexus Institute in the U.S.A., which has given a grant to BI Kolkata for the popularization of science and spirituality. With the help of this grant, and by your mercy, we will be able to increase our publications and actively distribute our literatures.

Your grandson His Holiness Ātmanivedana Swami has generously arranged for a custom-made mobile Bhaktivedanta Institute and Temple of Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Śrīmatī Subhadrā on wheels, called *Garuḍa*. This mobile preaching institute is equipped with a kitchen, sleeping quarters for eight, a bathroom, a gift shop, and even a retractable stage for scientific presentations, *harināma-saṅkīrtana,* and other public programs. *Garuḍa* is helping to widely distribute our BI publications in India and is often featured in the media. We plan to intensify the distribution of our publications and presentations in colleges and universities.

From the Bhaktivedanta Institute, Kolkata, we have been regularly propagating Kṛṣṇa consciousness in prestigious institutes throughout India, such as the Indian Institutes of Technology (IIT.) For example, in March of this year there was a panel discussion on Science and Religion for World Peace at IIT Kanpur, organized by our group members, who are also students at IIT Kanpur. Among the panelists were two professors of IIT Kanpur and a Muslim brother who is the founder of the World Organization of Religion and Knowledge (WORK). Thereafter, there was a spiritual picnic at Naimiṣāraṇya, where we held a seminar on “The Relevance of Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam in the Age of Science.” One of the speakers at the seminar was Prof. Sanjay Srivastava, a physicist who has become attracted to our publications and is now collaborating with us. The *Garuḍa Motor Institute and Temple on Wheels* and our team of devotees accompanied and supported us during these events.

In partnership with the Council for a Parliament of the World’s Religions, based in Chicago, the Bhaktivedanta Institute of Kolkata was one of the core organizing members of the International Summit for Peace, held in New Delhi in December 2003. There again, the President of India, His Excellency A. P. J. Abdul Kalam, strongly spoke about the necessity of the collaboration between science and spirituality for bringing about world peace.

In recent times, organizations such as the Templeton Foundation, Metanexus Institute, and many other institutes of science and religion/spirituality around the world have also sincerely promoted the use of scientific studies to prove the existence of God. Many important scientists from prestigious institutions, including Harvard University, Oxford University, and others are also engaged in such serious research work. All that you expected to see from the scientists is beginning to manifest, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Your predictions are all coming true.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, it is by your causeless mercy and desire that there is such a revolution around the world today in the field of scientific understanding of God. We now boldly proclaim, by way of following in the line of the most wonderful poem you composed in glorification of your divine master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Gosvāmī, entitled, “Adore, Adore Ye All the Happy Day”:

“Life Comes From Life” thou hast proved  
“Scientific” Calamity thou hast moved  
Speculation around all untrace  
The only hope Your Divine Grace  
Had you not come who had told  
The message of Kṛṣṇa, scientific and bold  
Would’ve fallen into ignorance, the human race  
Miracle done, Your Divine Grace.

On this most auspicious occasion, please bless us so that we will be able to render some insignificant service at your lotus feet.

Your servants at the Bhaktivedanta Institute, Kolkata, India.  
(written by Bhaktisvarūpa Dāmodar Swami)

## Bhaktivedanta Institute (Mumbai and Berkeley)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, our divine spiritual master,

We offer our repeated humble obeisances again and again at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

You are the founder-*ācārya* of ISKCON. Your books will be the law books for the Golden Age of Lord Caitanya. All sincere, unmotivated Vaiṣṇavas will turn to your books for answers to any question or for resolution of any controversy, and by reading and following your books they will also gain your direct association on the transcendental plane. Your books are especially relevant because they are written for souls who are immersed in the Western culture, and now everyone is following Western culture. What is it about your books and your teachings in general that cause so much change in people’s lives? Many answers could be given. Of course, Your Divine Grace is empowered by Lord Caitanya and the rest of the *paramparā*. Your own behavior, character, and motivation are spotlessly pure. Indeed, anyone who comes in touch with you, even cursorily, easily sees that your behavior is always sinless. As for ourselves, we see practically that to maintain sinless behavior is not at all easy, especially in the context of degraded modern culture. You instructed us that purity is the force. Of all the senses, the tongue is the most important to control, and so the key to keeping the senses under control is to always keep the tongue engaged only in Kṛṣṇa’s service. Your Divine Grace’s tongue is always vibrating Kṛṣṇa’s name, honoring only *prasādam,* never speaking *prajalpa,* and only glorifying Kṛṣṇa’s qualities, form, and name. In all your conversations, we can see the spotless purity of your tongue. When the senses are purified, one’s perceptions will improve.

Even some of the material scientists have come to feel the need for keener senses to study even matter. Albert Einstein said,

Our present way of applying the causal principle is quite superficial. . . . \[W\]e are like a juvenile learner at the piano, just relating one note to that which immediately precedes or follows. To an extent this may be very good when one is dealing with very simple and primitive compositions, but it will not do for the interpretation of a Bach Fugue. Quantum physics has presented us with very complex processes, and to meet them we must further enlarge and refine our concept of causality.

What is needed, he went on to say, is “the development of the perceptive faculties themselves.” He expected that a race of scientists would appear in the future who would be more able “to perceive the profound and manifold operation of causation in nature.”

Indeed, for want of this keener perception of sensible reality, science in general has been stuck without any truly fundamental discovery in most fields for a while now, thus triggering recently books with such titles as *The End of Science*. In quantum theory, for example, physicists at present see the spots as giving rise to an interference pattern in the two-slit experiment, but they are unable to fully explain the formation of this pattern. However, a keener perception, one in which the *form* of the interference pattern is ontologically prior, is possible.

It is precisely this point you have superbly revealed with your infallible divine vision and understanding of the plans of Lord Caitanya in your purport to *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 3.26.34:

This verse is the potential basis of great scientific research work, for it explains how subtle forms are generated from the ethereal element, what their characteristics and actions are, and how *the tangible elements*, namely air, fire, water and earth, *are manifested from the subtle form*. . . . Mental existence transforms into tangible form as soon as there is an opportunity due to . . . development of the gross elements from subtle form.” \[italics ours\]

Of course, it is not the case that we directly understood the scientific content of your purport above and then applied it to science. Rather, by diligently working *within* science as per your transcendental direction, which is your mercy, we at the Bhaktivedanta Institute in Bombay and Berkeley have been able to develop over long years a new scientific viewpoint called “the relational viewpoint,” which is gaining increasing recognition and appreciation from the worldwide community of scientists. It puts in place within science an advanced view of matter and causality, one that stems directly and beautifully from the theistic Sāṅkhya philosophy of Lord Kapiladeva and Lord Caitanya. In the process, we are therefore also gaining a glimpse of why the knowledge of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* is not only a science but a higher science, even with regard to matter. Once this new vision of matter is in place within science, the scientists’ understanding of the relation between matter and consciousness will have to necessarily change. We pray for your help and guidance in serving you in our efforts to further instantiate this new *bhāgavata* vision of matter within contemporary scientific thought, and we offer to you this very service made possible by your mercy, as one offers Ganges water to the Ganges.

*Your eternally aspiring servants at the Bhaktivedanta Institute in Mumbai and Berkeley.*

## Centro Studi Bhaktivedanta

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,Please accept my respectful obeisances unto your lotuslike feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

On this most auspicious day celebrating your Vyāsa-pūjā, I wish to express to you my most profound gratitude from the bottom of my heart.

I remember the sweet compassion of your face, together with your peaceful and unshakable composure in your role as spiritual warrior, and I feel immense gratitude for the path you have indicated, taught, and illuminated for me.

In order to advance on this path, I have and still am taking enormous advantage of your life model, your teachings, and your deeds: books, disciples, memories.

Throughout the years, at specific moments, I have more than once tried to honestly and critically re-examine the motivation that brought me to you and the teachings I have received from you, teachings upon which I have accepted you as master for my life.

Over the decades, my circumstances have almost totally changed—everything around me has undergone a transformation—yet your original teachings have kept their value, their freshness, and to this day are still a rich source of inspiration.

Although I had a certain level of genuineness in my motivations, I appreciate that the merit belongs to you, that is, to the power and legitimacy of your teachings, as they have nourished, purified, transformed, and sublimated those same motivations of mine. I therefore wish to express my infinite gratitude and always remember your brilliant and compassionate figure as an authentic *ācārya*.

Having realized that by serving you by preaching the sacred *bhakti* gospel with happy gratitude there can never be any separation between master and disciple, I heartily wish to keep on serving you. In joyous wonder I have realized that, once truly activated and purely maintained, the relationship between guru and disciple knows no distance in space or time. As a flame in a windless place burns steadily, so by God’s grace the light of this divine relationship can forever maintain its radiance.

I pray to you, my lord and master, that in your majesty you may bestow upon me, your servant, vigor and wisdom. Please qualify me to meet all trials, to manage all hardships life begets, and to wake up at last in pure love for God.

You have provided the essential seed, and I, by studying and practicing, have felt your teachings blossom and ripen. I feel this is the season when I’d like to deepen and secure my spiritual realizations: I would like you to unveil for me the secrets of divine love; I’d like to glow with virtue, joy, and compassion and offer them to all creatures with gratitude.

These are among the fundamental lessons I have gathered from you:

• to rapidly recognize the essential difference between *sat* and *asat*, between reality and illusion, between the steadiness of the soul and the impermanence of matter;

• to see everything as happening under Kṛṣṇa’s supreme control, and to see myself as His loving servant, at your service.

This vision and consciousness have happily allowed me to take part in the cosmic play of the Creator: to serve His project within yours, to enter consciously into the field of transformation and live the experience with joy, and by turning a negative perception into an improved opportunity to evolve and to obtain the supreme eternal *bhakti*, to operate in chaos while serving order.

I would like to keep myself as your authentic student: to transmit your authentic teachings, ease the sufferings of the world, and make the sacred lineage of the great saints and masters—the Vaiṣṇava *ācāryas*— glow in the West.

Offering you our heart and mind, words and service,  
*Your servants at Centro Studi Bhaktivedanta, Italy.*  
(written by Matsyāvatār dāsa)

## Cleveland Nāma Haṭṭa

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Kindly accept our respectful obeisances. All glories to you forever!
