You have truly built an unusual transcendental building in which the whole world can live peacefully! And by your desire, Lord Caitanya’s mission of love of God is moving forward in every town and village. The Cleveland Nāma-haṭṭa program exists only due to the causeless mercy of your good self. We don’t know how to properly appreciate you except that we want to love you! Please accept this song (to the tune of “Exodus”) as our insufficient offering on this, your Vyāsa-pūjā day, 2004. And please bless us to represent you in all we say and do. The lyrics were penned by one of your spiritual children, Kṛṣṇa-nandinīMātājī, and these words come from all of our hearts.

O dear Prabhupāda, we pledge our hearts to thee,  
For you have given us the key  
To life eternal, peace, joy, and freedom,  
And because of you pure devotees we will be.  
You walked this earth in absolute reality,  
A true personification of love and humility.  
You asked nothing for yourself  
But gave life to all else.  
O renowned one, resplendent one, renounced one.  
Please tell us, Śrī Guru, what can we do for you?  
What can one give for the gift of life?  
Just let us always be surrendered at your lotus feet,  
For surely then Śrī Kṛṣṇa’s favor we’ll meet.  
So take our hearts and mold them to Kṛṣṇa’s will,  
And on them let there be emblazoned the seal  
Of Kṛṣṇa consciousness—that’s love of God.

All glories to Your Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda!

*The Cleveland Nāma-haṭṭa Family.*

## Festival of India

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Thank you, thank you, thank you for the opportunity to serve so many temples and wonderful devotees all around North America. The opportunity for service you have given us is a perfect example of your unlimited mercy. Śrīla Prabhupāda, you know some of us are not up to the standard of temple devotees, lacking the determination to serve in more austere spiritual environs, which require regulation and serious surrendered endeavor on a daily basis. Although we are covered by ignorance, we still desire to serve your mission and fulfill your desires for creating a worldwide spiritual family. So you have given us a very flexible program to enlist us.

*Roustabout* is term for a carnival worker of little skill and rough character. This title befits many of us, myself certainly. But still you shower such crude souls with your mercy. When I consider my many petty displays of flawed character during the tour last year, conniption fits that occurred under the pressure of driven moments, highlighted by nondevotional language and criticisms of others, I see you, Prabhupāda, ever patient, ever the well-wisher, simply waiting for me to surrender to the blessed circumstances Kṛṣṇa has arranged for my personal purification. You are there steadily, ecstatically, perfectly situated in Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

You noted my behavior, or lack of it, when I retreated into laziness. You applauded the little extra efforts I made to shyly preach. You rewarded my desire to write a Kṛṣṇa conscious story. You gave me the excellent association of your dedicated servants Madhuhā Prabhu, Parigraha Prabhu, Phaṇi-bhūṣana Prabhu, Manu Prabhu, and Bhāgavatī-devī dāsī, who helped from behind the front lines with tubs of granola and constant encouragement. And you provided us with hundreds of devotees to care for us along the 15,000-mile pilgrimage-tour.

You gave us the chance to serve in many ways in various temples. You provided this simply because we came forward, took one step toward you and your service—a small step, perhaps even only a stumble or crawl—out of our determined ignorance. You saw this weak effort and rewarded it with the great experience of being able to assist your perfect plan for a few short exciting months. Thank you, again and again.

You have given the youth of today’s Hare Kṛṣṇa movement the opportunity to experience your presence, your message, and your mercy. They have tasted all this through the Festival of India, many for the first time in their budding devotional lives. Your wisdom in stressing the importance of these festivals is unsurpassed. Books fly out, people want to know why we are so joyous and celebrating. Your mercy comes in a downpour so torrential that everyone is drenched, not just to the skin, but to the soul.

I can’t estimate the value of the Festival of India’s service to Kṛṣṇa. But to me the opportunity to do this service was priceless. My realization of this also is simply due to your causeless mercy. You said if someone comes to the temple and turns just a screw for Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa never forgets that service. So what to speak of tent pitchers and stake drivers and display toters and tent folders and truck loaders and -unloaders—both those with the regular crew and those who join in for only one day. Everyone’s service is always remembered by Kṛṣṇa, simply because you have made the introduction and the opportunity available.

What is the result of all your mercy? A lady approached me in San Francisco. She said she wanted to thank someone connected with the festival for putting it on. Seven years ago, she said, she had attended the festival and seen the displays on vegetarianism and become a vegetarian as a result. She had somehow not connected with a festival since then, but she wanted to thank us for changing her life. How many stories like this are there? How many souls has your perfect message touched, “infecting” them with the beginnings of Kṛṣṇa consciousness and later transforming their mundane lives into spiritual celebrations? How can I praise someone who always continues to astound me? Your mercy is unrelenting, Śrīla Prabhupāda. We are so much blessed to be able to help you distribute it. Śrīla Prabhupāda, please keep our awareness of your mercy in the forefront of our minds, always. It is said that one who has a bona fide spiritual master never has a reason to lament.

The Festival of India Crew and helpers from around the world.  
(written by Jñānagamya dāsa)

## Global Varṇāśrama Educational Social and Cultural Organization

My dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my obeisances. All glories to your divine lotus feet!

Our attempts at manifesting your dream of *varṇāśrama* communities have been frail. Just yesterday I was reading your comments regarding establishing what you referred to as the “Mānava Dharma Mission,” another expression for implementing the mission of *varṇāśrama-dharma*. During a conversation with the governor of Uttar Pradesh in 1976, you mentioned how this was our next most important project, to introduce what you also called “village organization.”

Therefore, *mānava-dharma means to impregnate a human being with spiritual knowledge*. A human being must be educated spiritually. . . . Point Ten: The *mānava-dharma* mission, its constitution and program. Therefore *the constitution of mānava-dharma, or the institution of varṇāśrama, must be interesting for the whole world,* and it should be exemplified by practical demonstration. *The immediate program should be village organization,* as Mahatma Gandhi contemplated. In India the majority of the population is in the villages. The difficulty is that there is no sufficient supply of water to produce food grains. Mother nature, or mother Durgā, punishes the godless demons by restricting the supply of food grains. The godless demons are very enthusiastic to produce motorcars, skyscrapers, brothels, and cinemas, and many unnecessary demands of the body, but they are not interested in producing food grains. *This is the defect of the modern society. If food grains are produced in an organized way, human society can produce ten times what they are presently producing*. \[Italics added. Conversation with the governor of Uttar Pradesh, Vṛndāvana, April 1975\]

We are seeing how the very framework of society, beginning with the family nucleus and the village, are both in critical condition. In the *Bhagavad-gītā* you remind us that when the family traditions are no longer followed and, in particular, when women become unprotected, this is the downfall of civilization. Our social situation all over the world is in such deplorable condition because we have strayed from the basic principles of dharma. You, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have emphasized establishing *varṇāśrama* communities as a basis and means to re-establish dharma. You vision on this phase of our movement was very clear. On a number of occasions, you discouraged even staying in the cities:

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:** My, *this farming program, theoretically there is no comparison*. But practically people are accustomed in different way. To bring them to the program it will take some time. Otherwise my program is *assured happiness,* happiness assurance, if they get. . . . Have your own food grown. Keep cows. Have your own crops. Chant Hare Kṛṣṇa.

**Tamāl Kṛṣṇa:**That goes for all over the world.

**Śrīla Prabhupāda:***Don’t go to the city*. That is my determination. The hellish city. In city nobody has got the opportunity for living in such comfortable place. It is all Kṛṣṇa’s mercy that we have got. Otherwise if you go to the Bombay city, even here, these pigeon holes, three small rooms . . . \[Italics added. Morning walk, Bombay, April 1977\]

Yet we have remained so very slow in putting this into effect. I understand that unless a disciple learns to please his spiritual master, his success in spiritual life becomes endangered. I take it personally that I will not be successful in my spiritual life unless I learn to please you in a more substantial way regarding implementing *varṇāśrama* village communities. I earnestly pray that I can assist you much more in helping you realize this most important aspect of your mission. I am totally dependent on your blessings.

Your servants at Global Varṇāśrama Educational, Social, and Community Organization.  
(written by Bhakti Rāghava Swami)

## Hare Krishna School

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

As we continue to struggle with implementing your desire, I pray for the strength of conviction that I may be able to remain engaged in service to your transcendental mission. This past year we achieved a major milestone in developing one of your projects here in New Zealand. I was most fortunate to take part in an event of great historical importance, the opening of a wonderful new temple for the pleasure of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Giridhārī, Śrī Śrī Gaura-Nitāi, and Śrī Śrī Jagannātha, Baladeva, and Śrīmatī Subhadra—the Deities you installed in Auckland in 1972.

They had resided at the current location for the past thirty years, but in an old dilapidated farmhouse. Now They stand in full glory on a white marble altar, enthroned on a beautifully carved teak *siṁhāsana* crafted in Jaipur. The temple room is grand, with gorgeous oil paintings adorning the ceiling (created by Dina Bandhu Prabhu), a large symmetrical lotus as the centerpiece of the main temple room floor, a beautifully carved teakwood *siṁhāsana* for your *vyāsāsana,* which faces the altar, a large fully equipped kitchen downstairs for our regular mass *prasādam* distribution, a beautiful Deity kitchen upstairs for Their Lordships offerings, and a spotlessly clean and shining new paraphernalia room custom designed for Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Giridhārīs *pūjārīs*.

The spacious temple room has many windows, which provide the Deities with a breathtaking view of lush green pastures and, in the foreground, Tulasī-devīs beautiful house within a tastefully constructed garden of aromatic flowers and shrubs. A broad majestic staircase adjoining the main parking lot ushers guests into this truly magnificent monument, which is a testament to the love and devotion your followers have for you.

Year in and year out we meet challenges to your ultimate authority, to the authority of the *paramparā,* and to our own commitment to your instructions. And year in and year out we witness your supremacy, your brilliance, and your penetrating vision. The process you have given is perfect and complete, and any perception of incompleteness is due to our own lack of vision, commitment, and realization.

The New Zealand chapter of your movement is flourishing in many ways. Nāma-haṭṭa, *prasādam* distribution, Sunday feasts, *harināma,* and book distribution are still regularly going on, as are daily *Bhāgavatam* classes, wonderful dressing of our beautiful Deities, *guru-pūjā kīrtanas* and evening *āratis, rāja-bhoga* offerings and traveling preachers inspiring us. Communities of devotees here are following the regulative principles and chanting sixteen rounds a day.

By your mercy The Hare Kṛṣṇa School is also flourishing. We are now reaching our limit of fifty students and are working on ways to expand. We are running two school buses transporting children between the ages of four and twelve from one side of the city to the other. Some children spend almost one and a half hours traveling to and from school. They arrive lively and enthusiastic and emphatically announce that they would not go to any other school; the distance is no obstacle.

When they arrive at school they are greeted by a life-size portrait of you, standing in a serene posture holding a *daṇḍa* in one hand and a bead bag in the other. They start their day with obeisances, *kīrtana, Gītā śloka* recitation, a story from *śāstra,* and a character trait such as humility, courage, or truthfulness to contemplate for the day. They study mathematics, science, English, Sanskrit, health, and physical education, all within the context of the Vedic culture you so expertly transplanted around the world for the benefit of mankind.

This education is truly transformational, empowering, and ultimately liberating. Parents daily testify to the amazing changes in their children. They marvel at how responsible, respectful, and dutiful they have become since joining The Hare Kṛṣṇa School. They compare how it was before their children joined the school: the daily struggle they previously experienced motivating their children to get up in the morning, to complete their homework, or to keep their room clean. The parents boldly declare to their friends and family members the glories of the school and the devotees.

Annually we submit our mission statement to the Ministry of Education. It reads: “Inspired by the teachings of Śrīla Prabhupāda, in partnership with the community, we provide excellent quality spiritual and material education.” In the supporting documents we are required to define our goals for education, describe our vision and values, and explain our educational philosophy. Our motto is “Education for Eternity,” taken from your *Īśopaniṣad* purports.

We proudly present your teachings as the philosophical basis of our educational practices. Your teachings are succinct, unambiguous, direct, and challenging. The method for achieving success is applicable for all cultures, all age groups, and all times. The process of chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa is eternal, and education that is directed toward and inspired by this process is all-attractive. We base our character education program on the four pillars of religion and in this way establish the baseline for real human development.

You have given us everything to achieve perfection, Śrīla Prabhupāda. The more closely aligned we are with your processes and teachings, the more satisfaction, success, and sanity we experience. The technique you are teaching for reviving our dormant loving consciousness is flawless, but we are so complicated and sophisticated that we feel obliged to adjust, interpret, and extend the processes to suit our limited perception of reality.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, while we revel in the realms of relative truth, you patiently prescribe processes for realization of the Absolute Truth, the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. I beg to remain in the association of those devotees who are determined to follow in your footsteps and thus achieve success on the path of perfection. Please assist me in my ever-flawed attempts to live up to the goals and expectations you masterfully prescribe. I remain the humble and grateful servant of those servants who desire to follow your instructions and to live a life dedicated to the pleasure of the Supreme Absolute Truth, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa.

*Your servants at The Hare Kṛṣṇa School, in New Zealand.*  
(written by Prāṇa dāsa)

## House of Vaiṣṇava Culture for Śrīla Prabhupāda

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda, our beloved *jagad-guru,* and all glories to the transcendental anniversary of his appearance in this world!

Taking shelter in the shade of the beloved lotus feet of His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda, inspired and guided by him, we, devotees of his marvelous ISKCON, live fully dedicated to preach his sublime message in his House of Vaiṣṇava Culture.

Our lives reflect his teachings, and that is why we wish everyone to become aware of them and share with us this simple life with high thoughts. It is Śrīla Prabhupāda’s wish that the people of this Age of Kali understand the message of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* and this is also the wish of the whole *paramparā,* as well as the original wish of the Supreme Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Thus, we live exclusively for this:

*sa vai puṁsāṁ paro dharmo yato bhaktir adhokṣaje*  
*ahaituky apratihatā yayātmā suprasīdati*

**“The supreme occupation \[dharma\] for all humanity is that by which men can attain to loving devotional service unto the transcendent Lord. Such devotional service must be unmotivated and uninterrupted to completely satisfy the self.” (*****Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*** **1.2.6)**

Undoubtedly, this supreme occupation—Kṛṣṇa consciousness—is the great legacy of Śrīla Prabhupāda for all of us, and as it deals with the Absolute Truth, this occupation has inspired us, and it will inspire all who somehow listen to the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*. That is why we constantly work for the joy of Śrīla Prabhupāda, for the good of all, and of course for our own sake. Slowly all of humankind will recognize its eternal debt to Śrīla Prabhupāda, the great saint of the twentieth century. As the *ācārya* and in this age the most important propagator of the Holy Name of Kṛṣṇa, the *mahā-mantra*, Śrīla Prabhupāda has established the *saṅkīrtana* movement all around the world, as Lord Caitanya predicted:

*pṛthivīte āche yata nagarādi grāma*  
*sarvatra pracāra haibe mora nāma*  
*\[CB Antya-khaṇḍa 4.126\]*

**“In every town and village on this earth the glories of My name will be chanted.”**

Following Śrīla Prabhupāda’s footsteps and his order, we from the House of Vaiṣṇava Culture, which has been established in his name, daily fulfill our prescribed duty to preach the glories of Kṛṣṇa by performing the *saṅkīrtana-yajña,* the only remedy for the disease of the fallen souls of this age of darkness and ignorance.

We pray to Śrīla Prabhupāda that he always engage us in his mission and enlighten us with the truths of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. We wish today and forever that all blessings be poured upon his marvelous ISKCON and that the devotees fill the auspicious day of his Vyāsa-pūjā with introspection and meditation on the importance of pushing on his sublime mission.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

All glories to his faithful followers and representatives.

Śrīla Prabhupāda-*kī jaya*!

*Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servants at the House of Vaiṣṇava Culture for Śrīla Prabhupāda, in Sao Paulo, Brazil.*  
(written by Jaya Gokula dāsa)

## International Society for Cow Protection

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

The barn is on the hillside.  
The doors are open and there is a warm breeze flowing through.  
The cows sit on the hay-covered floor and look out down the hillside.  
If you listen carefully, you can hear them chewing their cud.  
Contented now that their stomachs are full, they rest.  
We, their guests, look at them with concern.  
Will we have the ability to continue to care for them?  
They eat a lot, are very large, don’t speak our language.  
How do we communicate with them when they are dying?  
How do we provide for their large appetites?  
Self-possession, confidence, renunciation, loyalty are their qualities.  
Peacefulness, patience (they’re very patient), and gratefulness are more qualities.  
But they are completely dependent on us who care for them.  
If they fall we must get their large bodies up.  
If they are in pain we must soothe them.  
Being human, we have the ability to reason.  
Being fortunate, we have met you, Śrīla Prabhupāda.  
The cows are in animal bodies that cannot reason.  
They cannot read your books to gain solace.  
They cannot chant the Holy Names to bring Kṛṣṇa to them.  
Yet they are Lord Kṛṣṇa’s favorite animals.  
And by protecting them we progress in spiritual life.  
“What makes the cow so special? Why protect them?” People ask.  
“There are so many cows and they are completely dependent.  
Protecting them is a continuous lifetime task.”  
There are practical reasons and there are spiritual reasons.  
But few can understand.  
The cow’s milk maintains our brains for higher aims of life.  
The cow’s milk makes us healthy and strong and able to live a long life.  
The cow’s milk is religious principles in liquid form.  
The bull is the emblem of the moral principle.  
He helps in the production of grains in the fields.  
Their dung and urine are pure and can cure us of many diseases.  
Their four legs represent the four pillars of religious life: truthfulness,  
mercy (compassion), cleanliness, and austerity.  
Products from milk, cow dung, and urine are essential in spiritual ceremonies.  
Protecting the cows means fulfilling the aim of life.  
Slaughtering the cows means to end human civilization.  
As disciples of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we have been taught these truths.  
Protecting the cows is acting upon these truths.  
Becoming closer to you and Lord Kṛṣṇa is the benefit.  
Please, Śrīla Prabhupāda, please allow us the ability to protect them.  
Please allow us their gentle and calming association.  
The barn is on the hillside.  
The doors are open and there is a warm breeze flowing through.  
They sit on the hay-covered floor and look out down the hillside.  
If you listen carefully, you can hear them chewing their cud.  
Contented now that their stomachs are full, they rest.

*Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servants at the International Society for Cow Protection and the ISKCON Ministry for Cow Protection and Agriculture.*  
(written by Chayā-devī dāsī)

## ISKCON Prison Ministry

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

Thank you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for giving us this most wonderful preaching program we call ISKCON Prison Ministry. I have grown to know that the prison program was started long ago by you and the other *ācāryas*. Even Lord Kṛṣṇa appeared in prison!

I understand more and more as I write the prison inmates that we are all inmates in the prison house we call the material world. All your analogies about the material world being a prison are very much alive in our service. For instance, in *Easy Journey to Other Planets* you write,

A man who is put into a certain cell cannot change at will without superior authority. Kṛṣṇa says in the *Bhagavad-gītā* that one should not try to change from one cell to another. That will not make anyone happy. If a prisoner thinks, “I am in this cell—let me request the warden to change my cell, and I will be happy,” that is a mistaken idea. One cannot be happy so long as he is within the prison walls.

And in *Elevation to Kṛṣṇa Consciousness* you write,

No government wants its citizens to act in such a way that they must go to prison and suffer tribulations. The prison house is undoubtedly constructed by the state government, but this does not mean that the government is anxious for its citizens to be put into it. Indirectly, the disobedient citizens force the government to construct the prison house. It is not done for the pleasure of the government, which has to spend a great deal of money in constructing and maintaining it. On the contrary, the government would be very glad to demolish prisons altogether provided that there are no disobedient citizens in the state. In the same way, this material world is created by the Supreme Lord, but the Supreme Lord does not will that living entities be put in it. The living entities themselves make that decision. The residents of this material world are therefore different from those who are eternally engaged in the transcendental pastimes of the Supreme Lord.

The following quotes from inmates show us how you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, have delivered them from the material world.

A few weeks ago I came across a *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*, first canto, part two, Creation. The book is from ISKCON. I’ve been reading it for about 3 weeks. I am truly amazed and humbled at the same time, by it and the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is*. I did not even know that this level of knowledge and wisdom was even in this world—Tony B., Lucasville, OH

The Pastor Mason in this jail was kind enough to lend me his personal copy of *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is* for reading in jail. It is a great solace to me through this very stressful time. I am going through, without it I would go crazy—no kidding.—Bhaktin Snehal D., Battle Creek, MI

I received the “How to Practice Kṛṣṇa Consciousness in Prison” you sent. I have prepared a small altar in my cell, according to directions. I’m trying the best I can with what I have.—Evan L., Watonga, OK

All my studies, prayers, meditations, and chanting have got me fired up like a steam engine. My consciousness has risen to new heights. Awesome! Whatever I read, contemplate on, pray about, sends insights, revelations, spiritual feelings beyond anything I can put on paper, *right now*!—Dan S., Susanville, CA

My entire life has prepared me for this—all the things I’ve been through and the things I’ve learned have brought me to this point in life, and I am so grateful to Krishna for this opportunity to become a devotee. I can’t imagine life any other way—Robert S., Navasoto, TX

Everyone is on death row; no one gets out alive. We are all in the prisons of our bodies, our minds, and the material world, in which Māyā keeps us engaged. But thanks to you, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you have freely given us Kṛṣṇa and the *mahā-mantra,* the means to escape this prison house. So as we endeavor to follow your instructions and preach to the other inmates of this prison house, we are becoming happy and looking forward to the day when we can see you in the spiritual sky.

*Your servants in the ISKCON Prison Ministry.*  
(written by Śyāma-priyā-devī dāsī)

## ISKCON Public Relations &amp; Communications Office (Mauritius)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our most humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

On the most auspicious day of your Vyāsa-pūjā, we would like to describe your glories. Just as Lord Saṅkarṣaṇa has been unsuccessful in describing the glories of Lord Kṛṣṇa though reciting them since time immemorial from His thousands of mouths, thousands of devotees from all over the whole planet have been trying to describe your glories over the decades, and we are still discovering new nectar day after day.

At the ISKCON Public Relations and Communications Office of Mauritius, we are continually pondering the prediction you made that Mauritius will become the first Kṛṣṇa conscious country in the world. Just as no one could see how Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s prediction that the holy names of Kṛṣṇa would be sung in all towns and villages would be fulfilled, so no one knows how your prediction about Mauritius will manifest. However, since you fulfilled the prediction of Lord Caitanya Mahāprabhu, we know you will also be leading the preaching to make Mauritius the first Kṛṣṇa conscious country. Even though our preaching effort in Mauritius is slack, we can see people becoming more favorable to ISKCON. We are aware that you have started to enlighten many Mauritians from within their hearts.

We are here only to help you carry out your mission, so you can use this office and the devotees serving in the office as you like. On our own, we are useless. It is only by your causeless mercy that we can do some service for Your Divine Grace.

Your servants in the ISKCON Public Relations & Communications Office, Mauritius.  
(written by Madana Gopāla dāsa)

## ISKCON Resolve

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my humble obeisances.

Being an ombudsman in ISKCON, I am meant to abide by the three principles guiding that service: impartiality, confidentiality, and independence. These are the cornerstones of an ombudsman’s work, and they are essential for helping those who seek assistance through ISKCON Resolve. In relation to your devotees and the ISKCON structure, I am trying to follow those guidelines. I’m afraid, however, that I can’t promise to be able to practice those three in relation to you:

IMPARTIALITY—How can I not be partial to you? It was you who boarded the *Jaladuta*. You who suffered two heart attacks en route to America. You who shared the use of the Agarwal’s refrigerator. You who lived on The Bowery and who performed innumerable other sacrifices just to give a person like me a chance to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa. It’s not going to be possible for me to treat you equally with others. No way. Even amongst pure devotees, you performed the greatest sacrifice, and gave me this greatest opportunity. You are my *ācārya,* and I pray to always be partial to your service.

CONFIDENTIALITY—Whatever individuals tell the ombudsman is not to be broadcast outside that discussion unless that person grants permission. Though I’m a loudmouth by nature, I’m learning to seal my lips and keep quiet in this service.

I can’t, however, keep everything a secret. So, if anything is to leak out, let it be that I want to serve you eternally. I won’t be able to kept that confidential. Rather, I pray to never stop myself from quoting you, glorifying you, wanting to help others recognize what you have done for them.

INDEPENDENCE—The office of the ombudsman is an alternative way for devotees to address their concerns without having to resort to dealing with the standard ISKCON authority structure, which tends to be more formal and judicial. Disputes and festering anxieties are addressed through dialogue and good communications.

Though the above independence is useful in many organizational situations, I don’t want to be independent of your mercy. I’ve misused my independence for so many lifetimes and have suffered so much wandering around this world of names. I’d rather now declare my dependence on you—your teachings, your mercy, your kindness. Please grant me the intelligence to surrender my minute independence to your service.

As it stands now, I’m neither a good ombudsman nor a good *śiṣya*. By your *śakti,* I hope I can become both, and thus get a chance to please you.

*Your servants at ISKCON Resolve.*  
(written by Braja Bihārī dāsa)

## ISKCON Television (ITV)

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

ITV offered the Complete Śrīla Prabhupāda DVD Library to you this year. Madhudvīṣa Prabhu worked tirelessly in authoring the DVD’s. All the persons who had the foresight to film and tape you are owed a great debt of gratitude. We sold out of the first replication of 1,000 sets and had to have another 500 made. Now we are laying in numerous languages with subtitles to make the library even more accessible. Your mercy has allowed us to be successful in this endeavor. We have no power of our own. Only by the benevolent blessing of a pure devotee is such an achievement possible. Thank you for your kindness in engaging us in serving you in this way. You have engaged our senses in the service of the *guru-paramparā* instead of in the slavery of Māyā. Each time a person tells us how much they appreciate having the complete video teachings of Your Divine Grace, our hearts are pacified. This world is full of quarrel. Conflicting desires are intersecting at a dizzying pace. The conditioned souls can find solace only in your words and instructions. When we are actually engaged in *bhakti-yoga,* there is no anxiety, only Vaikuṇṭha. Kṛṣṇa consciousness has become a worldwide phenomenon by your intense desire to deliver us from this planet of death. How can we ever repay you?

We take this opportunity of glorifying your appearance to reflect upon our service and uplift it to new levels of energy in your honor. Let us again recommit to extolling your glories, to fulfilling your mission, to living your truth—the Absolute Truth. Now, this moment, we again renew our vows to increase our production of Kṛṣṇa conscious programs and movies. We re-invigorate, in your honor, the process of producing shows that will inform, enliven, and inspire every captured ear about the glories of the Supreme Personality of Godhead and all of His plenary expansions and parts and parcels. In this age of electronic media, we promise to expand our catalog of titles. Just as you encouraged your book distributors to “double it,” so, too, we can increase the number of programs available from ITV unlimitedly.

We discovered some new footage that didn’t make it into the DVD library; it will be in the next one. In a class in Australia you said:

Unless one is spiritually realized, he cannot see equally everyone. Then *samaḥ sarveṣu bhūteṣu mad-bhaktiṁ labhate parām*. Then one can become real devotee of the Lord, after surpassing the *brahma-bhūtaḥ*stage. So this *bhakti* line is not so easy. But by Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mercy we have installed the Deity here in your country. You are very fortunate that Caitanya Mahāprabhu has come to your country to teach you how you become free of all anxieties. This is Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s mission. Everyone is full of anxiety, but everyone can be freed from all the anxieties if he follows the path chalked out by Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. And what is Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s instruction? Very simple.

*harer nāma harer nāma harer nāma eva kevalam*  
*kalau nāsty eva nāsty eva nāsty eva gatir anyathā*

This is not Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s personal version. This is in the scripture, Vedic scripture. *Bṛhan-Nāradīya-Purāṇa*. This instruction is there. As people are fallen in this age the method also has been offered very simple. They cannot follow any strong or severe austerities. It is not possible. They have been recommended simply to chant the holy name of God. That’s all. Anyone can do. It is not difficult at all. . . . If you have got your own God, then you chant His name. Caitanya Mahāprabhu doesn’t say that you chant simply Kṛṣṇa’s name. If you have got any relationship with God and if you know His name and address, (*laughter*) then you chant His name. Unfortunately, you do not know who is God; neither you know His address, neither His activities. So take this Kṛṣṇa. Here is a solid name. And we give you His address, His father’s name, His mother’s name, everything. So if you have got your own God’s name, Caitanya Mahāprabhu said you can chant.

Yes, Śrīla Prabhupāda, we can chant. That is your mercy and our qualification. Please continue to shower your mercy on us so we can chant, chant, chant even when the mind says can’t, can’t, can’t.

All glories to Your Divine Grace A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda! You are truly the *siṁha-guru,* the embodiment of Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s desires, the kindest soul we know.

Your servants at ITV.  
(written by Nṛsiṁhānanda dāsa)

## Journal of Vaishnava Studies

Please accept my most humble obeisances. All glories to your lotus feet, and all glories to your holy name on this most auspicious day!

Generally, when we think of the holy name, we immediately think of *Kṛṣṇa’s* holy name, and this is because you have trained us to be Kṛṣṇa conscious, not necessarily Prabhupāda conscious. And yet, on this day, we cannot but think of *your* holy name, and, in fact, if we think about it carefully, it can bring us to the deepest levels of Kṛṣṇa consciousness.

Śrīla A. C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda.

Let us begin with “Śrīla.” Generally, we refer to you as “His Divine Grace.” This is a sort of crude Western approximation of “Śrīla.” However, “Śrīla” is much sweeter. While it serves the function of an honorific title, much like “His Divine Grace,” it actually goes much deeper. You have explained on several occasions why great devotees like Śrīla Vyāsadeva and Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura, your beloved Gurudeva, have been given the title “Śrīla,” whereas the Supreme Lord Himself is called “Śrī” Kṛṣṇa (as opposed to Śrīla Kṛṣṇa). The word “Śrīla,” as you have explained it, is reserved for great devotees only, for it is made up of two words, *śrī*(“beautiful”) and *līlā* (“the Lord’s pastimes”). Put simply, one is called “Śrīla” when one becomes beautified by knowledge of the Lord’s pastimes, or by entering into these pastimes. I cannot think of anyone who deserves this title more than Your Divine Grace.

A. C. stands for Abhay Charanaravinda. Again this speaks of your beauty—it refers to one who is beautiful like a lotus due to his fearlessness, or one who is fearless due to having taken shelter at Kṛṣṇa’s lotus feet. It was this fearlessness that enabled you to board a freighter at seventy years of age; to avoid turning back when you had heart failure; to not give up when you saw the barbarians you had to work with; to stay enlivened when you received little support from your Godbrothers; to travel around the world initiating and training disciples for Mahāprabhu’s army. You are indeed the most fearless person I have ever had the good fortune to meet.

Bhaktivedānta. What could describe you more accurately? The perfect combination of devotion and knowledge. *Bhakti* personified, according to the *Bhāgavata-māhātmya,* was to travel south and then return to Vṛndāvana. After some time in Vṛndāvana, Bhakti was to go to other countries. Who has fulfilled this if not Your Divine Grace? And you brought *bhakti* with knowledge. You brought your *Bhāgavatams* for everyone to read, so they could understand the meaning of devotion. You continued to translate until your last breath, urging your followers to distribute this knowledge to everyone they met. “The world is suffering due to want of this knowledge,” you always said. And so you had the intense desire to give “*bhaktivedānta,*” the knowledge of pure devotion, to others, to alleviate their misery. By your unparalleled compassion, you have proven that you are the very embodiment of Bhaktivedānta.

And because of this, you are Prabhupāda. No doubt you are more comfortable calling your own Gurudeva by the lofty title “Śrīla Prabhupāda,” and Rūpa Gosvāmī, too, was often known by this name. But to us, your disciples, no one is more “Prabhupāda” than you! All other masters are subservient to you because you showed the greatest love, compassion, surrender, fearlessness, devotion, and knowledge—for all of the above reasons, in all the above ways. And in many other ways, too numerous to mention.

So on this holy day, I remember your holy name. I only pray that with each year I can understand a little more deeply the true import of your name, and what it means for myself and for the rest of suffering humanity. When you first arrived on Western shores, you asked Kṛṣṇa to make you dance, if He so pleased, and in so doing you might serve as His transcendental puppet. By this, you wrote, you might actually fulfill the purport of the name “Bhaktivedanta,” given to you by your respected Godbrothers. Śrīla Prabhupāda, if I may be so bold, it is clear to all of your direct disciples, and to the thousands upon thousands who have become your followers since your departure, that you have long since fulfilled the purport of that name. We sit at your feet and marvel as we realize that no name can do you justice, and so, on your advice, we simply chant the names you have taught us to chant: Hare Kṛṣṇa, Hare Kṛṣṇa, Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa, Hare Hare/ Hare Rama, Hare Rama, Rama Rama, Hare Hare. We chant these names, and pray for your mercy.

*Your servants at the Journal of Vaishnava Studies.*  
(written by Satyarāja dāsa)

## Krishna.com

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

The Krishna.com staff wants to thank you for allowing us to render service to you and Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Preaching through Krishna.com is a new experience: We do not need to find people; people find us.

Now spiritual seekers only have to type certain words on their computers to get immediate access to a wealth of information on Kṛṣṇa and devotional service. Your books and the unique spiritual knowledge contained in them are going out worldwide. And Krishna.com offers the personal touch, because people can ask questions of a live volunteer.

Since we started this project, not only have your books been distributed, but many new people have invited Kṛṣṇa into their hearts and homes, and some having taken the serious step of accepting spiritual initiation. Many others have started to reawaken their relationship with Kṛṣṇa by beginning the practice of chanting the *mahā-mantra*.

Another positive item in this revolutionary preaching is that people everywhere can hear live classes from Los Angeles and Alachua, Florida. Krishna.com has the potential for unlimited growth, but we know that we cannot be successful without your mercy. We pray that you are pleased with our efforts, and we beg that you empower us to beat the digital *bṛhad-mṛdaṅga* more and more.

Your humble servants on the staff of Krishna.com.

## Padma Academy

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our obeisances. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

What if every child could receive an education that awakened his or her dormant love for Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, a love that also causes the natural good qualities inherent in each soul to blossom? What if that education also lit the spark of innate curiosity, opening up every human mind to the wonder of knowledge, truth, and exploration? Beyond that, what if all children had teachers who knew them well? What if the teachers cared enough to help their students discover their interests and strengths so that they could find how they would like to serve Kṛṣṇa in their life and livelihood? And what if all teachers had the resources to do these things effectively?

One can scoff at these concepts, thinking them never-to-be-achieved utopias. Yet on a small scale these goals have been achieved at Padma Academy by the mercy of your instructions. Following those instructions is immensely satisfying and powerful. We have the conviction that it is powerful enough to make changes on a larger, global scale simply because the *śāstra* and history record great and unbelievable shifts by people who act as agents of Kṛṣṇa and His servants.

Our hope is that the service we do will spread beyond the boundaries of our own school and even beyond ISKCON, because there is a great need for transformation in education. As the world becomes a global village culturally and economically, education will have to change its very form, just as it did with the start of the industrial revolution. People everywhere are therefore searching for a way to educate their children that will meet present needs. You taught us to combine the best of the spiritual knowledge of Vedic culture with the best of present facilities. Following in this spirit, what we have pioneered at Padma Academy is a synergy of ancient and post-modern structure and pedagogy. This model has proven itself to have the flexibility needed in this age of globalization. The ancient traditions were based on the concept of complete equal worth and opportunity for all people. The success of ungraded classes and diversified instruction, two aspects of that tradition, show that true equity exists when each student gets what he or she needs. From the modern world come technology, the ability to have many schools within a school, and the full scope of vocations that didn’t exist in the ancient world.

And, today’s children sorely lack training in civilized behavior, what to speak of an awareness of the self or Kṛṣṇa. The need is therefore great from any perspective. We beg for your mercy and empowerment that we may act as your servants to meet this need, to serve the children of your ISKCON society and to show the world at large a way to a sane society.

Praying for your mercy, we remain,  
Your servants at Padma Academy, North Carolina.

## Prabhupāda Village

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at the dust of your lotus feet.

Another Vyāsa-pūjā is here, which allows us to contemplate our great fortune of having come in contact with such a superlative pure devotee as you.

We here at Prabhupāda Village have come together to develop a community with a desire to serve your mission and to extend to others the mercy we have received. We are struggling to build unity in the diversity that each of us brings to the community. The Temple of the Holy Name, which stands unfinished, symbolizes our uncompleted though essential task—both internally and externally.

To give mercy to others we have to begin by extending mercy to one another despite our differences. This is how we can show our love for you—by embracing one another, encouraging one another, and serving one another with devotion and humility.

We pray that you will guide us in our internal work to bring about the devotional qualities and purity that will allow us to do something wonderful in Prabhupāda Village for your pleasure. We all have an eternal debt to you.

You crossed the Atlantic Ocean with empty pockets and a heart filled with love for all of us who suffered on the other side. No one else did this. No one else cared enough to leave the comforts of their homeland to save us. You alone did this. You alone made all the arrangements to come to give us life and save us from self-destructing in the material world. Without your love, none of us would ever have known or had the chance to serve and try to love Lord Caitanya and Nityānanda, or Śrī Kṛṣṇa and Rādhārāṇī.

Who among us can imagine waking up in the morning with no Deities, no holy name, no *prasādam,* no philosophy, no understanding of who we really are, no stories about Kṛṣṇa lifting Govardhana Hill, killing the Pūtanā witch, or dancing with the *gopīs,* or about Śrī Gaura-Nitāi’s *saṅkīrtana* pastimes? Who can imagine such a stale and senseless life? So much we almost take for granted now. But how much you sacrificed to give us a chance to live, to dance, to sing, and to perfect our lives.

The clock is ticking, and these bodies of ours are becoming more difficult to manage. Let us do something with the remainder of our lives to make you proud of us. Allow us to put aside our differences and work in harmony. Let us use whatever gifts we have from Kṛṣṇa to glorify you and bring others to the shelter of your lotus feet. Let us at least endeavor to pay the interest on the huge debt we have accrued. We beg to remain at your lotus feet and to serve your great mission.

Begging to become the servants of your servants—eternally,  
*Your grateful servants of Prabhupāda Village, North Carolina.*  
(written by Karṇāmṛta dāsa)

## Radio Krishna Centrale

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

My beloved Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept my most humble and respectful obeisances at your divine lotus feet, whose shadow offers shelter to the whole universe.

Your desire moves the material and spiritual energy and makes everyone aware of the most amazing feature of Śrī Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Personality of Godhead: His willingness to let Himself be captured by the pure love of His pure devotee.

Your International Society for Krishna Consciousness has emerged from your intense desire to flood everyone with pure love of Godhead. Such intense desire is the heart pulsating within this ISKCON ocean, generating wave after wave of *bhakti*.

Your ISKCON movement is not just a wave but an ocean full of waves. And even if someone feels uncomfortable with the idea that ISKCON is an ocean and not just a wave in the ocean, still he has to sincerely and objectively accept that *this* wave is not an ordinary wave but an extraordinary one—a gigantic tsunami capable of destroying every material conception, construction, defense, and opposing element and flooding the whole world with pure love of Godhead.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we know that if we want to be able to call ISKCON “our movement” and identify with it, our desire has to be exactly like yours. In fact, our desire has to become one with yours and perfectly synchronized with yours so as to increase the strength of ISKCON’s heartbeat, avoid arrhythmia, and prevent stroke.

We have to maintain the awareness that Your Divine Grace is our extraordinary ISKCON founder-*ācārya,* Aṣṭottara-śata Śrī Śrīmad Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, at whose feet all other *prabhus* sit.

My dear spiritual master, whether I live or die, my only desire is to remain with Your Divine Grace.

I pray again and again: please keep alive and increase my enthusiasm to continue to serve you and try to please you.

Please accept us as voluntary instruments at your command, ready to preach the way you have shown us.

By myself, I’m certainly devoid of any good quality and worthless, but you are certainly the most expert in engaging everything and everyone in Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu’s transcendental service.

And when death strikes—that “Great Forgetfulness,” who is not just waiting for us but always walking with us, side by side and step by step—please let me remember your divine lotus feet, and let that remembrance continue life after life.

Please allow me to gain your eternal association among your surrendered, faithful, and chaste devotees.

Your humble servants at Radio Krishna Centrale, Terni, Italy.  
(written by Tridaṇḍi dāsa)

## Seminário de Estudos Vaiṣṇavas (Seminar for Vaiṣṇava Studies)

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

All glories to Śrī Śrī Guru and Gaurāṅga! All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!

All glories to our dear *paraṁ-guru,* who came to bring the mercy of Lord Caitanya and Nityānanda, and to bring the way of liberation for this heaviest age, and those who are most degraded.

His Divine Grace Śrīla Prabhupāda was not just a spiritual master. He was infused with *gaura-śakti*, the potency of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu, to spread His *saṅkīrtana* movement in every town and village of the world. All that the world outside of India knows about Kṛṣṇa is due to Prabhupāda’s efforts.

Today, due to his remarkable efforts, we have the great opportunity to study all his literary works, such as the *Bhagavad-gītā As It Is, The Nectar of Devotion,* etc. Through these literatures we can perceive all his intelligence, depth, and singular wisdom, producing in us more and more conviction that surrender to the lotus feet of Lord Kṛṣṇa is the only path to the light.

Also, we feel enlivened and refreshed by his purports, and we understand practically that problems and doubts exist only due to our not reading his books. In other words, suffering is due only to our tendency to remain far from the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Jaya* Śrīla Prabhupāda!

Śrīla Prabhupāda’s servants at SEVA, Seminário de Estudos Vaiṣṇavas, New Gokula Dhāma, Brazil.  
(written by Mahā Kṛṣṇa Nāma dāsa)

## Vaiṣṇavas C.A.R.E.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, who continue to exemplify true compassion for the suffering souls within this material world.

It is by the mercy of Your Divine Grace that Vaiṣṇavas C.A.R.E. has entered its third year and continues to offer assistance to terminally ill devotees with end-of-life needs. We are pleased to report that we currently have over seventy devotee volunteers worldwide who are prepared to help a dying Vaiṣṇava and his or her family within their community when the need arises. Because you instructed us that every soul who surrenders to Lord Kṛṣṇa is rare, our goal is that each and every Vaiṣṇava will be lovingly cared for by devotee caregivers who will give the gift of the Holy Names to those preparing to leave this world.

It is only by the mercy and inspiration of Your Divine Grace that we began this hospice project, and it is only by your mercy that we continue. We pray that more of your disciples and granddisciples will join us in this mission so that Vaiṣṇavas in every corner of the world can be well cared for. We humbly ask for your continued blessings and beg for the opportunity to better serve your devotees.

Your servants at Vaiṣṇavas C.A.R.E. (Counseling, Assistance, Resources, and Education for the Terminally Ill and Their Families),  
(written by Saṅgitā-devī dāsī)

## Vedic Academy of Spiritual Technology

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śūnyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet. All glories to Your Divine Grace!

Śrīla Prabhupāda, last year we sent you our Vision and Mission Statement. Since then, we are pleased to inform you that the infrastructure of our organization, the Vedic Academy of Spiritual Technology (VAST), is ready and new members have signed up.

Your dear disciple and our founder-chairman, His Holiness Śrīdhara Swami, left his body in Śrīdhām Māyāpur on March 14, 2004. He had a long-standing dream to develop nice presentations, along with visual aids, especially attuned to impact the corporate world. As chairman of VAST, His Holiness Śrīdhara Swami has left behind a dynamic and inspiring keynote address, which he delivered to all of us, excerpts of which we wish to share with you:

Śrīla Prabhupāda went to America with the idea of preaching to the intelligent people of America. There was some success. What he really wanted was the success that Lord Buddha had achieved one hundred years after his disappearance from this world. Emperor Aśoka took up the mission and spread it all over India. We have the best philosophy, best culture, best *paramparā* system. We have had some success, but not compared to what can be done by working smarter. Śrīla Prabhupāda wanted to preach to the leaders of India and the world and involve them in doing wonderful things to assist the preaching mission.

Let us get big results—that was Prabhupāda’s “disease.”Śrīla Prabhupāda said, “My ‘disease’ is that I can do nothing small.” His “disease” was that he had to execute the mission of his Guru Mahārāja, the mission of Lord Caitanya, in a big way.

Let us be result oriented for Guru and Gaurāṅga and get started now.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, what follows are excerpts of endorsements received from a few of your disciples regarding the importance of the VAST project. We hope these will please you and the devotees worldwide.

I am very much supportive of your efforts, and wish you all success in pioneering this valuable outreach effort, aimed at fulfilling one of Śrīla Prabhupāda’s cherished desires and practical strategies, namely that of bringing educated persons to embrace Kṛṣṇa consciousness so that many thousands of others in turn will follow their example.—Romapāda Swami

The VAST project fulfills a very acute need. It teaches interested managers and leaders the spiritual dimension, or put differently, an ever-fresh source of inspiration, by showing means and ways how to translate the powerful wisdom of the *Vedas* into the language of personal development and life skills.—Śacīnandana Swami

It is very important that we have projects that focus on innovative preaching. This approach is necessary for spreading a worldwide movement.

There is a wonderful team of competent devotees who are working together in this corporate preaching program. We pray that many supporters will come forth to assist so as to help in a very profound way in creating a more just, righteous, and peaceful world order at a time when the planet is endangered.—Bhakti Tīrtha Swami

Śrīla Prabhupāda, we are pleased to inform you that on April 11, 2004, at Hare Krishna Land in Juhu, Mumbai, VAST announced the launch of The Awakening, a stress management program tailor-made for today’s corporate professionals. It was a deep desire of His Holiness Śrīdhara Swami that such programs should be designed and conducted in a professional manner for the welfare of the corporate world.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, on this auspicious 108thanniversary of your divine appearance, we pray from our hearts for your blessings so that we can remain sincere in our service and successfully preach the philosophy of Kṛṣṇa consciousness to the intelligentsia around the world.

Your servants at the Vedic Academy of Spiritual Technology.

## Vṛndāvana Institute for Higher Education

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,

Please accept our humble obeisances at your lotus feet.

By your own example, you have taught us that the *cakora* bird would only drink from the water falling from the rain cloud. May we always search for and await your instructions.

As I write to you today, we just completed the closing ceremony for the first year of the second batch of Bhakti Vaibhava studies. The devotees studied Canto 1 and 2 of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* from December 2003 till April 2004. We hope that you will be pleased to know that this time all the teachers were grand-disciples of yours. We could directly experience the irrevocable and unchecked descent of mercy through successive generations of speakers and hearers. The message is alive.

This coming *cātur-māsya,* along with the first batch of Bhakti Vaibhava students, we will be hearing Canto 4. There will be workshops on devotional service in the modes of nature. We will also be introduced to Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura’s Daśamūla Tattva. The assessment is being refined as we progress so as to draw the best out of the students. As it stands, they write essays expressing their realizations and revealing their capacity to apply the philosophy and relate to your mission. The most interesting are the oral presentations, through which they gradually develop the make-up of effective teachers concerned with the learning of their audience. They have tests on their knowledge and understanding, and they memorize *ślokas* you were fond of or which best express various aspects of the Vaiṣṇava *siddhānta*. In addition, all of them must teach or involve themselves in preaching activities during the months off studies.

Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, you founded the International Society for Krishna Consciousness on the basis of the philosophy of the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* for you knew the truth therein: “Whenever pure topics of the transcendental world are discussed, the members of the audience forget all kind of material hankerings, at least for the time being. Not only that, but they are no longer envious of one another, nor do they suffer from anxiety or fear.” (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 4.30.35)

Thus you have blessed us, and we shall praise you so we always remain in the shade of your benevolent influence. A personality like you is like a wooden stove in the middle of a rough winter. We like to gather together around you in the form of your disciples’ memories of you and the purports of your books. When we come together in this way and live the practical application of your teachings, we feel the soothing warmth of transcendental family ties. *Bodhayantaḥ parasparam*. We are the *acyuta-gotra,* as you called us, and our identity is becoming more real little by little, entering through the holes of our ears as we gather tight around you.

This year is your 108thbirth anniversary, so we are going to dedicate our Kārttika semester to you. After the book *Bhāgavata* recitation is over, we will hear about you, the person *Bhāgavata*. Please bless us that we may be allowed to continue facilitating the devotees’ access to the transcendental sound vibration, as well as to practical training. We need intelligence and certainly more organization.

When this letter reaches you on your appearance day, along with many more from all over the world, may you please be especially merciful this time. Please empower us to grow and bloom into the kind of saintly personalities that you envisioned. You are giving us everything we need. Of this we have no doubt. Kindly bless us that we may be able to follow you. And if you so desire, please empower the willing souls to spread the chanting and hearing of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* to every temple, farm, and preaching center. We beg for your mercy for this to happen.

Torn apart between the bliss of your association and the pains of separation from you, we remain  
Your servants at the Vṛndāvana Institute of Higher Education.
